### M26-S04 — Provider webhook — BFF route, internal processing, exact-200 contract (UC-114)

**Agent:** `bff-ts + backend-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-114 (all steps and A1–A6) and UC-134 (main flow), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API (the webhook route and the internal route), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built) (webhook decorator rule) and § Event Handlers, `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-R1), `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` § Testing the billing provider — fake, sandbox, contract suite and clock, `docs/BFF_ARCHITECTURE.md` § Module & Controller Naming Conventions, `docs/ANTI_PATTERNS.md` (guards vs interceptors; `@Public()`), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Subscription (reactivation), § Aggregate: Charge, `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.billing_accounts` (the UNIQUE standalone index on `(provider, provider_customer_id)`) and § `billing.subscriptions` (`current_period_*`, `next_due_date`, `anchor_day`)
**Dependencies:** M26-S01 (webhook token), M26-S02 (charge model, inbox, factory), M26-S03 (events and topics), M25-S14 (provider port, `BillingProviderRegistry` and fake), M25-S15b (suspend / reactivate events and cache invalidation), M25-S13 (access cache invalidation), M25-S12 (`IClock`), M25-S08 (aggregates and repositories), M25-S16 / M25-S20 / M25-S21a (the gating decorators every new route carries)
**Pattern:** Adapter (provider webhook → normalised `ProviderEvent`) + **one shared application service `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`** (M26-D1); Transaction Script with a subscription row lock (B-R1).

**Description:**
Make the provider's word the source of truth, safely.

**BFF** (`billing.webhook.public.controller.ts`, in the existing `features/billing` module but its own controller per the BFF naming rule — it is public and provider-called, never mixed with the authenticated `billing.controller.ts`): `POST /v1/public/billing/webhooks/asaas` with `@Public()` **and** `@SkipThrottle()` (no tenant slug arrives, and the BFF carries no tenant-access annotation, M25-S33; the backend route below carries the annotations), and `@HttpCode(200)` (Nest's default `201` for `POST` would make Asaas count a failure). It verifies the `asaas-access-token` header with a **timing-safe** comparison against `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` (wrong / missing → `401`, nothing stored — **authenticity is the BFF's job and its unit test; the backend never sees the token** and `parseWebhook` only normalises), then forwards the already-parsed JSON body to `POST /internal/billing/webhooks/asaas` through a **dedicated tenant-less call** (`BackendHttpService.postWithoutTenant`, new: `post()` takes its headers from the request's user and `postForPublic()` requires a tenant id, so neither fits a caller with no user and no tenant; the new method sends only `X-Internal-Key`). **Which URL Asaas calls (Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18), H2):** both BFF Cloud Run services are `INGRESS_TRAFFIC_INTERNAL_ONLY`, so the only public path is the `ikaro-web` same-origin gateway (`apps/web/app/v1/[...path]/route.ts`); the URL registered in the Asaas account is `https://<web-host>/v1/public/billing/webhooks/asaas` and the gateway forwards the `asaas-access-token` header untouched. The gateway attaches the BFF shared-secret header itself, so the web-only guard already passes for that path and **no `@BypassWebOnlyGuard()` is needed**; the gateway-resolved `x-real-client-ip` makes every Asaas delivery one client, so `@SkipThrottle()` is mandatory (a retry storm must never meet a per-IP `429`). Whether the production edge (Cloudflare / WAF / Cloud Armor, M17-S36) lets Asaas's source addresses through to that path is unverified and is an S39 gate item. The body is not kept as raw bytes: Asaas authenticates with a static token header, not a body signature, and the BFF's JSON parser re-serialises the object (raw-body preservation is Stripe's need, M28, and is not built here). If `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` is unset (production before S39) every request fails the token check, so the route answers `401` and calls no backend (fail closed, with a unit test; nothing is registered at Asaas before S39, so no delivery is expected).

**Backend** (`InternalApiGuard`, `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(ANY_AUTHENTICATED)`): `ProcessProviderWebhookUseCase`. **Provider resolution** (every M26 use case obtains the adapter from `BillingProviderRegistry.resolve(merchantProfileKey)`, never a bare port): before the tenant is known the adapter is resolved from the provider in the route plus `resolveMerchantProfileKey(APP_ENV, 'BR')` (M25-S07) and used only for `parseWebhook` (local, no network); once the tenant is known, `fetchChargeStatus` uses the registry entry of the **subscription's own** `merchant_profile_key`. The use case resolves the tenant from the provider customer id and, when that customer is not yet known locally, from the payment's `externalReference` (our charge id, which exists locally as a `PENDING` charge before the provider is ever called; the field's presence in the webhook payload was observed in the sandbox (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 2, check 11) and is re-checked in S37) so a webhook that outruns S05's customer save is not dropped (neither resolves → `200`, logged, no state change — A2), normalises the event (the M25-S14 normaliser emits `PaymentConfirmed`, `PaymentOverdue`, `PaymentCancelled`, `PaymentRefunded`, `PaymentPartiallyRefunded`, `PaymentChargeback` and `Ignored`; **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): this story splits the dispute events** — the Asaas normaliser (`asaas-event-normalizer.ts`) maps `PAYMENT_CHARGEBACK_REQUESTED`, which is an *opened* dispute, to a new `PaymentDisputeOpened` type (log only, `billing_dispute_opened`) instead of today's `PaymentChargeback`, and `PaymentChargeback` then means a **lost** chargeback that is applied; the Asaas event that finalizes a lost dispute is unobserved (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A11 / G5), so until S37 verifies it `PaymentChargeback` is produced by the fake only. Why: mapping an opened dispute to `PaymentChargeback` would suspend a tenant on a dispute the provider has not decided. This story applies `PaymentConfirmed`, `PaymentRefunded` and `PaymentChargeback`, confirms-only `PaymentOverdue` (below), logs `PaymentDisputeOpened`, and acknowledges `PaymentCancelled`, `PaymentPartiallyRefunded` and `Ignored` with `200` and no state change unless its `/story-discovery` decides otherwise; auto-billing events are not handled in M26) and, in **one transaction**: `ProviderEvent.insertIfAbsent` (duplicate → `200`, no-op), then `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` — which loads the subscription `findByTenantIdForUpdate` (B-R1) **first**, then the charge, applies the fact and writes the outbox rows (`ChargePaid`, `SubscriptionReactivated`, `SubscriptionSuspended` as applicable). **Any event the adapter does not normalise** (Asaas also sends `PAYMENT_CREATED`, `PAYMENT_UPDATED` and more; `PAYMENT_DELETED` is normalised to `PaymentCancelled`), an unknown customer **and** unknown `externalReference`, and an amount mismatch after the re-read all end in **exactly `200`**, logged, with no state change — a non-2xx would count toward Asaas's documented pause rule (15 consecutive failures, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G3) for events that are not failures.

**The paid signal is method-dependent**: Asaas Pix emits only `PAYMENT_RECEIVED`; boleto `CONFIRMED` then `RECEIVED`; card `CONFIRMED` then `RECEIVED` ~32 days later — the first of `CONFIRMED` / `RECEIVED` for a charge yields the single `PaymentConfirmed`, the later is a no-op. **For Asaas a state-changing event is applied only after `fetchChargeStatus` confirms it and the amount matches** (a leaked token cannot forge a payment; **`NormalizedChargeStatus` carries no amount today, so this story adds `amountMinor` (and `disputeOpen: boolean`) to it, to the fake and to the contract suite, and the "amount matches" check compares it with the local charge's `amountMinor`** — Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18); the webhook event already carries `amountMinor`, but the re-read is the truth) — that re-read is a provider call and therefore happens **before** the transaction opens, never inside it; it also supplies the `receiptUrl` (`NormalizedChargeStatus.receiptUrl`, M25-S14), stored by `markPaid(method, paidAt, receiptUrl | null, now)` and carried by `ChargePaid`. To protect the provider's rate limit (25,000 requests per 12 h per account, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A13) a replayed or duplicate delivery must not cost a re-read: a cheap read-only `ProviderEvent` existence check by `(provider, eventId)` runs first and returns `200` with no provider call when the event is already in the inbox (the authoritative dedupe stays `insertIfAbsent` inside the transaction).

**What a paid fact writes (one definition; the aggregate methods are unit-tested per row, and S06/S20 reuse them):**
- *PERIOD charge, subscription `TRIALING`:* the charge is marked paid and `has_ever_paid` is set; the subscription stays `TRIALING` with `current_period_*` untouched (the sweep's `activateAfterTrial`, S06, writes them at trial end).
- *PERIOD charge, subscription `ACTIVE`:* `markPeriodPaid` marks the charge paid, sets `has_ever_paid`, sets `current_period_end = max(current_period_end, charge.period_end)` and sets `current_period_start = charge.period_start` only when that start is today or earlier in the subscription timezone (an invoice issued 10 days early does not change the current period). It never touches `anchor_day` or `next_due_date` (advanced by S05 at issue).
- *PERIOD charge, subscription `PAST_DUE`:* the same writes, and the subscription becomes `ACTIVE` (`past_due_since` and `grace_until` cleared); the period is **not** re-dated — it is the charge's own period.
- *RECOVERY charge, subscription `SUSPENDED` (`NON_PAYMENT`, `PAYMENT_REVERSED`, `CANCELED`):* `reactivateOnPayment` makes the subscription `ACTIVE` (reason, `suspended_at`, the purge-warning stamps and `cancel_at` cleared), sets `current_period_start` = the payment moment, `current_period_end` = one cycle later, `anchor_day` = the payment's tenant-local day clamped to 28, `next_due_date` = the day after the new period's end, and `has_ever_paid`; the recovery charge's own period stays empty. An `OPERATOR` suspension is never lifted by a payment.
- *Stale charge paid:* if the provider could not cancel a stale charge because it was already paid (UC-114 step 4), **that charge is the payment** and is applied by the rule above for its kind, and the recovery charge is cancelled; if both are ever paid, the second is recorded `REFUNDED` (the actual refund is made in the provider console).
- *Refund / chargeback:* a partial refund (`refunded_amount_minor` only) and an opened dispute change no access — an opened dispute is only logged with a structured `billing_dispute_opened` field (S36 turns it into a metric and an alert; no new table). A **full confirmed refund** or a **lost chargeback** (`PaymentChargeback`: the fake only until S37 verifies the Asaas event that finalizes a lost dispute — `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A11, G5; a re-read that reports `disputeOpen` with the charge still `PAID` is the opened-dispute case, never a suspension) that leaves the current period without settled payment suspends immediately with `PAYMENT_REVERSED` (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase` inserts the `RECOVERY` charge `PENDING` through the S02 factory in the same transaction; creating it at the provider after commit is the `CreateRecoveryChargeAtProviderUseCase` that S06 introduces and adds to this use case's after-commit step — until S06 ships, S05's retry creates it). **Exception — a full refund of the first invoice while the subscription is `TRIALING`:** nothing settled is being reversed, so there is no `PAYMENT_REVERSED`; the charge becomes `REFUNDED` and, because `UQ_billing_charges_period` ignores only `CANCELED` charges (a replacement period charge is impossible), S06's trial-end rule treats a `REFUNDED` first charge as unpaid and the recovery path applies at trial end. (This differs from the "re-issue" wording of the decision record; the canonical docs `DOMAIN_MODEL`, `RUNBOOKS` and UC-113 are aligned to this in the audit fix.)
- *`PaymentOverdue` is confirm-only:* after the re-read it records the inbox row and a log line and changes no charge or subscription state — the local due-date sweep (S06 rule 1) alone owns `PENDING` → `OVERDUE` and `PAST_DUE`, and a webhook never sets `past_due_since`.
- *Out-of-order:* "legal" is defined in S02 (a provider-verified paid fact may revive a `CANCELED` charge; nothing pays a `REFUNDED` / `CHARGEBACK` one); an illegal event is logged and left to reconcile (S07).

Access-cache invalidation after commit is **automatic**: `CachingSubscriptionRepository.save()` evicts the tenant's cached access record, so this story adds no explicit invalidation call (M25-S13). Response: **exactly `200`** after the commit — never `201`, `204` or a redirect; a backend failure returns non-2xx so the provider retries.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`ProcessProviderWebhookUseCase`**: parse and tenant resolution, normalisation, the `fetchChargeStatus` re-read outside the transaction, then the single transaction above.
2. **`ApplyChargeFactUseCase`** (shared, M26-D1): the paid / refunded / chargeback transitions on a charge and the resulting subscription transition (an overdue fact is confirm-only); called by S07 and S15 later.
**Backend HTTP surface:** new controller `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-webhook.controller.ts` — `POST /internal/billing/webhooks/{provider}` (`InternalApiGuard`, `@NoFeatureGate()`, `@SuspensionAccess(ANY_AUTHENTICATED)`).
**BFF endpoint spec:** `POST /v1/public/billing/webhooks/asaas` — no JWT, no tenant header; request = the provider's JSON; response = empty `200`. (`/stripe` is M28.)
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none new (the env var `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` is S01's).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/process-provider-webhook.use-case.ts`, `apply-charge-fact.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts` each) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-webhook.controller.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/http/billing/webhooks.http` (new)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.webhook.public.controller.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.component.spec.ts`) (new), `billing.webhook.schemas.ts` (new — the payload is forwarded, not parsed into a DTO)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify — register the webhook controller)
- `apps/bff/src/shared/http/backend-http.service.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — `postWithoutTenant`, sends `X-Internal-Key` only)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A paid Pix, boleto or card charge reactivates a `PAST_DUE` or `SUSPENDED` subscription without any human action, once, regardless of how many times the provider sends the event.
- [ ] A forged request (wrong token) changes nothing; an unknown customer changes nothing; an event type Ikaro does not act on is acknowledged with `200` and changes nothing.
- [ ] A full refund that leaves the period unpaid suspends the tenant (`PAYMENT_REVERSED`); a lost chargeback does the same **once the Asaas event that finalizes a lost dispute is verified** (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A11 / G5, S37) — until then `CHARGEBACK_LOST` is proven on the fake only and the Asaas normalizer maps an unverified chargeback event to "no state change, log"; a partial refund or an opened dispute never changes access; a full refund of the first invoice during the trial never suspends by itself.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Every charge write loads the charge with `findByIdForUpdate` (lock B-C1, after B-R1) before changing it, and `markPaid` is called only after the provider re-read (S02 discovery)
  - [ ] BFF: wrong / missing token → `401`; timing-safe comparison used; success → exactly `200` (not `201`); `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` unset → `401` and no backend call (fail closed); the tenant-less call sends no `X-Tenant-ID`
  - [ ] Normalisation table for the three payment methods (Pix `RECEIVED` only; boleto `CONFIRMED`→`RECEIVED`; card `CONFIRMED`→`RECEIVED`), including "whichever arrives first wins, the later is a no-op"
  - [ ] `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`: each row of the "what a paid fact writes" list (period fields, `anchor_day`, `next_due_date` per charge kind and status); the already-paid-stale-charge case; the double-paid refund case; out-of-order paid-after-cancelled applied only when provider-verified
  - [ ] Negative guarantees, each with its own test: a **partial refund** changes no access; an **opened dispute** changes no access and only logs `billing_dispute_opened`; `PaymentOverdue` changes no charge or subscription state and never sets `past_due_since`; a full refund of the first invoice while `TRIALING` produces `REFUNDED` and no suspension
  - [ ] The `fetchChargeStatus` mismatch (re-read `amountMinor` differs from the local charge's) → event not applied, logged, `200`; an unnormalised event type → `200`, nothing stored
  - [ ] Normaliser and contract suite: Asaas `PAYMENT_CHARGEBACK_REQUESTED` → `PaymentDisputeOpened` (never `PaymentChargeback`); `NormalizedChargeStatus` carries `amountMinor` and `disputeOpen` on the fake and in the contract cases; the lost-chargeback path is driven by the fake only and tagged fake-only in the suite (S37 turns it into a sandbox or recorded case)
  - [ ] The BFF webhook controller needs no web-only-guard bypass: a component test through the real guard with the shared-secret header passes and carries no per-IP throttle
  - [ ] A duplicate delivery already in the inbox returns `200` with **no** `fetchChargeStatus` call (the pre-check)
  - [ ] `markPaid` receives the `receiptUrl` from the re-read and `ChargePaid` carries it
- Integration:
  - [ ] End to end on the fake: simulate each delivery option from the fake — NORMAL, DUPLICATE, DELAYED, OUT_OF_ORDER, provider 5xx — and assert the resulting charge / subscription state each time
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-R1):** two webhooks for one charge arriving together produce one `ChargePaid`; a webhook racing a `suspend` on the same subscription serialises (the row lock holds, no lost update)
  - [ ] Inbox idempotency: replaying the same `eventId` is a `200` no-op with no second outbox row
  - [ ] The transaction contains no provider I/O (`transactional-io` detector passes; the re-read happens before)
  - [ ] A webhook that arrives before the customer id was stored, or before the charge is `READY`, is resolved through `externalReference` and applied
  - [ ] Unknown customer **and** unknown `externalReference` → `200`; missing `InternalApiGuard` key → `401`
  - [ ] The backend controller carries `@NoFeatureGate()` and an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` (the route-completeness detector passes); the BFF controller carries none (M25-S33)
- Tenant isolation: [ ] a webhook resolves exactly one tenant from the customer id and never touches another tenant's charges; two tenants with the same amount and due date are not confused
- E2E: none — covered by integration (the full money loop E2E is S35)
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S05 — Issue the next charge, creation retry and the `cron-billing-issue-charges` trigger (UC-113)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-113 (all of it, A1–A11), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.subscriptions` (indexes) and § `billing.charges`, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Charge (price snapshot), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing cron triggers, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built) (trigger jobs; Billing's jobs do not use the shared tenant-job helper) and § Transactions, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_TESTING.md` § Standalone index for a cross-tenant system job, `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook (cron trigger row), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned)
**Dependencies:** M26-S02 (charges, factory, the creation lease), M26-S03 (`ChargeIssued` topic), M26-S04 (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase`: a webhook that lands between the insert and `READY` is applied by it), M25-S04 (`GetFeaturePriceAtUseCase`), M25-S14 (provider port, `BillingProviderRegistry` + fake), M25-S12 (`IClock`), M25-S13 (the tenant-info port `BillingPlatformPort`, which this story extends with `isActive`), M25-S16 / M25-S20 / M25-S21a (gating decorators on the new controller)
**Pattern:** Trigger handler → job use case selecting through a standalone index, one subscription per transaction **taking the subscription row lock `findByTenantIdForUpdate` (B-R1) first** — it inserts a charge and advances `next_due_date`, a subscription write that must serialise with a plan save (S19) and a webhook (S04); **PENDING → READY** two-step creation (M26-D3); its own standalone-index selection with no operational-status filter (the retry query reaches `SUSPENDED` tenants' recovery charges).

**Description:**
Issue every due charge exactly once. A cron publisher `CronBillingController` (`POST /cron/billing-issue-charges`, `InternalApiGuard`, local / manual only, `@NoFeatureGate()` + an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(ANY_AUTHENTICATED)`) publishes the trigger `cron-billing-issue-charges` (`CRON_BILLING_ISSUE_CHARGES_TRIGGER`); `IssueNextChargesTriggerHandler` (class name unique across the codebase) is registered with `ITriggerBus.registerTrigger` and calls `IssueNextChargesUseCase`. **Selection:** subscriptions `TRIALING` or `ACTIVE`, not `exempt`, not cancelling beyond the period, whose `next_due_date` is within `CHARGE_ISSUE_LEAD_DAYS` (10), through the standalone partial index on `next_due_date` created in M25-S03 (`EXPLAIN` asserted) via a `findDueForIssueForJob` selector added to `ISubscriptionRepository` (the port today has `findByTenantId`, `findByTenantIdForUpdate`, `save` and the cross-tenant reads `listSuspendedTenantIds`, `findTenantIdsNotEntitledTo` and `listSummaries`; **every new selector is also forwarded by `CachingSubscriptionRepository`, uncached**, because that decorator implements the whole port and is the bound implementation). The query selects on the UTC date plus the lead plus one day of slack, then each subscription re-checks `next_due_date <= today(subscription timezone) + CHARGE_ISSUE_LEAD_DAYS` before it is processed (tenant-local dates, as in S06). **Per subscription, one transaction:** compute the lines from the items scheduled for the next service period, each amount read from the price version in force on that period's **start date** (`effective_from <= period_start`); insert the `Charge` (`PERIOD`, `providerCreationStatus = PENDING`) and its lines, and advance `next_due_date` by one cycle in the **same** transaction (`UNIQUE(tenant_id, subscription_id, period_start)` makes a re-run a no-op). Then, **outside** the transaction (`transactional-io` passes), the charge is **claimed** with S02's `claimForProviderCreation` lease (a creator that does not win the claim stops; the lease is why the manual `POST /cron/…`, a second instance and the retry query below can never create two provider charges), the adapter comes from `BillingProviderRegistry.resolve(subscription.merchantProfileKey)` (a `ProviderNotSupportedError` for a key with no adapter — a US subscription before M28, or a staging `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` one before S37 — is skip-and-log, never a crash: this story owns that guard and its test, S06–S08 reuse it); then `ensureCustomer` (syncing the identity, BR tax id required, with our tenant/customer reference as `externalReference` so a repeated call updates the same provider customer; the returned `provider_customer_id` is saved on the `BillingAccount` in its own short transaction **before** `createCharge`, so a fast webhook can resolve the tenant) and `createCharge` (`billingType = UNDEFINED`, our charge id as `externalReference` and as the idempotency key, so a concurrent or repeated creator finds the first provider charge instead of creating a second one — M26-D3, no separate lookup; the port already carries `returnUrl` on `CreateProviderChargeInput`, so **this story only sets it**). **`returnUrl` (owner: this story; Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18)) is `${FRONTEND_URL}/dashboard/billing?payment=return&charge={chargeId}`**, with `FRONTEND_URL` the backend's existing validated env var (`apps/backend/src/config/env.validation.ts`), so S28 reads the charge id from the marker and calls `payment-return` for exactly that charge; S37 maps it to Asaas's callback only where the callback-domain rule allows. Why: S28 needs a charge id in the marker and neither S05 nor S15 stated one. Then a further short transaction stores the provider id, payment link, expiry and `READY` (if a webhook already marked the charge paid in between, this step stores the ids but leaves the status alone and `ChargeIssued` is not published for a paid charge); **`ChargeIssued` is published only after `READY`**. **Creation retry** runs inside the same job: any charge left `PENDING` is re-attempted — through the same claim and the same idempotent `createCharge` — without inserting a duplicate period charge (A2); a charge held for lack of identity (below) makes no provider call and does not increment `provider_creation_attempts`, so it never trips the "creation stuck" alert (S36: a charge `PENDING` provider creation with 3 or more attempts or older than 1 hour). **TRIALING:** the first charge is issued from the manager's **confirmed** selection (`plan_confirmed_at` set — S19), due at trial end, as soon as it is within the 10-day lead; if still unconfirmed at `trial_ends_at − PLAN_CONFIRM_FALLBACK_DAYS` (3) the job issues it from the default paid items and the email states the total and the due date (A10; **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): the "which features" wording is dropped**, because `ChargeIssued` carries no line list and S11's copy states total and due date only; UC-113 A10 and UC-130 are aligned to it). **Holds:** BR customer without a valid CPF / CNPJ (A8) or without the full billing address (A9) → no provider charge and **no charge row either** (the subscription is simply left due, so nothing is inserted and `next_due_date` does not advance), a banner flag on the summary (`summary.addressRequired`, banner copy points to "Dados de cobrança") — **no hold email**: UC-113 A8/A9 say "banner and email", but no email kind exists for a hold and none is invented, so the banner plus the S09 trial-ending reminders are the notice (UC-113 A8/A9 are aligned to this); the trial-ending reminders (S09) already warn before the end; issuing **resumes automatically** when the identity is saved (S16 triggers a re-evaluation by leaving the subscription due) on the next run; no operator step. **The one other hold is the sweep-created `RECOVERY` charge** (S06 at a `NON_PAYMENT` / `PAYMENT_REVERSED` suspension — nobody is at the keyboard): it is inserted `PENDING` as usual but the provider customer and charge are not created while the CPF / CNPJ or the full billing address is missing; the charge stays `PENDING` with no payment link, `summary.addressRequired` is true, the suspended manager (who can still reach the billing page) is pointed to "Dados de cobrança" by the banner, the suspended page and the S12 suspended email (which says the payment link follows in the next email), and `POST …/payment-link` on it answers `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`; saving the identity lets this story's retry query create it and `ChargeIssued` then sends the link. **A manager's own reactivate (UC-134, S20) never creates a held charge**: with no address it answers `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` and creates nothing (UC-134 A6 stands). This sweep-hold extension is documented in UC-115 by the docs owner. A **trial held for lack of an address** keeps its end date — the hold never extends access: S06 rule 2 suspends it if no charge is paid, and its recovery charge is held the same way until the address is saved. **Skips:** `exempt` (A1), `SUSPENDED` (A4), `PAST_DUE` (deliberate: the selection is `TRIALING` / `ACTIVE` only, so no next `PERIOD` charge piles onto an unpaid one; the first run after the overdue charge is paid and the subscription is `ACTIVE` again issues it, because its `next_due_date` was not advanced for the skipped period), `cancelAt` passed for the period (A5), `is_active = false` (A11 — the M27 reference; guard written now, read through the M25 tenant-info port `BillingPlatformPort`, which **this story** extends with an `isActive` field by extending Platform's `get-tenant-billing-info.use-case.ts` (its output type and spec), backed by the existing `platform.tenants.is_active` column; S08 later adds the list method to the same port — the access answer does not carry it, so no second port is created), no renewable price version (A6 — never issued; a logged alert only). **A6 is prevented by a CI / seed-consistency test, never by a boot crash** (a bad price row must not take the whole backend down): the test asserts that every country with prices in the seed migration has a complete set — a `selectable` or in-use price for every (feature, tier) the country's plan can hold — and that every country the app can sign up (BR only until M28; the US list is seeded everywhere, see UC-127 A7) is among them; at runtime a missing renewable price skips that subscription, logs `billing_price_missing` with the subscription id and lets the job continue.

**Devops half (cron trigger).** New trigger → playbook row "A new Pub/Sub topic (… cron trigger) + its app code". **PR sequence:** 2 PRs + 1 Foundation apply. PR1 (`infra-app-mix-ok`): the `registerTrigger()` call site, `pubsub-catalog.json` regenerated (the topic auto-provisions from it — no `envs/*` pubsub edit) **and the `google_cloud_scheduler_job` entry (`ikaro-cron-billing-issue-charges`) in `modules/scheduler`'s `locals.jobs` in this same PR** (`variables.tf` rejects any catalog `cron-*` topic with no matching job); schedule `30 4 * * *` (daily, M26-D5), **created paused in production**. PR2 (`foundation` only, **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18)**): the scheduler-publisher grants are a hard-coded event array (`for event in ["cron-reminders", …] : "scheduler_publisher_${event}"`) in **both** `infra/terraform/foundation/envs/staging/main.tf` and `infra/terraform/foundation/envs/prod/main.tf`, so a new cron topic gets no publisher grant until it is added there; this story replaces the array in both roots by the cron entries of `local.workload_topics` (`for event in keys(local.workload_topics) : event if startswith(event, "cron-")`), which covers the eight existing topics unchanged and every later trigger story (S06–S10) with no Foundation edit of its own; the playbook forbids mixing `foundation/**` with other `infra/terraform/**` paths in one PR, hence a second PR, merged after PR1 has been applied (rule 1: the topic must exist before Foundation grants on it). Why: the alternative is an edit of both roots in each of six stories, and a forgotten edit is a silent "scheduler cannot publish". The scheduler module has no pause switch today, so this story adds it: a module variable `paused_job_keys` (set of `locals.jobs` keys, default empty) feeding `paused = contains(var.paused_job_keys, each.key)` on `google_cloud_scheduler_job.cron`, set in `envs/prod/main.tf` to the six billing job keys (S39 empties it after launch readiness) and left empty in staging. The module's hard-coded test fixtures (`tests/resources_unit_test.tftest.hcl` lists the cron topic map and asserts `length(...) == 8`; `tests/variables_unit_test.tftest.hcl` carries the `cron_topic_ids` fixture at lines 10-18 and the rejects-missing-key case at lines 48-58) and the "all 8" text of `cron_topic_ids` in `variables.tf` (the description and the validation `error_message`) are updated here, and **every later trigger story (S06–S10) updates the same fixtures and count** for its own job; then the manual Foundation apply.
**IAM / permissions:** scheduler publisher → the new trigger topic (the catalog-derived grant PR2 above introduces); push-subscription invoker for the backend — derived from the catalog by Foundation; no new principal.
**Live-verification check:** on staging after the Foundation apply: `gcloud pubsub topics get-iam-policy projects/<staging>/topics/ikaro-cron-billing-issue-charges` populated (the scheduler service agent listed as publisher); then `gcloud scheduler jobs run ikaro-cron-billing-issue-charges --location=<region>` and observe the structured log line `billing_issue_charges_run` with its `correlationId` and the count of subscriptions processed (zero is a valid pass on an empty staging; the line name is fixed because S36's alerts read it, and the same `billing_<job>_run` shape names the other five jobs' lines). Pass = IAM policy populated (including the scheduler publisher) and one `billing_issue_charges_run` line observed.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`IssueNextChargesUseCase`** (the job) and its per-subscription **`IssueChargeForSubscriptionUseCase`** (two transactions as above), 2. **`RetryPendingChargeCreationUseCase`** (the retry query).
**Backend HTTP surface:** new controller `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/cron-billing.controller.ts` — `POST /cron/billing-issue-charges` (publisher only; `InternalApiGuard`; `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(ANY_AUTHENTICATED)`).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A subscription ten days from its due date gets exactly one charge with a payment link and one email; running the job twice, or crashing between steps, never creates a second charge or loses the first.
- [ ] A trial that has confirmed its plan is invoiced from the confirmed selection; one that has not is invoiced from the default selection three days before the end, and the email says plainly the total and the due date that were invoiced.
- [ ] A tenant without a valid CPF / CNPJ or billing address gets no provider charge, sees the banner pointing to "Dados de cobrança", and is invoiced automatically on the next run once the identity is saved.
- [ ] Exempt, suspended, cancelling-past-the-period and deactivated tenants are never invoiced.
- [ ] A sweep-created recovery charge for a tenant without a valid CPF / CNPJ or billing address is held and becomes payable (and emailed) as soon as the identity is saved; a trial held for lack of an address is still suspended on its end date.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] The post-provider-call writes (`READY`, creation failure) each run in their own short transaction and re-read the charge with `findByIdForUpdate` (lock B-C1) instead of saving a copy loaded before the provider call; a charge a webhook marked `PAID` in between keeps its status and still stores the provider id and link (S02 discovery)
  - [ ] Line computation reads each price from the version in force at the **period start** (a version effective after the period start is ignored; an invoice issued early uses its future period's price)
  - [ ] Each alternative flow A1–A11 (one named test each), the trial first-charge rule and the fallback day
  - [ ] Handler calls exactly one use case, rethrows, passes `correlationId`
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-R1):** the issue job racing a plan save (S19) or a webhook on one subscription serialises — exactly one charge for the period carrying the final selection (S19 owns the named race test; this story provides the lock)
  - [ ] **Concurrency (creation lease):** two concurrent creators (the issue job twice, and the issue job racing the retry query) produce exactly one provider charge on the fake, and the second gets the first back from the idempotent `createCharge` (the fake finds it by `externalReference`)
  - [ ] The provider calls (`ensureCustomer`, `createCharge`) are outside any transaction (`transactional-io` passes)
  - [ ] **Idempotency:** running the job twice produces one charge; killing the process after the first transaction and re-running resumes at the provider call without a duplicate (`PENDING` → `READY`)
  - [ ] Provider failure (fake 5xx) leaves the charge `PENDING`; the retry query re-attempts it without a duplicate period charge; `ChargeIssued` is not published before `READY`
  - [ ] `provider_customer_id` is committed before `createCharge` is called; a webhook that lands before `READY` is applied (S04 resolves it) and the `READY` step keeps the paid status and publishes no `ChargeIssued`
  - [ ] The job's `EXPLAIN` uses the `next_due_date` partial index; one slow subscription does not block the rest (per-run limit + per-subscription transaction)
  - [ ] The price-set consistency spec fails when a seeded country has an incomplete price set; at runtime a missing renewable price skips that subscription with `billing_price_missing` and the job continues (no boot failure)
  - [ ] Hold: a held `PERIOD` issue inserts nothing and leaves `next_due_date`; a sweep-created `RECOVERY` charge held for a missing address makes no provider call and increments no attempts
  - [ ] A subscription whose tenant-local date is one day ahead of or behind UTC is selected exactly when its local `next_due_date` is within the lead (the ±1 day slack plus the local re-check)
  - [ ] The controller carries `@NoFeatureGate()` and an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` (route-completeness detector)
  - [ ] A `PAST_DUE` subscription is not selected, and one is issued on the first run after its overdue charge is paid; a subscription whose adapter is missing (`ProviderNotSupportedError`: a US key before M28, a staging sandbox key before S37) is skipped and logged, never throws, and the job continues
  - [ ] `returnUrl` passed to `createCharge` equals `${FRONTEND_URL}/dashboard/billing?payment=return&charge={chargeId}` for the charge being created
- Tenant isolation: [ ] one run issues charges for several tenants, each charge carrying its own `tenantId`; the job never reads `RequestContext`
- E2E: none — covered by integration; live check above
- [ ] Checkov clean
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S06 — Status sweep and the `cron-billing-status-sweep` trigger (UC-115)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-115 (all of it), UC-134 (recovery charge), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Subscription (status ladder, settlement margins, `graceUntil`), § Context 7 (constants), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.subscriptions` (partial indexes on `trial_ends_at`, `past_due_since`) and § `billing.provider_health`, `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-R1; the open-recovery unique), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built) and § Transactions, `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook (cron trigger row)
**Dependencies:** M26-S02 (charges, recovery factory), M26-S03 (`SubscriptionPastDue` topic and the events), M26-S05 (the trigger constants file and `CronBillingController` this story extends), M25-S15b (`SubscriptionSuspended` events), M25-S13 (cache invalidation), M25-S14 (`BillingProviderRegistry`, the typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`), M25-S12 (`IClock`), M26-S04 (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase` for the stale-charge-already-paid case and the trial-end re-read, and `suspend(PAYMENT_REVERSED)`), M25-S16 / M25-S20 / M25-S21a (gating decorators on the controller)
**Pattern:** Trigger handler → sweep use case, **one transaction per subscription** with the subscription row lock (B-R1); the recovery-charge factory from S02; its own standalone-index selection with no operational-status filter (the sweep must also evaluate `SUSPENDED` / non-operational tenants — cancel-at, recovery charge, freeze). Provider I/O (the trial-end re-read, the stale-charge cancel, the recovery-charge creation) is **always outside the transaction** (M26-D3).

**Description:**
Make time move the ladder. Hourly trigger `cron-billing-status-sweep` at minute 40 (`40 * * * *`, M26-D5: after the hour's reconcile at minute 10, so a missed webhook is corrected before the sweep judges the charge) (`CRON_BILLING_STATUS_SWEEP_TRIGGER`; publisher `POST /cron/billing-status-sweep` on the existing `CronBillingController`, which keeps `@NoFeatureGate()` + an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(ANY_AUTHENTICATED)` on the new route); `StatusSweepTriggerHandler` calls `SweepSubscriptionStatusesUseCase`. All dates are evaluated in `subscriptions.timezone` against `IClock.now()`; `exempt` subscriptions are never touched; each rule selects through its own standalone index via a `…ForJob` selector added to `ISubscriptionRepository` (and, for rule 1, to the charge repository), on the UTC date with one day of slack and a per-subscription local-date re-check, exactly as S05. Rules, each a separate private step with its own tests: **(1) Overdue** — a `PENDING` charge past its tenant-local due date **and with `provider_creation_status = READY`** becomes `OVERDUE` (**Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18):** a charge whose provider creation is still `PENDING` — no payment link ever existed, e.g. a recovery charge held for a missing billing address or one the provider has not accepted yet — is neither marked `OVERDUE` nor moves the subscription to `PAST_DUE`, because the payer never had a way to pay; the creation retry (S05) and the "creation stuck" alert (S36) own that state; the rule-1 selector therefore filters on it) (this sweep is the only writer of `OVERDUE` and `past_due_since`; S04 treats the webhook's `PaymentOverdue` as confirm-only); for an `ACTIVE` subscription it becomes `PAST_DUE` with `past_due_since` (event `SubscriptionPastDue`). **(2) Trial end** — a `TRIALING` subscription whose `trialEndsAt` plus the settlement margin has passed (`PAYMENT_SETTLEMENT_MARGIN_HOURS` 24 h, or `PAYMENT_PENDING_MARGIN_BUSINESS_DAYS` 3 business days — Monday to Friday in the tenant calendar, **no holiday calendar in M26** (a known limitation, stated) — when `payment_return_seen_at` shows the manager returned while the charge is still open) is re-read from the provider **before the per-subscription transaction opens** (never inside it) — **if the provider says the first charge is paid, the fact is routed through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` (S04) and the tenant is not suspended** (a missed webhook at trial end must never lock out a paying tenant; rule 5 then activates it); with its first charge unpaid (a `REFUNDED` first charge counts as unpaid, S04), or no charge at all, it becomes `SUSPENDED` (`NON_PAYMENT`) **directly** — there is no `PAST_DUE` after a trial; access is unchanged during the margin. **(3) Past due** — a `PAST_DUE` subscription becomes `SUSPENDED` (`NON_PAYMENT`) when its grace has run out: if `grace_until` is set, when `now > grace_until` (the operator override replaces the 14 days; M25-S15b's `ExtendGrace` refuses a date earlier than `past_due_since + 14 days`), otherwise when `now > past_due_since + 14 days`. **On every `NON_PAYMENT` suspension the sweep cancels the stale open charge (locally and at the provider) and issues the recovery charge** (a `PAYMENT_REVERSED` suspension is applied by the webhook, S04, not by the sweep; `CANCELED` and `OPERATOR` issue none) through the S02 factory, whose price map this use case resolves with `GetFeaturePriceAtUseCase` for the subscription-timezone date: the stale charge is cancelled locally and flagged `provider_cancel_pending` and the `RECOVERY` charge is inserted `PENDING` in the suspension's transaction; **after commit** the provider side runs through two shared use cases **owned by this story** — `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` (local `CANCELED` + `provider_cancel_pending`, then the provider cancel, clearing the flag on success; S19, S20 and M25-S15b's `set-tenant-exempt` hook depend on it) and `CreateRecoveryChargeAtProviderUseCase` (the PENDING → READY recipe, M26-D3, behind S02's creation lease and the `externalReference` lookup; S20 reuses it, and this story adds its call to S04's `ProcessProviderWebhookUseCase` after-commit step for a `PAYMENT_REVERSED` recovery charge) — so the manager lands on a payable "Pague 1 mês para reativar" (UC-115) within the same sweep run, and S05's retry query picks up any left `PENDING`. If the provider refuses to cancel because the stale charge is already paid (the port's typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`), that charge is the payment and the case follows `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` (S04) instead (A3); any other cancel failure leaves `provider_cancel_pending` set for reconcile (S07). If the billing identity or address is incomplete, the recovery charge is held `PENDING` without a provider call (S05 holds — the sweep-created recovery charge is the one hold in M26): the suspension still happens, `summary.addressRequired` is set, and the S12 suspended email points to "Dados de cobrança" and says the payment link follows. **(4) Cancel-at** — `cancelAt` reached → `SUSPENDED` (`CANCELED`) (no recovery charge for `CANCELED`). **(5) Trial → paid** — a `TRIALING` subscription past `trialEndsAt` whose first charge is `PAID` becomes `ACTIVE` through `activateAfterTrial`: `current_period_start` = `trialEndsAt`, `current_period_end` = one cycle later (the first charge's own period), `anchor_day` = the trial-end local day clamped to 28; `next_due_date` is left as S05 advanced it. **(6)** A confirmed payment converts `PAST_DUE` / `SUSPENDED` to `ACTIVE` through S04, never here. **Suspension freeze (A4):** `billing.provider_health` has one row per `merchant_profile_key`; its aggregate, repository and entity are created in this story (S07 and S08 reuse them) and the sweep reads the row **of each subscription's own `merchant_profile_key`**; **no row means not frozen** (the row is created lazily by the first reconcile / health write, tested). When that row's `freeze_active` is set (written by S08; `false` until S08 ships) the sweep still marks `OVERDUE` / `PAST_DUE` but suspends nobody **for non-payment** (rules 2 and 3). `cancelAt` suspensions (rule 4) are **exempt from the freeze**: they are the manager's own decision at a known date, not a payment judgement, so a provider outage must not extend a cancelled subscription. The provider cancel call is **outside** the transaction; if it fails the local state still changes, `provider_cancel_pending` stays set and reconcile (S07) retries the provider side. A payment arriving during the sweep: the row lock makes the paid state win (A2). Each suspension publishes `SubscriptionSuspended` and invalidates the access cache after commit.

**Devops half (cron trigger).** **PR sequence:** as S05 — 1 PR + 1 Foundation apply (PR1, `infra-app-mix-ok`: the `registerTrigger()` call site, the regenerated catalog and the `ikaro-cron-billing-status-sweep` `locals.jobs` scheduler entry in the same PR, plus the scheduler test fixture and job count; no `envs/*` edit — S05 already put the key in `paused_job_keys`; then the manual Foundation apply); schedule **hourly**, created paused in production (S39 enables it). **IAM / permissions:** derived from the catalog; no new principal. **Live-verification check:** `gcloud pubsub topics get-iam-policy …` populated; `gcloud scheduler jobs run ikaro-cron-billing-status-sweep` and observe the `billing_status_sweep_run` log line with its processed counts. Pass = both observed.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`SweepSubscriptionStatusesUseCase`** (the job; one `SweepOneSubscriptionUseCase` per candidate subscription, each in its own transaction with `findByTenantIdForUpdate`).
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/cron-billing.controller.ts` — `POST /cron/billing-status-sweep` (publisher only).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/sweep-subscription-statuses.use-case.ts`, `sweep-one-subscription.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/events/status-sweep-trigger.handler.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] An unpaid trial is suspended one day after it ends (three business days when the manager already returned from a boleto page), with no `PAST_DUE` in between; an unpaid `ACTIVE` charge becomes `PAST_DUE` the day after its due date and `SUSPENDED` fourteen days later with a ready-to-pay recovery charge.
- [ ] A payment that lands during the sweep always wins.
- [ ] While the provider is unhealthy (freeze), nobody is newly suspended for non-payment (a tenant's own scheduled cancel still takes effect).

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Every charge the sweep changes is loaded with `findByIdForUpdate` (lock B-C1, after B-R1) first, and the sweep never calls `markPaid` (S02 discovery)
  - [ ] Every rule 1–5 with table-driven dates, including a UTC-3 tenant and a DST zone, the 24 h and 3-business-day (Monday–Friday) margins, and `grace_until` overriding the 14 days in both directions (a later `grace_until` extends, an earlier one never shortens)
  - [ ] `exempt` never touched; running twice is idempotent (A1)
  - [ ] Recovery charge issued on `NON_PAYMENT` (the `PAYMENT_REVERSED` one belongs to S04), not on `CANCELED` or `OPERATOR`; the factory receives the price map for the subscription-timezone date
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-R1):** the sweep deciding to suspend while a webhook confirms payment on the same subscription serialises and the paid state wins; the loser leaves no recovery charge
  - [ ] **Concurrency (`UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery`):** two sweeps overlapping on one subscription produce one open recovery charge
  - [ ] Provider cancel failure → the local state still changes, `provider_cancel_pending` stays set and the suspension is not rolled back; the `ChargeAlreadyPaidError` refusal follows the paid path
  - [ ] **Trial-end re-read:** a `TRIALING` subscription past `trialEndsAt + margin` whose first charge the provider reports `PAID` (webhook missed) is not suspended — the fact is applied and rule 5 activates it; the same with the provider reporting it unpaid suspends
  - [ ] **Concurrency (creation lease):** a suspension's post-commit recovery creation racing S05's retry produces one provider charge
  - [ ] No row in `provider_health` for the profile key means not frozen
  - [ ] No provider I/O inside any sweep transaction (`transactional-io` passes): the trial-end re-read happens before it, the stale-charge cancel and the recovery-charge creation after commit; a suspension leaves a `READY` recovery charge with a payment link within the same run (fake provider)
  - [ ] Freeze active → `PAST_DUE` still recorded, no non-payment suspension; the freeze row is looked up by the subscription's `merchant_profile_key` (a frozen profile does not freeze another); a `cancelAt` suspension still happens under a freeze
  - [ ] A suspension for a tenant with an incomplete billing address leaves a `PENDING` recovery charge with no provider call, and S05's retry creates it once the address is saved
  - [ ] Each rule's selection uses its standalone index (`EXPLAIN`); one failing subscription does not stop the rest
  - [ ] A past-due `PENDING` charge whose `provider_creation_status` is still `PENDING` (a held recovery charge, a charge the provider has not accepted) is not marked `OVERDUE` and its subscription does not become `PAST_DUE`; the same charge with `READY` is
  - [ ] The controller route carries `@NoFeatureGate()` and an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)`
- Tenant isolation: [ ] each transition touches only its own tenant's rows; the job passes explicit `tenantId`s
- E2E: none — covered by integration; live check above
- [ ] Checkov clean
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S07 — Reconcile, the on-demand refresh and the `cron-billing-reconcile` trigger (UC-116)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-116 (all of it), UC-114 (step 3: the shared apply), UC-119 (the on-demand refresh), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.provider_health`, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing cron triggers, `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned) (Drift, Provider incident), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built), `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook (cron trigger row)
**Dependencies:** M26-S02 (charge selectors), M26-S04 (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase`, the shared apply), M25-S14 (the fake, `BillingProviderRegistry`, `fetchChargeStatus`, `readAccountHealth`), M26-S05 (trigger constants and `CronBillingController`), M26-S06 (the `ProviderHealth` repository and entity; `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` and the `provider_cancel_pending` flag this story's third step retries), M25-S03 (`provider_cancel_pending` column and the `IDX_billing_charges_paid_recent` index), M25-S16 / M25-S20 (gating decorators on the cron route)
**Pattern:** Trigger handler → job use case (its own standalone-index selection, no operational-status filter); reuses `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` (M26-D1) — no second implementation of the paid transition; a **single-charge refresh use case** for the on-demand path. The provider read (`fetchChargeStatus`) is outside every transaction; only the apply is transactional (`transactional-io` passes).

**Description:**
Guarantee a missed webhook never leaves a paying tenant locked out. Trigger `cron-billing-reconcile` (manual publisher `POST /cron/billing-reconcile`): a **daily** full pass and an **hourly** at-risk pass, scheduled at minute 10 of every hour (`10 * * * *`, M26-D5: before the sweep at minute 40) with `FULL_RECONCILE_HOUR_UTC = 4`. **One trigger, one scheduler job, hourly** — the trigger handler receives no payload (`ITriggerBus.registerTrigger` handlers take none and the scheduler job body is a fixed `{}`), so the two scopes are chosen by the handler from `IClock.now()`: every run reconciles the **at-risk set**, and the run in the configured daily hour (constant `FULL_RECONCILE_HOUR_UTC`) also runs the **full set**. Because a Pub/Sub redelivery after that hour would silently skip the day's full pass, the job records `last_full_reconcile_at` (the nullable column that already exists on `billing.provider_health` since M25-S03, per merchant key; **no migration and no DDL change here**) and runs the full pass whenever it is older than 24 h and the clock is at or past `FULL_RECONCILE_HOUR_UTC`. Stateless apart from that, testable with the fake clock, and it keeps the family at six scheduler jobs (seven with M27's purge, as `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` counts). The cron table in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (§ Billing cron triggers) already says "hourly, full pass once a day".
**Scopes (R10).** *At-risk (every hour):* open charges (`PENDING` / `OVERDUE`) of `TRIALING` subscriptions whose `trial_ends_at` is within 14 days, of `PAST_DUE` subscriptions, of `SUSPENDED` subscriptions (the open `RECOVERY` charge), **and any open charge with `due_date <= today`** (so a missed webhook is corrected before the sweep turns a paying `ACTIVE` tenant `PAST_DUE` and emails it). *Full (daily):* every open charge, plus every charge `PAID` within the last **35 days** (a card `RECEIVED` arrives about 32 days after `CONFIRMED`, and a refund or chargeback can follow a payment) through the standalone partial index `IDX_billing_charges_paid_recent` `(paid_at) WHERE status = 'PAID'` (M25-S03). Selection is keyset-paged by `(due_date | paid_at, id)` **within one run**, oldest first, so a page never re-reads the same rows; a run that hits the per-run limit is **truncated** and is not a complete pass. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): there is no cross-run cursor** — `billing.provider_health` has no cursor column and the job stays stateless apart from `last_full_reconcile_at`: the next hourly run simply re-selects the at-risk set (oldest due first, which is also the most urgent, and a charge leaves the set once it is settled), and a truncated full pass leaves `last_full_reconcile_at` untouched so the next hourly run tries the full pass again. The per-run limit is a constant sized well above the expected open-charge count, and a truncated run logs `truncated=true` (S36 warns on it). Why: a cursor needs a new column and a reset rule, while the limit only exists as a safety valve against one slow run.
For each charge: `fetchChargeStatus` (provider call, outside any transaction, through `BillingProviderRegistry` by the charge's `merchant_profile_key`) and apply the difference through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` — the exact same paid / overdue / refunded / **chargeback** logic as the webhook. **Drift** is fixed and counted: the daily pass emits a structured log line per differing charge and the `billing_kpi_daily` counts line (S36); any difference is a warning and more than 5 in one pass is critical (S36). **Third step — provider cancels:** charges with `provider_cancel_pending = true` (set by S06 / S19 / S20 before the post-commit provider cancel, M26-D3) are retried: `cancelCharge` through the registry, flag cleared on success; if the provider reports the charge already paid (typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`) that charge is the payment and goes through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`. A run **advances `provider_health.last_reconcile_ok_at` only when it is a complete (non-truncated) pass and at least one provider call succeeded** — a run with nothing to check calls `readAccountHealth` per merchant key so an empty at-risk set cannot report success while the provider is down; the freeze (S08) reads that column. Provider unreachable → the run fails and a log-based alert signal is emitted; it retries on the next run (A1). A local charge with no provider id older than 24 h → re-attempt creation through S05's retry use case, or alert (A2). **`RefreshChargeFromProviderUseCase`** (single charge, tenant-scoped) is the on-demand path S15 calls from `payment-return`: one provider read + one apply, returning the updated charge; it never throws on a provider error (the page keeps polling).

**Devops half (cron trigger).** **PR sequence:** as S05 — 1 PR + 1 Foundation apply (PR1, `infra-app-mix-ok`: the `registerTrigger()` call site, `pubsub-catalog.json` regenerated, and the one hourly scheduler job `ikaro-cron-billing-reconcile` in `modules/scheduler`'s `locals.jobs` in the same PR; `modules/pubsub` reads the catalog, so there is no per-topic `envs/*` edit — only `envs/prod` sets the `paused` flag S05 added; then the manual Foundation apply); the job is created paused in production (S39 enables it). Like S05, update the scheduler module's test fixtures (`modules/scheduler/tests/resources_unit_test.tftest.hcl` and `variables_unit_test.tftest.hcl`, which hard-code the cron topics) and the "all 8" text in `variables.tf`; the Foundation publisher grant is the catalog-derived list S05 introduced. **Live-verification check:** `get-iam-policy` populated; `gcloud scheduler jobs run ikaro-cron-billing-reconcile` and observe the `billing_reconcile_run` log line with counts (charges checked, drift fixed, scope AT_RISK or FULL, truncated or complete). Pass = both observed.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`ReconcileChargesUseCase`** (at-risk and full scopes, plus the provider-cancel retry step), 2. **`RefreshChargeFromProviderUseCase`** (on demand).
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `cron-billing.controller.ts` — `POST /cron/billing-reconcile` (publisher only; `@NoFeatureGate()` plus an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` as the M25-S16/S20 route-completeness detector requires); the on-demand refresh is called in-process by S15, not over HTTP by itself.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none (`last_full_reconcile_at`, `provider_cancel_pending` and `IDX_billing_charges_paid_recent` all exist from M25-S03).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/reconcile-charges.use-case.ts`, `refresh-charge-from-provider.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts` each) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/provider-health.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/repositories/typeorm-provider-health.repository.ts` (+ specs) (modify — S06 created the `ProviderHealth` aggregate, port, entity and repository for the freeze read; this story adds the `last_reconcile_ok_at` write per `merchant_profile_key`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/charge-repository.port.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/repositories/typeorm-charge.repository.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/src/test/repositories/billing/in-memory-charge.repository.ts` (modify — the `…ForJob` keyset selectors: at-risk open charges, full-pass open + recently-paid charges, `provider_cancel_pending` charges; each with an `EXPLAIN` integration test)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/provider-health-repository.port.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/entities/provider-health.entity.ts`, `apps/backend/src/test/builders/billing/provider-health.builder.ts`, `provider-health-entity.builder.ts` (modify — the `last_full_reconcile_at` read and write; S06 maps every existing column of the table in the entity and aggregate)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/billing-constants.ts` (modify — `FULL_RECONCILE_HOUR_UTC = 4`, the per-run limit constant)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (modify, only if a new job-path error appears)

- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/events/cron-trigger-names.constants.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/cron-billing.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/billing/cron.http` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A payment the provider confirmed but Ikaro never heard about is picked up within the hour for an at-risk tenant (daily for everyone else) and reactivates the tenant.
- [ ] A manager who returns from the payment page sees the updated state on the next poll without waiting for the webhook.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Every charge change goes through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`, and the step that clears `provider_cancel_pending` after the provider confirms runs in its own short transaction that re-reads the charge with `findByIdForUpdate` (lock B-C1; S02 discovery)
  - [ ] Drift matrix: provider says paid / overdue / refunded / chargeback / unchanged × local status (table-driven; a provider `overdue` is confirm-only — no local change, the S06 sweep owns it); the apply path is `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`, asserted by a spy on the shared service
  - [ ] Provider unreachable → run fails, `last_reconcile_ok_at` not advanced; a complete pass with at least one successful provider call advances it; a truncated pass does not; a pass with nothing to check calls `readAccountHealth` and advances only if it succeeds (an empty at-risk set cannot report success during an outage)
  - [ ] Keyset paging within a run: pages are disjoint and ordered oldest first; with a per-run limit smaller than the open-charge count the run is marked truncated, `last_reconcile_ok_at` and `last_full_reconcile_at` are not advanced, and the next run re-selects from the start (there is no cross-run cursor)
  - [ ] Provider-cancel retry: a `provider_cancel_pending` charge is cancelled at the provider and the flag cleared; a provider that reports it already paid goes through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`; a failing cancel stays pending for the next run
  - [ ] The full pass runs when `last_full_reconcile_at` is older than 24 h and the clock is at or past `FULL_RECONCILE_HOUR_UTC`, including on a redelivery after that hour (fake clock)
- Integration:
  - [ ] End to end on the fake: drop a webhook (fake option), run reconcile, assert the charge is paid and the subscription reactivated, with `ChargePaid` published once
  - [ ] A local `PENDING` charge without a provider id older than 24 h is re-attempted
  - [ ] The at-risk scope runs every hour; the full scope runs once a day (fake clock); at-risk selects only at-risk charges (trial ending within 14 days, `PAST_DUE`, `SUSPENDED` recovery, any open charge due today or earlier), full selects open charges and charges `PAID` within 35 days (`EXPLAIN` asserted for both, standalone indexes incl. `IDX_billing_charges_paid_recent`); the job reaches `SUSPENDED` tenants with an open recovery charge (no operational-status filter)
  - [ ] An `ACTIVE` tenant whose payment webhook was missed and whose charge is due today is corrected by the at-risk pass before the sweep marks it `PAST_DUE`
  - [ ] No provider call inside a transaction (`transactional-io` passes)
  - [ ] `RefreshChargeFromProviderUseCase` on an unreachable provider returns the unchanged charge, never throws
- Tenant isolation: [ ] the refresh use case is tenant-scoped (another tenant's charge id → not found); the job passes explicit `tenantId`s
- E2E: none — covered by integration; live check above
- [ ] Checkov clean
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S08 — Self-heal, provider health, suspension freeze and the `cron-billing-self-heal` trigger (UC-140)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-140 (all of it), UC-110 (the shared create-trial use case), `docs/BOUNDED_CONTEXTS.md` § 7. Billing Context (the narrow tenant-info port and its exact-path `permittedEdges`), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.provider_health`, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Entry point: `TenantAccessPort` (provisioning window), `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned) (Provisioning, Provider incident), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built), `docs/BOUNDED_CONTEXTS.md` § Rule 2 — Communication via Events or BFF Only, `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook (cron trigger row)
**Dependencies:** M26-S06 (the `ProviderHealth` repository and entity), M26-S07 (reconcile writes `provider_health`), M25-S09 (the registration repository), M25-S11 (`CreateTrialSubscriptionUseCase`), M25-S13 (the access port **and the read-only tenant-info port with its exact-path `permittedEdges` entry**, which M25 creates for the provisioning window), M26-S05 (trigger constants and `CronBillingController`), M25-S16 / M25-S20 (gating decorators on the cron route)
**Pattern:** Trigger handler → four small independent steps (its own selection, no operational-status filter); **reuses** the read-only Billing → Platform tenant-info port that M25 created (exact-path `permittedEdges`, the Notification precedent `INotificationPlatformPort.getTenantInfo`) — M26 adds no new cross-context edge here.

**Description:**
Resolve the common failure modes without an operator. Hourly trigger `cron-billing-self-heal` (minute 25, `25 * * * *`, M26-D5; manual publisher `POST /cron/billing-self-heal`), four steps that fail independently (provisioning repair (1) with its stale-registration release (1b), provider health (2), suspension freeze (3)): **(1) Provisioning repair** — tenants older than `PROVISIONING_GRACE_MINUTES` (15) with no subscription get their trial created through `CreateTrialSubscriptionUseCase` (idempotent, `UNIQUE(tenant_id)`). **How the tenants are found without a cross-context SQL join** (`docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` describes this as an anti-join, but CLAUDE.md §8 forbids a join across contexts): this story **extends the M25-S13 tenant-info port** (`BillingPlatformPort`, existing exact-path `permittedEdges` entry) with `listTenantsCreatedBefore(cutoff, limit, after?)` — a **keyset page** ordered `created_at ASC, id ASC`, where `after` is the `(createdAt, id)` of the last row of the previous page, so a per-run limit never re-reads the same first tenants and a tenant beyond the first page is still reached (the job pages until exhausted or until its per-run cap, and resumes from the oldest unrepaired tenant on the next run; bounded work per run, not a full re-listing that starves the tail) — returning `{tenantId, countryCode, timezone, slug, createdAt, isActive}` (the key, the port's four documented tenant fields and the `isActive` field S05 added) — backed by a new read-only Platform use case `ListTenantsCreatedBeforeUseCase` (added to the same edge's `targetPaths`) that selects through a standalone index on `tenants.created_at` — and Billing then filters out the tenants that already have a subscription through its own `subscriptions` repository (`findExistingTenantIds(pageOfTenantIds)`, called per page). **Inactive tenants (`isActive = false`) are skipped, never repaired.** No second port, no new edge direction. The `isActive` field was added to the port by S05 (its A11 guard) and is simply returned here. **What the repair passes to `CreateTrialSubscriptionUseCase` (Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18)):** `tenantId`, `slug`, `countryCode`, `timezone` from the tenant-info page row, a fresh `correlationId`, and **no** `registrationId` or `eventId`; `occurredAt` is `IClock.now()` at repair time, so the repaired trial starts then with a full 14 days (the use case's "never the wall clock" rule is about the *event's* provisioning instant, which a repair does not have — the injected clock is not the wall clock and the fake clock drives the test). `adminEmail` is read by the use case only to stamp `acceptedBy` for a `SELF_SIGNUP` registration, and the repair has none, so this story makes `adminEmail` optional on the use case input (`adminEmail?: string`; the `TenantProvisioned` handler keeps passing it) rather than inventing a placeholder, and the billing email stays unset so S03's `recipientEmail` helper resolves `null` and Notification falls back to the managers (S12). Why: `BillingTenantInfo` carries no admin email and a trial started at the tenant's creation could already be partly spent. **Known limitation, stated:** the repair runs `CreateTrialSubscriptionUseCase` without the original event's `registrationId` (the `exempt` flag now lives on the registration, which the repair cannot locate from the tenant alone, because it is linked to the tenant only when the handler completes it), so an operator-provisioned `exempt` tenant whose event was lost would get a paying trial (the owner tenant is still exempt by slug, and the operator can use UC-137 afterwards); if the tenant-info port can expose the registration reference cheaply S08 passes it, otherwise this is documented in `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing ("Provisioning"). **(1b) Release stale registrations** — a `RESERVED` `signup_registrations` row older than 1 hour (an orphan left by a failed signup step 3, S24) is released by `SignupRegistrationRepository.releaseExpiredReservations(cutoff)` (it releases operator registrations too: a tenant created from one that expired before the Billing handler completed it is repaired by (1) as a default trial, and the operator uses UC-137 if it was meant to be exempt) (M25-S09 deliberately left this sweep to M26 — `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.signup_registrations`), so the Google account's trial is not blocked by an orphan; the count is logged. **"Released" means the `RESERVED` row is deleted** (the status column has only `RESERVED | COMPLETED`). This sweep is only the **backstop**: S24 releases its own registration immediately on any failure after the reserve step through the idempotent `POST /internal/billing/registrations/{id}/release`. The release job selects through a standalone index on `(status, created_at)`-equivalent access (the table is tiny and the filter is `status = 'RESERVED' AND created_at < cutoff`; its `EXPLAIN` is asserted like the other cross-tenant jobs, adding a partial index in M25-S03 if the plan is a scan of a non-trivial table). Self-heal repair failures are alerted **log-based**: the job is stateless, so "N failures" is a log-based metric over repeated structured error logs for the same tenant id within a window (S36), not a persisted counter (A1). **(2) Provider health** — ask each provider adapter for its webhook state through the port's account-health read and webhook-queue re-enable (both are in the M25-S14 port and already listed in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Provider port, M26-D10 — nothing to add to the port or the doc); a paused or disabled webhook raises the alert and is re-enabled once through the API (`PUT /v3/webhooks/{id} {interrupted: false}`, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 4); the alert is closed only after a delivery succeeds after the call, and if the call fails or no delivery succeeds it stays raised with the runbook link (A2); the result is recorded in `billing.provider_health`. **"A delivery succeeded after the re-enable" (Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18)):** a `provider_events` row for the merchant profile's provider with `processed_at` set and `received_at` newer than the `updated_at` the re-enable step wrote on the `provider_health` row — a real processed webhook, not a cleared flag, and no extra column. Limitation: S07's `last_reconcile_ok_at` write and the freeze write also touch `updated_at`, which can only delay closing the alert (never close it early), so on a quiet account it stays raised until the next delivery and the runbook names the operator's manual check. **(3) Suspension freeze** — if reconcile (S07) has not succeeded for 24 h, or the provider is unreachable, `freeze_active` is set and S06's sweep suspends nobody for non-payment; it **clears itself** after the next successful reconcile. **Flap-proof rule:** the freeze is *set* when `last_reconcile_ok_at` is older than 24 h or the health read fails on two consecutive runs (**Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): the "two consecutive failed reads" signal is a stored counter**, new column `billing.provider_health.health_read_failures SMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, added by an expand-only migration: step 2 increments it on a failed health read and resets it to 0 on a successful one, and the freeze is set when it reaches 2; why: the job is stateless between runs and the table had no field that could carry the count); it is *cleared* only when `last_reconcile_ok_at` is newer than the freeze start **and** the current health read succeeds (S07 advances `last_reconcile_ok_at` only after a real provider call, so an empty reconcile cannot clear it). **No `provider_health` row means "not frozen"** (the row is created lazily by the first write; a fresh environment is never frozen by an absent row, and `last_reconcile_ok_at = NULL` on an existing row counts from `updated_at`); a test pins this. Setting and clearing keep the table's invariant `CHK_billing_provider_health_freeze` (`freeze_active = (freeze_since IS NOT NULL)`): both fields are written together in one statement. Tenants are never locked out because Ikaro or the provider was down, so no operator grace is needed. Nothing here raises a domain event (a repaired trial raises nothing new).

**Devops half (cron trigger).** **PR sequence:** as S05 — 1 PR + 1 Foundation apply (PR1, `infra-app-mix-ok`: the `registerTrigger()` call site, `pubsub-catalog.json` regenerated, the `ikaro-cron-billing-self-heal` entry in `modules/scheduler`'s `locals.jobs` (plus both scheduler test fixtures and the `variables.tf` text, as S05) and the two migrations (the index and the `health_read_failures` column); no per-topic `envs/*` edit and no Foundation edit (S05's catalog-derived publisher grant covers it) — `modules/pubsub` reads the catalog; then the manual Foundation apply); schedule hourly, created paused in production (S39 enables it). **Live-verification check:** `get-iam-policy` populated; `gcloud scheduler jobs run ikaro-cron-billing-self-heal` and observe the `billing_self_heal_run` log line with the four step results (repair, release, health, freeze). Pass = both observed.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`RepairMissingSubscriptionsUseCase`**, 2. **`ReleaseStaleSignupRegistrationsUseCase`**, 3. **`CheckProviderHealthUseCase`**, 4. **`EvaluateSuspensionFreezeUseCase`** — orchestrated by `SelfHealTriggerHandler` → `SelfHealUseCase`, each step failing independently.
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `cron-billing.controller.ts` — `POST /cron/billing-self-heal` (publisher only). The Platform tenant-info reads are in-process ports, not new public endpoints.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** two expand-only migrations — the standalone `created_at` index on `platform.tenants` (`AddTenantsCreatedAtIndex`, see Files; no column change) and `billing.provider_health.health_read_failures SMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (`AddHealthReadFailuresToProviderHealth`, a constant default, no rewrite, no backfill needed: an absent count is 0). The index is a plain `CREATE INDEX`, **not** `CONCURRENTLY`: `CONCURRENTLY` cannot run inside a transaction and this codebase runs every migration under `migrationsTransactionMode "all"` (precedent and reasoning: `1748400000013-AddStartedAtIndexToChatbotSessions.ts`, `1748500000009-AddResourceIdToScheduleClosuresAndOpenings.ts`); `platform.tenants` is small and nothing is in production yet.
