### M26-S16 — Billing identity read and save (UC-118)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-118 (all, A1–A6), UC-113 (A8, A9 holds), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Manager API (identity), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: BillingAccount, `docs/VALUE_OBJECTS_REFERENCE.md` (`Address`, `AddressSpec`, `CountrySpec`), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Adding a new error — checklist
**Dependencies:** M25-S06 (`TaxId` VO, `CountrySpec` tax-ID entry), M25-S08 (`BillingAccount`), M26-S14 (BFF controller and types)
**Pattern:** Command use case on the `BillingAccount` aggregate; shared `Address` / `TaxId` VOs; validation by `CountrySpec`.

**Description:**
`GET /v1/billing/identity` and `PUT /v1/billing/identity` (every `PUT` field optional): `{ taxIdType: 'CNPJ'|'CPF'|'EIN'|'OTHER'|null, taxId, legalName, billingEmail, address: { street, number, complement?, neighborhood?, city, state, zipCode } }` → `200` with the same shape. **BR: the tax id is present from signup, editable but never blankable** (the provider needs it): "never blankable" means an **existing** id can be replaced and never cleared; a `PUT` **may supply a missing one** (an operator tenant created without a tax id, UC-110 A7, supplies it here), and an `exempt` account is **not required** to have one (no charge is ever issued for it, so a missing id there is neither an error nor a blocker); **US**: optional, no CNPJ / CPF type offered (A3; US is not offered in M26 but the validation is country-driven, not hard-coded). Validation by the billing country's `CountrySpec` through the shared VOs: an invalid tax id for the billing country (BR: bad CPF / CNPJ check digits; US: an `EIN` that is not 9 digits, a declared `OTHER` over 32 characters) → `422 BILLING_IDENTITY_TAX_ID_INVALID` (A1; this story adds the code to `BillingErrorCode` and both locale files, and the use case catches the VO's `TaxIdValidationError` (`apps/backend/src/shared/value-objects/tax-id.vo.ts`, whose own code is a `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_*` one) and throws a Billing error carrying the new code). **Alphanumeric CNPJ rule:** a CNPJ is 12 alphanumeric characters plus 2 check digits (`CNPJ_PATTERN` in the VO), so neither layer may assume digits only: the BFF Zod schema accepts letters and punctuation for a `CNPJ` and leaves normalisation and check-digit validation to the backend VO (one rule, one layer), and the web form (S29) must not strip or mask letters out; whether Asaas accepts an alphanumeric CNPJ is `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G17, verified by S37. The billing-page copy and the UI hint (S29's) must say alphanumeric CNPJs are accepted — `journeys/manager/prototypes/billing/dev-notes.md` still says "14 dígitos". Invalid email → `422 BILLING_IDENTITY_EMAIL_INVALID` (A2; **coexistence with the shared `Email` VO:** the use case catches `EmailValidationError` (its own code is an `EmailErrorCode`, kept for every other caller) and throws the Billing error carrying `BILLING_IDENTITY_EMAIL_INVALID`, the same pattern as the tax id), wrong postal code or state format → the existing `Address` error codes (A5). **Partial-PUT semantics:** every field is optional, but the stored `address` is an `Address | null` (`docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: BillingAccount) and `Address.create` requires street, number, city, state and zipCode (`apps/backend/src/shared/value-objects/address.ts`), so a stored address is complete by construction. A `PUT` that sends `address` **merges** the sent sub-fields over the stored address (or over nothing) and validates the merged whole through `Address.create(…, countrySpec.address)`; if the merge is still incomplete the call fails with the existing `Address` field errors and nothing is saved (a half-typed address is never stored). "Address missing" is therefore simply `address == null`. The full address is required **before the first paid plan selection** (NFS-e): until saved, `summary.addressRequired` is true (S14) and `PUT /plan` is rejected `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` (S19). The identity is **synced to the provider customer the next time a charge is issued** (S05) — saving never calls the provider. Saving an identity is also what lets a held subscription be issued automatically (UC-113 A8/A9): no re-trigger is needed because S05's selector sees the subscription due. It never blocks *using* the product. The form's labels / placeholders / fields come from `CountrySpec` on the web (S29); the API only validates.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`GetBillingIdentityUseCase`**, 2. **`UpdateBillingIdentityUseCase`** (partial update; BR tax-id blanking rejected).
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `billing.controller.ts` — `GET /billing/identity`, `PUT /billing/identity`.
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends the BFF billing controller — `GET /v1/billing/identity`, `PUT /v1/billing/identity` (`@Roles('MANAGER')`); the Zod body schema lives in `billing.schemas.ts` and **mirrors the backend validation by construction** (CLAUDE.md §8: grep both layers for duplicate schemas).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** **`BILLING_IDENTITY_TAX_ID_INVALID` and `BILLING_IDENTITY_EMAIL_INVALID` are first thrown here and are added by this story** (M25-S06 deferred the former: the `TaxId` VO throws `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_*`, and this story's use case catches `TaxIdValidationError` and throws its own `BILLING_IDENTITY_TAX_ID_INVALID`) (`BillingErrorCode`, both `errors.json`, mapper). Tax id, legal name and billing email are PII/financial data: never written to logs, span attributes or domain events by this story.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/get-billing-identity.use-case.ts`, `update-billing-identity.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/billing-account.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `updateIdentity` with the merge-then-validate rule; "address missing" is `address == null`, no separate completeness method)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/billing.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — M25-S06 created it; map the identity errors, including the shared `Email` VO error and the `Address` error, `error-mapper-coverage` / `shared-vo-error-mapper-coverage`)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts` (modify — `BILLING_IDENTITY_EMAIL_INVALID`), `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json` and `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (only if needed)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.controller.ts`, `billing.schemas.ts`, `billing.types.ts`, `billing.mapper.ts` (+ specs), `apps/bff/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A manager can see and edit the billing identity with country-correct validation; a BR tenant can never blank its CPF/CNPJ.
- [ ] Saving a complete identity releases a held charge without any operator step.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Each of A1–A6; BR blanking rejected; partial update leaves untouched fields; US optional tax id
  - [ ] Partial `address` PUT: merged over the stored address and validated whole for BR and US field sets; an incomplete merge stores nothing and returns the `Address` field errors
  - [ ] No tax id / legal name / billing email in any log line or event payload (spy on the logger in the integration test)
  - [ ] Backend routes carry `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)` (decorator spec); the BFF routes carry no annotation (M25-S33)
- Integration:
  - [ ] Save → read round trip; invalid values return the documented codes and statuses (422) through the error mapper
  - [ ] A `PUT` with a missing BR tax id on an account that has none stores it; a `PUT` that sends an empty tax id to an account that has one is rejected and keeps the stored value; an `exempt` account saves an identity without a tax id
  - [ ] An alphanumeric CNPJ with valid check digits is accepted through the BFF schema and the backend; one with bad check digits → `BILLING_IDENTITY_TAX_ID_INVALID`
  - [ ] **Hold release:** a subscription held by missing address (S05 fake) is issued on the next job run after the address is saved
  - [ ] `STAFF`/`CUSTOMER` → `403`; reachable while `SUSPENDED`
- Tenant isolation: [ ] a manager reads and writes only their tenant's billing account; a cross-tenant `PUT` test: tenant A's manager saves an identity and tenant B's account (same country, both with an identity) is byte-for-byte unchanged, and each `GET` returns only its own tenant's values
- E2E: none — covered by integration; the tab E2E is in S35
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S17 — Plan reads — catalog and preview (UC-136)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-136 (main flow 1–2, A1–A6, A11), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Manager API (plan routes), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Subscription (item dependency graph, next-period scheduling), `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` (chatbot tier source), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.subscription_items`
**Dependencies:** M25-S04 (`FeaturePrice`, `ListSelectablePricesUseCase`), M25-S08 (`Subscription` dependency graph and item scheduling), M26-S02 (the charge repository, for the next-period-paid fact), M26-S14 (BFF controller and types)
**Pattern:** Query use cases over the `Subscription` aggregate's pure methods (`validateSelection`, `previewSelection`); no persistence in this story.

**Description:**
The read side of the Plano tab. `GET /v1/billing/plan` returns the catalog for the tenant's country (every selectable feature with its tier prices, dependency hints — Loyalty needs Booking; chatbot tiers Básico / Plus), the **current** selection, the **scheduled next-period** selection (when one exists; `selection` already includes scheduled future items, which a preview test pins) and the effective dates ("entra em dd/mm" / "sai em dd/mm"); features without a list price for the country/tier are shown as unavailable (A6). `POST /v1/billing/plan/preview` takes the proposed `{ items: [{ featureKey, tier }] }` and returns each change with its effective date (the next paid service-period start) and that period's new total, or the specific rejection — dependency unmet / dependents active (`BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET`, `BILLING_FEATURE_HAS_DEPENDENTS`, A1), empty selection (`BILLING_PLAN_EMPTY`, A5) — **without saving anything**. Read-only states are reported, not enforced here: `PAST_DUE` / `OPERATOR_SUSPENDED` (an `OPERATOR` suspension, A2) and a scheduled cancel (A4) set `readOnly` with the reason so the tab can explain; **a `SUSPENDED` subscription for `NON_PAYMENT`, `PAYMENT_REVERSED` or `CANCELED` is *not* read-only (UC-136 A14, decided 2026-11-16): `readOnly` is `null`, every change's effective date is `null` ("vale a partir do pagamento", never a calendar date), the previewed total is the one-month recovery charge that the selection would issue, and `deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid` does not apply (no next invoice exists while suspended)**; trial (A3) reports that all features stay active through the trial and the selection only determines the first paid period. The BOOKING-removal impact (A11) is **not** computed by preview (it needs S18); the removal guard is checked **at save only** (S19), whose `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` response names what blocks it (UC-136 A11), while the UI shows a generic note beforehand — consistent with S30 and prototype `08i`. This keeps the removal check out of the preview. **Effective dates account for an already-paid next invoice (UC-136 A7 / A12):** the aggregate does not know about charges, so each use case loads the tenant's next-period `PERIOD` charge (`ChargeRepository`) and passes one fact, `nextPeriodPaid: boolean`, into `previewSelection`; when true, every change's effective date is the period start **after** the paid one and the response says so (`deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid: true`). Save (S19) uses the same fact, so preview and save never disagree. **`exempt`** subscriptions are `readOnly` with reason `EXEMPT` (UC-117 A7: no plan editing) and a save is rejected `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` (S19). **Price source and `upcoming`:** the catalog's tier prices come from M25-S04's `ListSelectablePricesUseCase` (the tenant's country, "today" in the subscription's timezone) — the same use case S23 serves publicly — and this story adds the `upcoming` projection to it (the earliest version with `effective_from` after today, `announced_at IS NOT NULL` and `superseded_at IS NULL`), which S23 then reuses unchanged. A selected tier with no price in force for the country: preview returns `422 BILLING_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY` (UC-136 A6; **this story is the code's first thrower and owns it**, S19 reuses it) instead of a total, and `GET /plan` shows the tier `available: false`. **Response shapes (defined here, mirrored in `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts`; `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Manager API already carries a `Plan shapes` draft whose open names this story finalises — `catalog` becomes `features` and `next` / `effectiveFrom` becomes the `scheduled` block — while `readOnly: null | { reason }` and `SelectionChangeKind` are decided there as written):** `GET /plan` → `{ countryCode, currency, readOnly: null | { reason: 'PAST_DUE' | 'OPERATOR_SUSPENDED' | 'CANCEL_SCHEDULED' | 'EXEMPT' }, trial: { active: boolean, planConfirmed: boolean }, features: [{ featureKey, requires: FeatureKey[], tiers: [{ tier, unitPriceMinor | null, available: boolean, upcoming?: { amountMinor, effectiveFrom } }] }], current: [{ featureKey, tier, endsAt: date | null }], scheduled: [{ featureKey, tier | null, startsAt: date }] | null, totalMinor }` (`upcoming` per tier = an announced future price version, so the Plano tab can show the price-change note); `POST /plan/preview` → `{ changes: [{ featureKey, kind: 'ADD' | 'REMOVE' | 'TIER_CHANGE', fromTier | null, toTier | null, effectiveFrom: date }], newTotalMinor, effectiveFrom, deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid }` or the specific rejection (`422`-class problem with the code). `PUT /plan` response is defined in S19.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`GetPlanCatalogUseCase`**, 2. **`PreviewPlanSelectionUseCase`**.
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `billing.controller.ts` — `GET /billing/plan`, `POST /billing/plan/preview`.
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends the BFF billing controller — `GET /v1/billing/plan`, `POST /v1/billing/plan/preview` (`@Roles('MANAGER')`); preview body `{ items: [{ featureKey, tier }] }` (unique `featureKey`s; a duplicate is a `400` from the Zod schema). Backend routes carry the same decorators.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** error codes first thrown here (raised by the aggregate's `validateSelection` / `previewSelection` from M25-S08 and surfaced by this story's routes): `BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET`, `BILLING_FEATURE_HAS_DEPENDENTS`, `BILLING_PLAN_EMPTY`, `BILLING_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY` (default `422` each; confirm at story discovery). **Definitively absent today** (no `packages/types` member, no `errors.json` entry; `FeatureDependencyUnmetError`, `FeatureHasDependentsError` and `PlanEmptyError` exist in `billing-domain.error.ts` without a `code`): this story adds the four to `BillingErrorCode` and both `errors.json` files, gives the three domain errors a `code` (`implements DomainErrorShape`), and removes their three `error-mapper-coverage` exceptions from `architecture-policy.json`.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/get-plan-catalog.use-case.ts`, `preview-plan-selection.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/subscription.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — M25-S08 already names `validateSelection` and `previewSelection` (S17 consumes both, S19 consumes `validateSelection`); modify to add the `nextPeriodPaid` input to `previewSelection` and an "effective at payment" result (`effectiveFrom = null`, total = the one-month recovery charge) when the subscription is `SUSPENDED` for a payment reason (UC-136 A14); the dependency hints reuse the already-exported `FEATURE_REQUIREMENTS` of `plan-selection.ts`, so no new accessor)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/list-selectable-prices.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — the `upcoming` projection above; S23 reuses it)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/errors/billing-domain.error.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `code` fields and `implements DomainErrorShape` on `FeatureDependencyUnmetError`, `FeatureHasDependentsError`, `PlanEmptyError`, plus the no-price error), `packages/architecture-check/architecture-policy.json` (modify — remove the three matching `error-mapper-coverage` exceptions)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/billing.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.controller.ts`, `billing.schemas.ts`, `billing.types.ts`, `billing.mapper.ts` (+ specs), `apps/bff/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — `BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET`, `…_HAS_DEPENDENTS`, `…_PLAN_EMPTY`, `…_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY`)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts` (modify — the four members), `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json` and `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (only if needed)
- `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (modify — § Billing — Manager API, `Plan shapes`: replace the two drafted names with the final ones above (`features`, `scheduled`) and remove "shown as drafted"; nothing else in that paragraph changes; S19 adds `PUT /plan`)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A manager sees every feature with its price, what they have, what is scheduled, when each change takes effect, and a live total; an impossible selection is explained, never silently accepted.
- [ ] A newly selected feature is shown as starting at the next paid period ("começa em dd/mm"), never as instant.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Preview for add / remove / tier change with effective dates (UTC-3 and DST tenants); dependency and dependents rejections; empty selection; unavailable price (preview of a selection with a tier that has no price → `422 BILLING_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY`, `GET /plan` shows `available: false`); `upcoming` only for an announced, non-superseded future version
  - [ ] Read-only flags for `PAST_DUE`, `SUSPENDED`, scheduled cancel and `exempt`; trial reports first-paid-period semantics
  - [ ] `nextPeriodPaid = true` moves every effective date to the following period start and sets `deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid` (UC-136 A7)
  - [ ] Backend routes carry `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)` (decorator spec); the BFF routes carry no annotation (M25-S33)
- Integration:
  - [ ] Catalog and preview through the HTTP layer; preview persists nothing (row counts unchanged)
  - [ ] `STAFF`/`CUSTOMER` → `403`
- Tenant isolation: [ ] two tenants with different current and scheduled selections: each manager's `GET /plan` and `POST /plan/preview` return only their own tenant's current and scheduled items and a total computed from their own selection
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S18 — BOOKING removal guard and the `bookableUntil` window cap (UC-136 A11)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-136 (A11), `docs/BUSINESS_LOGIC_REFERENCE.md` § Planned: the billing `bookableUntil` cap and § Booking — Booking Window (M23-S33), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Choosing a race-condition primitive, and where its lock port should live, `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks and § 7. Adding or changing a lock, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Context 7 (removal guard), `docs/BOUNDED_CONTEXTS.md` § 7. Billing Context ("Billing → Booking"), § Rule 2 — Communication via Events or BFF Only, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Layering inside `application/`, `docs/ANTI_PATTERNS.md` (new cross-context Port+Adapter when one exists), `plan/M24-MULTIVERTICAL-CLASSES-SESSIONS.md` § Billing interaction (planned)
**Dependencies:** M25-S08 (`Subscription`), M25-S13 (the access answer the cap rides on — this story edits its files, listed below). The cap is *written* by S19 (`scheduleItems` produces the pending-removal item this story derives `bookableUntil` from); S19 depends on this story, not the reverse.
**Pattern:** Port + Adapter across contexts (Billing → Booking) behind **one** use case; a pure `bookableUntil` input threaded through the existing window resolver (`resolveEffectiveBookingWindow` / `assertWithinEffectiveBookingWindow`, `booking-window.helpers.ts`); Strategy-free — no new abstraction beyond the input. The race primitive is an **OPEN design item** (below), not decided here.

**Description:**
Two linked changes, both in the Booking context, so removing the BOOKING feature can never strand a commitment. **(1) The guard.** Removing `BOOKING` (which includes recurring reservations and availability alerts) is rejected while any `PENDING` / `INFO_REQUESTED` / `APPROVED` booking, `PENDING_APPROVAL` / `ACTIVE` recurring schedule or `ACTIVE` alert extends on or after the removal's **effective date** → `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE`, and the response names what blocks it. A new Booking use case `GetBookingFeatureRemovalImpactUseCase(tenantId, effectiveDate)` runs **three queries** — bookings ending on or after the date; recurring schedules with `endsOn >= date`; tenant-wide active alerts with `expiresAt >= date` — counting the occurrence bookings of an active schedule **once**, and returns counts plus a bounded list of blockers. **The `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` problem body (defined here, thrown and translated by S19):** `params: { effectiveFrom: 'YYYY-MM-DD', bookings: number, recurringSchedules: number, alerts: number, blockers: [{ category: 'BOOKING' | 'RECURRING_SCHEDULE' | 'ALERT', id, date }] }` (at most 10 blockers; ids and dates only, no customer data), so the web (S30) can name the categories and counts and link to each. Billing reaches it through **one** port `BillingBookingPort` with an adapter in `contexts/billing/infrastructure/cross-context/billing-booking.adapter.ts` registered as an exact-path `permittedEdges` entry (grep `infrastructure/cross-context/` first: this is the first Billing → Booking edge, so no existing adapter is reusable). It is read-only and never a SQL join. **When M24 lands**, class-session bookings, enrollments and waitlist entries must be counted by the same use case — the M24 story that introduces each record extends this use case's queries in the same story (stated as an AC with a recount note, and already written into `plan/M24-…`). **(2) The cap.** Once a removal is saved, the bookable window is capped at its effective date: a new optional `bookableUntil` input is honoured by **every window consumer that bounds a new commitment** (enumerated in Files from a grep of the real code) — request / authenticated / staff booking, the customer reschedule, the recurring-schedule window helpers (a new schedule whose **whole term** reaches the cap is rejected, because the guard blocks on `endsOn >= date`; the existing rule checks only the first occurrence), the availability-alert create / update validation and `selectableDays` / `bookingWindow` context, the booking-by-id reschedule options and the manifest `booking` block — so no new commitment is accepted beyond the effective date once a removal is pending. **Routes that are deliberately not capped (`docs/BUSINESS_LOGIC_REFERENCE.md` § Booking — Booking Window): availability and availability-summary reads** are untrimmed on purpose (staff and the alert sweep use the same endpoints, and `GetAvailabilitySummaryUseCase.validateRange` would throw), so `schedule-availability-summary.controller.ts` and `get-availability-summary.use-case.ts` do **not** change; the public page stays inside the cap because it takes its range from the clamped manifest value. **Staff and manager reschedules, including the M23-S08 worklist, are exempt from the booking window today (UC-069 A3)** — that exemption is why the AC below cannot say "any channel": the route list is enumerated, and **OPEN: decide at `/story-discovery` whether the cap overrides the exemption for staff / manager reschedule and the worklist** (recommended yes: a rescheduled commitment beyond the effective date is exactly what the guard counts, so leaving it exempt reintroduces the stranding; it must then be listed as a deliberate exception to the window-exempt rule). **How the web sees the cap (decided):** the backend caps the effective `maxBookingAdvanceDays` it already returns — `effective = min(configured, whole days from today to bookableUntil in the tenant timezone)` — in the manifest `booking` block and in the reschedule-options window, so the date pickers (`apps/web/app/[slug]/booking/page.tsx` reads `manifest.booking.maxBookingAdvanceDays`; `ReschedulePicker.tsx` reads `window.maxAdvanceDays`) need **no web change**. **Boundary and conversion (stated, not implied):** `bookableUntil` is the removal's effective date as a `YYYY-MM-DD` calendar day, the first day the feature no longer applies; a start is converted to its tenant-local date with `utcDateToLocalDate(startsAt, timezone)` (the helper the existing window rule uses) and is rejected when that date is `>= bookableUntil`, so the last bookable day is `bookableUntil − 1` (the existing rule's last day is `today + maxAdvanceDays − 1`, so the clamped day count is `min(configured, whole days from the tenant-local today to bookableUntil)`, floored at 0 = nothing bookable). Example: today = 10 Dec, `bookableUntil` = 15 Dec → 5 days, last bookable day 14 Dec. **Staleness (stated):** the access answer comes from `CachingSubscriptionRepository`, whose operational TTL is 300 s with **no cross-instance broadcast**, so another instance can keep returning `bookableUntil = null` for up to 300 s after the save; and the manifest's day count is a build-time snapshot served from a cache (ISR / `max-age=300`) that also drifts by one across local midnight. The picker can therefore offer a day for a few minutes that the server then rejects (the server check, reading uncached at write time, is authoritative) — the guarantee is "the server never accepts beyond the cap", not "the picker never offers one". The manifest `booking` block is built by Platform's `GetHotsiteManifestUseCase` (`mapBookingSettings`), not by the BFF; Platform reads the cap through the shared `TenantAccessPort` (no new cross-context edge), and S19 revalidates the manifest after a plan save that sets or clears a cap. **The check-then-act gap — NOT closed by the order below (Decided at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18, correcting an earlier claim that it was).** Counting under the subscription row lock is not enough: Booking's write paths do not take B-R1 (`docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks: Billing never opens a Booking transaction). The three steps below are kept because they shrink the window, but they **do not close the interleave**: **(a)** `SavePlanUseCase` (S19) commits the scheduled removal and its cap first (the scheduled item with its effective date is the pending-removal state; `bookableUntil` derives from it); **(b)** from then on Booking's new-commitment save paths re-read `bookableUntil` uncached (a cache-bypassing read of just that value, `TenantAccessPort.getBookableUntil`) immediately before saving and reject beyond it; **(c)** after (a), `SavePlanUseCase` re-runs the removal-impact guard and, if a blocker appeared, reverts the scheduled removal (a compensating transaction under B-R1) and returns `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` naming it. **The failing interleave:** a Booking transaction reads `bookableUntil = null` in (b) *before* (a) commits and saves a booking beyond the date; (a) commits; (c) recounts and sees nothing, because the booking transaction has not committed yet; the booking transaction then commits. Both checks ran and the booking is stranded. Steps (b) and (c) each close only the case where the other side has already committed. **OPEN: decide at `/story-discovery` the primitive that closes it** (`docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Choosing a race-condition primitive: no row exists to lock for a booking about to be created, so the candidates are primitive 3 or an explicit acceptance), recorded with its options and not invented here: **Option A** — both transactions take one per-tenant advisory lock keyed by a shared helper (the plan save's guard-and-commit, and every new-commitment save path in Booking), with the lock port started in the context that owns the race and its key namespaced from day one (`docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md`: a key cannot change once it has protected production traffic); **Option B** — accept bounded stranding: the interleave window is the length of one Booking transaction, and a reconciliation sweep reports any commitment that extends past an effective date after it has passed (operator-visible), with the product consequence stated. **Whichever is chosen the story ships:** a `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` entry (a row in § Billing locks and its place in § 2 / § 4 lock order relative to B-R1 and Booking's locks, per § 7) and a deterministic **interleave integration test** (hold the Booking transaction between its cap read and its commit, run the plan save and its recount, release it, and assert the outcome the chosen option promises: Option A — the second side waits and sees the first's effect; Option B — the stranded booking is reported). **How Booking learns the cap — decided here: `bookableUntil` rides on the shared `TenantAccessPort` answer** (the access answer is already cached and read by every consumer; the story updates `get-tenant-access.use-case.ts`, the caching adapter, the BFF access mapper and DTO, and `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Entry point: `TenantAccessPort` in the same change). The alternative — a Booking-owned projection updated from `PlanChanged` — is recorded and rejected for adding an event consumer, a table and a second source of truth; story discovery may revisit it only if the cached answer proves too coarse. **Trial case:** during a trial every feature stays active and the selection only fixes the first paid period, so a selection that omits `BOOKING` is not a "pending removal item" with an `endsAt` but the **absence** of a scheduled `BOOKING` item from the first paid period; `bookableUntil` is then that period's start (the trial end), and its derivation (a pure function in the use case, unit-tested) must cover both shapes: a scheduled removal of a running item, and a first-paid selection without `BOOKING`. The cap is not expressible as "days", hence a date input to the window resolver. **Non-regression contract (M26 backward-compatibility owner):** with no cap set — every tenant today — every window consumer returns exactly what it returned before.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`GetBookingFeatureRemovalImpactUseCase`** (Booking), 2. the cap input threaded through `resolveEffectiveBookingWindow` / `assertWithinEffectiveBookingWindow` (`booking-window.helpers.ts`) and its consumers.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none new — the guard is called in-process by S19; the cap affects existing routes' results only. (If the `bookableUntil` source needs an internal read endpoint, story discovery adds `GET /internal/tenants/{tenantId}/booking-cap`; not assumed.)
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A tenant cannot remove BOOKING while it has live bookings, recurring schedules or alerts reaching the removal date, and is told exactly what blocks it.
- [ ] After a removal is saved, no new booking, recurring schedule or alert can be created beyond the effective date through any enumerated route: `POST /bookings`, `POST /bookings/authenticated`, `POST /bookings/staff`, the customer reschedule, the recurring-schedule request, alert create / update, and (per the OPEN decision on the window-exempt routes) the staff / manager reschedule and the worklist; the story's discovery lists every route and each has a test.
- [ ] A tenant with no scheduled removal sees no change in any booking window.
- [ ] Decision recorded: the cap rides on the shared access answer (cached) plus one uncached read at save time; the alternative (a Booking-owned projection fed by `PlanChanged`) was rejected for adding a consumer, a table and a second source of truth.
- [ ] The race primitive (or the explicit acceptance of bounded stranding) is decided at discovery and recorded in `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md`; the earlier claim that the three-step order closes the race is not repeated anywhere.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] The three queries' boundary (a booking ending exactly on the effective date blocks; one ending the day before does not); occurrence bookings of an active schedule counted once
  - [ ] Window resolver: `bookableUntil` clamps each consumer; `null` equals today's result (table-driven across all consumers)
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Non-regression:** the existing window / availability / recurring / alert integration suites pass unchanged, plus a test that a `null` cap equals today's result
  - [ ] **Race after the cap commits:** a booking, recurring schedule or alert created after the scheduled removal committed but before the cached access answer expires is rejected beyond the effective date (Booking's save re-reads `bookableUntil` uncached; a second backend instance holding a stale cached answer still rejects)
  - [ ] **Race before the cap commits, the case the recount catches:** a booking beyond the effective date that **committed** after the guard's first count but before the plan-save commit is caught by the post-commit re-count, the scheduled removal is reverted and `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` is returned (test in S19's integration suite using this story's use case)
  - [ ] **Interleave (the case the order alone does not close):** a Booking transaction that read `bookableUntil = null` before the plan save commits and commits after the recount — the outcome promised by the primitive chosen at discovery (Option A: serialised; Option B: reported as stranded), deterministic, with the concurrent integration test and mutation check that `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § 7. Adding or changing a lock requires
  - [ ] Boundary: a start on `bookableUntil − 1` (tenant-local, including a UTC instant that falls on the next UTC day) is accepted and one on `bookableUntil` is rejected; the clamped manifest day count matches; a first-paid selection without `BOOKING` during a trial yields `bookableUntil` = the trial end
  - [ ] The availability and availability-summary reads are unchanged by a cap (untrimmed), and a recurring-schedule request whose term reaches the cap is rejected
  - [ ] The manifest `booking.maxBookingAdvanceDays` and the reschedule-options `window.maxAdvanceDays` are clamped to the whole days until `bookableUntil` in the tenant timezone, and unchanged when it is `null`
  - [ ] End to end: a tenant with an `APPROVED` booking past the date → `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` with the blocker named; after the booking is cancelled the guard passes
  - [ ] The adapter is the only Billing → Booking import (`architecture-check` cross-context detector passes with the one new exact-path entry)
- Tenant isolation: [ ] counts are strictly per `tenant_id`; another tenant's bookings never block this tenant
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S19 — Plan save and trial confirm (UC-136, UC-130)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-136 (main flow 3, A1–A13), UC-130 (first charge), UC-113 (A10), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Manager API (`PUT /plan`, `POST /plan/confirm`), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Subscription (scheduling, `plan_confirmed_at`), `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-R1 order), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built) and § Transactions
**Dependencies:** M26-S14 (creates the backend `billing.controller.ts`, both `billing.http` files and the BFF billing types this story extends), M26-S02 (`RecoveryChargeFactory`, for the suspended branch), M26-S05 (`IssueChargeForSubscriptionUseCase` and the provider-creation claim), M26-S06 (`CancelOpenChargesUseCase`, the recovery/provider-cancel steps and `provider_cancel_pending`), M26-S16 (address completeness), M26-S17 (selection validation, preview, the `nextPeriodPaid` rule and the first throw of `BILLING_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY`), M26-S18 (removal guard port, the cap and the uncached read), M26-S03 (`PlanChanged` event), M26-S04 (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase` for the "just paid" path)
**Pattern:** Command use case with B-R1 (subscription row lock) → charge rows; cancel-and-re-issue after commit with provider I/O outside the transaction (M26-D3).

**Description:**
Persist the manager's plan choice. **`PUT /v1/billing/plan`** `{ items: [{ featureKey, tier }] }`: under `findByTenantIdForUpdate` it validates the proposed **final dependency graph** (A1), non-empty (A5), a list price exists for the country/tier (A6, `BILLING_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY`, thrown first by S17's preview and reused here), the full billing address is saved (A10, `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`), the subscription state allows it (`PAST_DUE`, an `OPERATOR` suspension and a scheduled cancel are rejected as read-only — A2/A4; a payment-based suspension is allowed and takes the **suspended branch** described below — A14; trial: all features stay active, the selection only fixes the first paid period — A3). **Where the BOOKING removal guard runs relative to B-R1 (stated):** the guard is a read-only Booking query reached through S18's `BillingBookingPort`, and Billing never opens a Booking transaction nor holds B-R1 across a Booking read (`docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks), so it runs **outside** the B-R1 transaction — once before it opens (the pre-check that keeps the common rejection write-free) and once after the commit (S18's recount, which reverts the scheduled removal under a new, short B-R1 transaction if a blocker appeared); every other validation above stays inside the B-R1 transaction. The race this leaves open is S18's OPEN design item, not closed here. It then **schedules** the item / tier changes at the next paid service-period start (current entitlements are unchanged) and publishes `PlanChanged`. If the next invoice is already issued and **unpaid**, it cancels and re-issues it with the provider call **after commit**. **In one transaction (under B-R1):** the old charge goes `CANCELED` with `provider_cancel_pending = true` (when it has a provider id), **and the replacement `PERIOD` charge is inserted `PENDING` with the same `period_start`, the new lines / amount (price versions in force on that period's start) and `provider_creation_status = PENDING`, without advancing `next_due_date`** (it was already advanced when the original was issued; `UQ_billing_charges_period` ignores cancelled rows, so the same `period_start` is legal). Nothing is ever left without a charge for the period, so a crash after commit loses nothing: S05's creation retry creates the replacement at the provider (A8, alert on repeated failure). **After commit, outside any transaction:** `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` (S06) cancels the old charge at the provider and clears `provider_cancel_pending` on success (S07 retries it otherwise), then the replacement is claimed and created at the provider through S05's creation step (M26-D3 / the S05 claim), raising `ChargeIssued` after `READY`. If the provider refuses to cancel the old charge because it was just paid (typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`, A12), that old charge **is the payment** — it is applied through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` (S04; the local `CANCELED` is legally revived by a provider-confirmed paid fact), the replacement is cancelled locally, the selection is **re-dated**: the scheduled items that were set to start at that period's start are moved to start at the following period start (a second, short B-R1 transaction; `previewSelection`'s `nextPeriodPaid` rule, S17) and the response says the change applies from the following renewal. A **paid** invoice found at save time is treated the same way without any provider call: items start at the period after the paid one (A7). A cancel of the old charge racing an in-flight provider creation is handled where the READY step lives (S05 / `Charge.markCreationReady`, S02): storing ids on a `CANCELED` charge sets `provider_cancel_pending`, so the orphan provider charge is cancelled by S07's retry; this story owns the race test (below). **Suspended branch (UC-136 A14, decided 2026-11-16: a suspended manager may choose a smaller plan before paying).** For a subscription `SUSPENDED` for `NON_PAYMENT`, `PAYMENT_REVERSED` or `CANCELED`, nothing is entitled, so the selection is **not scheduled**: under B-R1 the aggregate replaces the plan's items outright (the new open-ended items start at the save moment; entitlement stays none until the status changes) and the use case then (a) for `NON_PAYMENT` / `PAYMENT_REVERSED`, cancels the open `RECOVERY` charge and inserts its replacement `PENDING` in the **same transaction** through S02's `RecoveryChargeFactory` (new lines and amount from the price versions in force that day, one-month, `UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery` respected), with the provider cancel and creation **after commit** exactly like the next-period re-issue above (a provider failure keeps the selection and S05's retry creates the replacement, A8); (b) for `CANCELED`, issues nothing (no charge is open: S20's `reactivate` issues the recovery charge from the saved selection). If the provider refuses to cancel the old recovery charge because it was just paid (`ChargeAlreadyPaidError`), that charge is the payment (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase`) and reactivates the subscription on the plan it was issued for; the selection just saved is **re-dated** into an ordinary next-period change (the same second short B-R1 transaction as A12) and the response says so; a payment that lands later on the cancelled charge takes the same path. `PlanChanged` carries `effectiveFrom = null`. The BOOKING removal guard counts commitments from the save moment. An `OPERATOR` suspension is rejected `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` (the operator alone lifts it, so the plan could not be paid for). **`POST /v1/billing/plan/confirm`** (trial only): confirms the current default selection unchanged, sets `plan_confirmed_at` and returns `200 { planConfirmedAt }`; `409 BILLING_PLAN_ALREADY_CONFIRMED` on a confirmed trial (A13); on a subscription that is not `TRIALING` (active, past due, suspended, exempt) `409 BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`; when the trial end is within the 10-day lead it triggers first-invoice issuing through the same per-subscription use case after commit. During the trial, `PUT /plan` also sets `plan_confirmed_at` (the same action, labelled "Confirmar plano"). **Other save-time rules:** `exempt` subscriptions are rejected `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` (UC-117 A7); the access cache needs no explicit call (`CachingSubscriptionRepository.save()` evicts after commit) while `bookableUntil` rides on the access answer (S18), and when the save sets or clears a BOOKING cap the hotsite manifest is revalidated best-effort after commit (through the existing frontend-revalidation port; its `booking` block carries the clamped window); re-saving replaces any previously scheduled, not-yet-started items of the same features through the aggregate's `replaceScheduledItems` (a `SubscriptionItem` rejects `endsAt <= startsAt`, so a scheduled item that has not started cannot be "ended" — it is removed and the new one inserted; the GIST exclusion is the backstop). **Response (`PUT /plan`, defined here, written into `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`):** `200 { planConfirmedAt: string | null, effectiveFrom: 'YYYY-MM-DD', deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid: boolean, newTotalMinor: number, openCharge: { id, amountMinor, status, hasPaymentLink } | null }`. **Rejection statuses (defaults, confirmed at story discovery):** `BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET`, `…_HAS_DEPENDENTS`, `…_PLAN_EMPTY`, `…_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY` → `422`; `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` → `409` (params per S18); `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` → `409`; `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` → `422` (code owned by S15); `BILLING_PLAN_ALREADY_CONFIRMED` → `409`.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`SavePlanUseCase`**, 2. **`ConfirmPlanUseCase`**; both end with the after-commit provider re-issue/issue hook. `SavePlanUseCase` runs S18's order for a BOOKING removal: guard (outside B-R1), commit the scheduled removal, post-commit re-count with revert on a new blocker, plus whatever primitive S18's discovery chooses for the interleave.
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `billing.controller.ts` — `PUT /billing/plan`, `POST /billing/plan/confirm`.
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends the BFF billing controller — `PUT /v1/billing/plan` (body as preview), `POST /v1/billing/plan/confirm` (`@Roles('MANAGER')`); the backend routes carry the same decorators.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** error codes first thrown here, each added to `BillingErrorCode` and both `errors.json` files: `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE`, `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BILLING_PLAN_ALREADY_CONFIRMED`. `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` is S15's; `BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET` / `…_HAS_DEPENDENTS` / `…_PLAN_EMPTY` / `…_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY` are S17's (this story reuses them).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/save-plan.use-case.ts`, `confirm-plan.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/subscription.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `scheduleItems`, `replaceScheduledItems` (removes the not-yet-started scheduled items of the changed features and inserts the new ones, unit-tested with a scheduled item that has not started, which `endedAt` cannot end), `confirmPlan` and `replaceSelectionWhileSuspended` (the A14 branch: end-dates the open items at the save moment, discards every future-dated scheduled item, inserts the new open-ended items from the save moment; touching windows are allowed and the GIST exclusion is the backstop, like `setExempt`; rejected unless the status is `SUSPENDED` with reason `NON_PAYMENT`, `PAYMENT_REVERSED` or `CANCELED`))
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — plan rejection errors)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts`, `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify — the three codes above), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (only if needed)
- `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (modify — `PUT /plan` and `POST /plan/confirm` response shapes and rejection statuses)
- `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (modify — name the plan save × issue job race test)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/billing.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.controller.ts`, `billing.schemas.ts`, `billing.types.ts`, `billing.mapper.ts` (+ specs), `apps/bff/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A manager can change the next-period plan; the change starts at the next paid period, current access is unchanged, and an unpaid next invoice is replaced with the new amount.
- [ ] A manager whose account is suspended for non-payment, a reversed payment or a cancellation can save a smaller plan **before paying**: the recovery charge is re-issued at the new amount, paying it reactivates the account on the saved plan, and no months are owed for the gap (an operator suspension still cannot edit the plan).
- [ ] During the trial, "Confirmar plano" fixes the first invoice's content; confirming twice is refused; an unconfirmed plan is invoiced from the default selection three days before the end (S05), which the banner and emails say.
- [ ] Removing BOOKING is refused with the blocker named; an empty plan, an unmet dependency and a missing address are refused with their specific messages.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Every charge this use case changes is loaded with `findByIdForUpdate` (lock B-C1, after B-R1) first, and it never calls `markPaid` (S02 discovery)
  - [ ] Each of A1–A13 (one named test each), including trial semantics
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-R1):** a plan save racing the issue job (S05) on the same subscription serialises — exactly one charge for the period carrying the final selection
  - [ ] Unpaid next invoice cancelled and re-issued with the new amount on the fake; the old one stays as `CANCELED` history; **the cancel and the replacement `PENDING` charge (same `period_start`, no `next_due_date` advance) commit in one transaction** — a simulated crash right after commit leaves a `PENDING` replacement that S05's retry creates (no period without a charge); provider failure keeps the schedule and the retry fixes it
  - [ ] "Refuses to cancel — already paid" (fake `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`): the old charge is applied as the payment, the replacement is cancelled, the scheduled items are re-dated to the following period start and the response says `deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid`
  - [ ] **Race:** a plan save cancelling a charge whose provider creation is in flight leaves the orphan provider charge marked `provider_cancel_pending` and cancelled by S07's retry
  - [ ] **Pre-commit booking race (S18):** a booking beyond the effective date that **committed** between the guard's count and the save commit → post-commit re-count reverts the scheduled removal and returns `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` (the uncommitted-booking interleave is S18's OPEN design item and its own test)
  - [ ] The guard is called outside the B-R1 transaction (no Booking read while the subscription row lock is held)
  - [ ] `POST /plan/confirm` on `ACTIVE`, `PAST_DUE`, `SUSPENDED` and `exempt` → `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`; a preview and a save of the same selection agree when a scheduled future item already exists (`selection` includes it)
  - [ ] `exempt` → `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`; the access cache is invalidated after commit and the manifest revalidated when a cap is set or cleared
  - [ ] Backend routes carry `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)` (decorator spec); the BFF routes carry no annotation (M25-S33)
  - [ ] **Suspended branch (A14):** `NON_PAYMENT` with an open recovery charge → items replaced, old recovery charge `CANCELED` with `provider_cancel_pending` and a `PENDING` replacement at the new amount in one transaction, exactly one open recovery charge afterwards (concurrency: a save racing the sweep / a reactivate under B-R1); `CANCELED` → items replaced and no charge touched, then S20's reactivate prices the recovery charge from them; `OPERATOR` suspension and `PAST_DUE` → `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`; `ChargeAlreadyPaidError` → the old charge is the payment and the selection is re-dated to the next period; the access answer stays `operational: false` until payment
  - [ ] Paid invoice never changed; `PlanChanged` published once per save that changes the selection (`added[]` / `removed[]` relative to the previously effective-or-scheduled selection); `POST /plan/confirm` of the untouched default publishes **no** `PlanChanged` (nothing changed); confirm idempotency (second → 409)
  - [ ] No provider I/O inside the transaction (`transactional-io`)
- Tenant isolation: [ ] a save affects only the caller's tenant's items and charges
- E2E: none — covered by integration; the tab E2E is S35
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S20 — Cancel and reactivate (UC-133, UC-134)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-133 and UC-134 (all), UC-114 (step 4), UC-115 (A3), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Manager API (`cancel`, `reactivate`), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Subscription and § Aggregate: Charge (recovery charge), `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-R1; the open-recovery unique), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built)
**Dependencies:** M25-S15b (the operator touchpoints, `SetTenantExemptUseCase`), M26-S02 (recovery factory, charges), M26-S03 (`SubscriptionCancelled`), M26-S04 (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase` for the already-paid case), M26-S05 (the provider-creation step and claim, reached through S06), M26-S06 (suspension and recovery behaviour it must agree with, **and `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` / the recovery provider-creation step it reuses**), M26-S13 (`SeedBillingStatesUseCase`, extended here with the two cancel states)
**Pattern:** Two command use cases under B-R1; the shared `RecoveryChargeFactory` (given resolved unit prices by the use case) and S06's `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`; PENDING → READY creation for the recovery charge (M26-D3).

**Description:**
**Cancel** (`POST /v1/billing/cancel`, any manager; nothing is refunded): `ACTIVE` → set `cancelAt` = **the end of the last paid period**, computed as `max(period_end)` over the subscription's `PAID` `PERIOD` charges, falling back to `current_period_end` (invoices are issued 10 days early, so the next period may already be paid and `current_period_end` alone would cut off a paid month); cancel immediately every unpaid charge for a period beginning on or after that date (locally with `provider_cancel_pending`, the provider call after commit through `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`, S06), response `200 { cancelAt }`; `PAST_DUE` → immediate: cancel any open charge and become `SUSPENDED` (`CANCELED`), `200 { status: "SUSPENDED", cancelAt: null }`; `TRIALING` with the first `PERIOD` charge already `PAID` → `cancelAt` = **the end of that first paid period** (the same `max(period_end)` rule; access continues through the trial and the paid month, nothing is refunded, and the sweep's trial → `ACTIVE` conversion still happens at trial end, the cancel then takes effect at period end — owner decision R5; a `cancelAt = trialEndsAt` would cut off the month the customer paid for); `TRIALING` with nothing paid → immediate suspension (A3, canonical). Already cancelling → `409 BILLING_ALREADY_CANCELLING` (A1; default status `409`). A `SUSPENDED` or `exempt` subscription cannot be cancelled: `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED` (default `409`; new code owned here — nothing to cancel). If the provider cancel fails, the local state is saved and reconcile (S07) retries — the manager sees success only after the local save (A2). `SubscriptionCancelled` on every cancel (emailed to the billing email by S12 — and, for an immediate cancel, the `SubscriptionSuspended` (CANCELED) manager email is suppressed there so the manager gets one email); `SubscriptionSuspended` for the immediate cases; the access cache is invalidated after commit. **Reactivate** (`POST /v1/billing/reactivate`): not allowed for an `OPERATOR` suspension (only the operator lifts it; `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED`, default `409`, new code owned here — distinct from the operator route's `BILLING_UNSUSPEND_NOT_ALLOWED`) or after the data is purged (a new signup is needed, UC-134 A1; same code) — **the purged case cannot be produced or tested before M27 (no purge exists in M26)**, so M26 ships no purged-tenant branch or test and M27's purge story adds the branch and its test (G11). Reactivate on a subscription that is neither cancelling nor `SUSPENDED` (nothing to reactivate) → `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED` too. **Within the paid period, `clearCancel` clears `cancelAt` and restores billing for the period it had cancelled:** cancel cancelled any unpaid charge for a period beginning on or after `cancelAt`, but `next_due_date` had already been advanced past it when that charge was issued, so the issue job would never select that period again; `clearCancel` therefore re-creates the cancelled period's `PERIOD` charge in the same transaction (`PENDING`, same `period_start`, lines at the versions in force on that period's start, no `next_due_date` advance; the provider creation follows after commit through S05's claim) when that period is within the issue lead or already issued, and otherwise simply leaves `next_due_date` for the issue job. If `SUSPENDED` (`NON_PAYMENT`, `PAYMENT_REVERSED` or `CANCELED`): cancel any open `PERIOD` charge (locally and at the provider, never re-dated) and issue **one** `RECOVERY` charge for one month through S02's factory — amount fixed at issue from the versions in force that day, **lines = the selected plan: the current items, or the selection the manager saved while suspended (UC-136 A14, S19's suspended branch; for a `CANCELED` suspension this is the only moment the recovery charge is priced)** — `PENDING` then `READY` after the provider call (outside the transaction, through the shared creation step of S05 / S06 with its claim; provider failure → `BILLING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (default `503`) with retry, A3). **A manager reactivate with an incomplete billing identity or address returns `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` (default `422`, code owned by S15) and creates and cancels nothing** (UC-134 A6 — nobody is "away" here, the manager can fix it now; the only held recovery charge is the one the status sweep creates, S06); response `200 { cancelAt: null, openCharge: { …, kind: "RECOVERY" } | null }` — **never a URL** (the web then calls `payment-link`). Calling reactivate again while a recovery charge is open returns that charge (the unique makes it exactly one). If the provider refuses to cancel the old charge because it was just paid, **that charge is the payment** and reactivates the subscription through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`; the recovery charge is cancelled (A4). On payment (S04) the status becomes `ACTIVE`, the period starts at the payment, `anchor_day` is the payment's tenant-local day clamped to 28, and no months are owed for the gap. **Open-charge cancellation is one shared use case, `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`, built in S06** (local `CANCELED` + `provider_cancel_pending` in the caller's transaction, provider cancel after commit, S07 retries; a provider that reports the charge already paid goes through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`) — this story, S19 and **the operator's exempt-set hook** call it, and does not rebuild it. A cancel racing an in-flight provider creation is handled where the READY step lives (a `CANCELED` charge that receives provider ids gets `provider_cancel_pending`). M25-S15b's `SetTenantExemptUseCase` left a no-op hook for "exempt cancels any open charge locally and at the provider" (UC-137 step 1) and this story fills it in `set-tenant-exempt.use-case.ts`.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`CancelSubscriptionUseCase`**, 2. **`ReactivateSubscriptionUseCase`**.
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `billing.controller.ts` — `POST /billing/cancel`, `POST /billing/reactivate`.
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends the BFF billing controller — `POST /v1/billing/cancel`, `POST /v1/billing/reactivate` (`@Roles('MANAGER')`); no request body. The backend routes carry the same decorators.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** error codes first thrown here, each added to `BillingErrorCode` and both `errors.json` files: `BILLING_ALREADY_CANCELLING`, `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED`. `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` and `BILLING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` are S15's. `docs/ERROR_CATALOG.md` § `BILLING_*` already lists `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED` and `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED` (both `409`; status confirmed at discovery), so no catalog edit.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/cancel-subscription.use-case.ts`, `reactivate-subscription.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new; `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` is S06's)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts`, `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify — the three codes), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (only if needed)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/seed-billing-states.use-case.ts` (+ specs) (modify — S13 created it; add the two states that need a cancel: `ACTIVE` with a scheduled cancel and `SUSPENDED` for `CANCELED`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/set-tenant-exempt.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — M25-S15b created it and left the documented no-op hook (M25 S15b: "in M25 no charge can exist, so this is a no-op hook that M26-S20 fills in"); fill it by calling S06's `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`: setting `exempt` cancels every open charge locally and at the provider, and publishes no email-triggering event)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify)
- `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (modify — name the reactivate and cancel × webhook race tests)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/subscription.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `scheduleCancel` and `clearCancel`; `suspend(reason)` already exists from S04 (`CANCELED` is one of its reasons), so nothing is added for suspend reasons; `clearCancel` also restores the cancelled period's charge as described above, which the aggregate signals and the use case performs)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/billing.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.controller.ts`, `billing.schemas.ts`, `billing.types.ts`, `billing.mapper.ts` (+ specs), `apps/bff/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A manager can cancel (access continues to the paid period end), can undo a scheduled cancel, and can reactivate a suspended or cancelled subscription by paying one month; nothing is refunded and no months are owed for the gap.
- [ ] An unpaid trial or a past-due tenant that cancels is suspended at once, with wording that nothing was paid.
- [ ] A suspension by the operator cannot be lifted by the manager.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Every charge cancel or recovery write is loaded with `findByIdForUpdate` (lock B-C1, after B-R1) first, and neither path calls `markPaid` (S02 discovery)
  - [ ] Cancel per status (A1–A4 and the paid-trial rule); reactivate per reason (A1–A4); recovery amount from today's versions; `anchor_day` clamp to 28
  - [ ] `cancelAt` = `max(period_end)` of `PAID` `PERIOD` charges: with the next period's invoice already `PAID` it is that period's end, not `current_period_end`; a `TRIALING` subscription whose first charge is `PAID` gets `cancelAt` = that first period's end (not `trialEndsAt`)
  - [ ] Error cases: cancel on `SUSPENDED` / `exempt` → `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED`; reactivate on an `OPERATOR` suspension or with nothing to reactivate → `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED` (the purged-tenant case arrives with M27); reactivate with no billing address → `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` and no charge created or cancelled
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Concurrency (`UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery`):** two simultaneous reactivates produce one open recovery charge and both responses carry it
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-R1):** a cancel racing a webhook payment serialises; a payment arriving first leaves the subscription `ACTIVE` with a scheduled cancel only if requested after
  - [ ] Provider cancel failure → local state saved, reconcile later; provider create failure on reactivate → `BILLING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, charge `PENDING`, retry covered by S05's retry query
  - [ ] "Old charge already paid" path reactivates without a recovery charge
  - [ ] **Cancel then reactivate inside the paid period** with the next period's invoice already issued and cancelled: `clearCancel` re-creates that period's charge (same `period_start`, no `next_due_date` advance) — the period is invoiced exactly once
  - [ ] **Race:** a cancel of a charge whose provider creation is in flight leaves the orphan provider charge marked `provider_cancel_pending` and cancelled by S07's retry
  - [ ] A sweep-held recovery charge (S06) is created at the provider by S05's retry once the address is saved, and a manager `reactivate` meanwhile returns that same open charge (no second recovery charge)
  - [ ] Backend routes carry `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)` (decorator spec); the BFF routes carry no annotation (M25-S33)
  - [ ] `seed` now also creates `ACTIVE` with a scheduled cancel and `SUSPENDED` for `CANCELED`, through the normal cancel use case
  - [ ] Setting a tenant `exempt` (operator) cancels its open charge locally and at the provider through `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` (UC-137 step 1)
  - [ ] No provider call inside a transaction (`transactional-io` passes)
  - [ ] Events: `SubscriptionCancelled`/`SubscriptionSuspended`/`ChargeIssued` exactly as documented
- Tenant isolation: [ ] actions affect only the caller's tenant; `STAFF`/`CUSTOMER` → `403`
- E2E: none — covered by integration; the pages' E2E is in S35
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S21 — Chatbot tier → model and caps mapping (Platform)

**Agent:** `backend-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` § chatbot ("Tier source" and the cap fields), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Entry point: `TenantAccessPort` (`features: [{ key: 'CHATBOT', tier }]`), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § OpenRouter chatbot outbound HTTP resilience (the retry / `provider.ignore` / `reasoning` / `provider.sort` / timeout rules) and § Billing (partly built), `docs/USE_CASES.md` — the chatbot **settings** use cases ("Configure chatbot module", UC-027 Section B / UC-026 chatbot section; UC-033 / UC-034 are the guest chatbot use cases), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Schema-level enforcement, M25-S02 (the validated Plus model and caps)
**Dependencies:** M25-S02 (settled Plus model, caps and OpenRouter validation), M25-S13 (the access answer carries the CHATBOT tier), M25-S17 (the chatbot routes' `@RequiresFeature(CHATBOT)` gates, which this story's "inert" rule complements)
**Pattern:** A pure mapping `chatbotConfigForTier(tier)` in code constants (never in Billing), consumed by the chatbot use cases; the tier read goes through the shared `TenantAccessPort`.

**Description:**
The chatbot becomes a tiered, billed feature (`CHATBOT`, `BASIC` / `PLUS`; display names Básico / Plus). Billing stores only the tier on the subscription item; **Platform maps tier → model + caps in code constants** (`apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/chatbot.constants.ts`, never in Billing). From now on the per-tenant `llmProvider`, `llmModel` and the cap fields are **no longer a manual-override path: they are derived from the tier** — the "direct database update or ad hoc script" path described in the settings Validation Rules is removed by this story, and it **must precede the Plano tab (S30)**. `BASIC` keeps today's code defaults exactly (the `DEFAULT_*` caps in `chatbot.constants.ts` and the current OpenRouter model, which is `DEFAULT_OPENROUTER_MODEL = 'deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731'`, today a **non-exported** `const` inside `infrastructure/llm/openrouter-llm.adapter.ts` — this story exports it and the tier table references it rather than duplicating the id, so `BASIC` stays the adapter's own default), so existing behaviour does not change; `PLUS` is defined by the caps and behaviour M25-S02 validated, so its model can change without repricing (the tier table, `BASIC` and `PLUS` on the same caps with `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5` for `PLUS`, is in `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` § 7; **only the prefixed form is an OpenRouter slug**, so the unprefixed `claude-haiku-5.5` must not appear in code; **the tier table carries a `provider` field beside the model slug**: a slug is meaningful only to its provider, so `PLUS` names `openrouter` (`OPENROUTER_PROVIDER_NAME`) with `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`, while `BASIC` leaves `provider` unset = the platform's configured `CHATBOT_LLM_PROVIDER`, exactly today's behaviour and the `fake` provider the E2E stack uses; S21 also decides the spend-breaker value `CHATBOT_GLOBAL_DAILY_SPEND_LIMIT_USD`, a `BASIC` provider price ceiling (`max_price`, **OPEN**, below) and whether a per-tenant monthly bound is needed, as recorded there). **A per-request price ceiling needs a port change:** the adapter's `max_price` is a fixed constant in `OPENROUTER_PROVIDER_PREFERENCES` (US$ 10 / 50, `openrouter-llm.adapter.ts`) and `ChatCompletionRequest` (`llm-provider.port.ts`) has only an optional `model`, so a tier-specific ceiling means a new optional `maxPrice?: { prompt: number; completion: number }` on `ChatCompletionRequest`, which the OpenRouter adapter uses in place of the constant when set (the other adapters ignore it); `PLUS` leaves it unset. A trial tenant gets `BASIC`; a tenant that loses the entitlement (suspended, or `CHATBOT` not in its plan) keeps its `knowledgeText` but the feature is **inert** until entitled again. `knowledgeText` and `maxKnowledgeTextLength` are unaffected. **The Plus `knowledgeText` length is unaffected: `maxKnowledgeTextLength` stays fixed for every tier** (the settings doc says so; M25-S02's mention of a Plus `knowledgeText` length is corrected by its owner), so the tier tables carry only the conversation / message / output-token / history caps and the model. **Fallbacks:** when the access answer has no `CHATBOT` item and the tenant is not entitled → inert; when the access answer fails open (transient lookup error) the tier resolves to `BASIC`, never to an error. Entitlement is already refused at the HTTP choke point (M25-S17 annotates the chatbot routes `@RequiresFeature(CHATBOT)`), so "inert" here means only that `ResolveChatbotConfigUseCase` returns a not-entitled result the use cases honour (no LLM call, no counters touched) for internal callers that bypass the route gate; the public widget sees the typed refusal the gate returns. The `ChatbotSettingsSchema` (in `packages/validation/src/tenant-settings.ts`, not the BFF) keeps rejecting the cap and provider fields (`.strict()`), unchanged. Stored per-tenant `llmProvider` / `llmModel` / cap values are **ignored** after this story (derived values win) — a one-time inventory shows no production tenant has an override (pre-production, none can exist; the story still logs and counts any it finds in local / staging).

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`ResolveChatbotConfigUseCase`** (tier from the access port → constants), consumed by `SendChatMessageUseCase`, the cap checks (`chatbot-cap-check.helpers.ts`, `get-chatbot-cap-status.use-case.ts`, `get-chatbot-status.use-case.ts`) and the chatbot settings reads.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none new — existing chatbot and settings routes return derived values.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/chatbot.constants.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `CHATBOT_TIER_CONFIG`, each entry carrying an optional `provider` and a `model`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/resolve-chatbot-config.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/chatbot-cap-check.helpers.ts`, `get-chatbot-cap-status.use-case.ts` (+ specs) (modify — the `chatbotSettings.X ?? DEFAULT_X` cap reads; they take the tier-derived values. Only **seven of the eight** `DEFAULT_*` chatbot constants are tier-mapped: `DEFAULT_MAX_KNOWLEDGE_TEXT_LENGTH` stays fixed for every tier. The same `?? DEFAULT_` reads also live in `send-chat-message.use-case.ts` and `get-chatbot-status.use-case.ts`, which take the derived values too), `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/llm/openrouter-llm.adapter.ts` (+ spec) (modify — export `DEFAULT_OPENROUTER_MODEL` for the `BASIC` entry and honour a per-request `maxPrice` in place of the fixed `max_price` constant), `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/ports/llm-provider.port.ts` (modify — the optional `maxPrice` on `ChatCompletionRequest`), `apps/backend/src/shared/value-objects/tenant-settings-data.ts` (+ spec) (modify — the legacy cap / provider fields stay readable but are ignored and counted)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/send-chat-message.use-case.ts`, `get-chatbot-status.use-case.ts`, `get-tenant-by-id.use-case.ts`, `update-tenant-settings.use-case.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/value-objects/tenant-settings.vo.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/cross-context/platform-tenant-settings.adapter.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/services/llm-provider-registry.service.ts` (+ specs) (modify — the verified readers of `llmModel` / `llmProvider`; they take the derived values)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/value-objects/validators/chatbot-settings.validator.ts` (+ spec) (modify)
- `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` (modify — the chatbot "Tier source" paragraph loses "planned" once built)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A Básico tenant's chatbot behaves exactly as today; a Plus tenant gets the Plus model on the same caps as Básico; a tenant without the CHATBOT feature (or suspended) gets an inert chatbot that resumes, with its knowledge text intact, when entitled again.
- [ ] **OPEN: decide at `/story-discovery` (the `BASIC` price ceiling).** Recorded at M25-S02: `BASIC`'s measured worst case is about 95% of R$ 39 at the most expensive eligible endpoint, because the adapter's `max_price` (US$ 10 / 50) admits every DeepSeek flash provider (`docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` § 7, Chatbot Settings). Decide a tier-specific provider price ceiling for `BASIC`: about US$ 0.15 input / 0.50 output per million tokens leaves about 19 endpoints and, at list prices, brings the worst case to about 33% of R$ 39; confirm availability and speed against the live endpoint list before adopting it. This deliberately changes `BASIC`'s routing, so it is the one exception to "behaves exactly as today" above; its model, caps and answers stay the same. **Options:** (a) ship `BASIC` with the ceiling (the per-request `maxPrice` above) after confirming endpoint availability and speed; (b) keep today's `max_price` and **accept explicitly** that the observed-routing bound is 35.4% of R$ 39 against `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md`'s "at most 35%" rule (and about 95% at the highest eligible endpoint), recording the breach there. The story must not ship without one of the two written down; neither is chosen here. Also decide whether `CHATBOT_GLOBAL_DAILY_SPEND_LIMIT_USD` (US$ 1 a day, platform-wide) changes, and whether a per-tenant monthly cost bound is needed, since the daily cap counts new conversations only.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] `BASIC` mapping equals today's defaults (**non-regression: snapshot of the current effective values**); `PLUS` equals the M25-S02 values (provider `openrouter`, model `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`, never the unprefixed id); trial → `BASIC`; with the platform provider `fake` a `BASIC` tenant still resolves to `fake`
  - [ ] The OpenRouter adapter sends the request's `maxPrice` when set and the fixed constant otherwise (request-body assertion)
  - [ ] Not entitled / suspended → inert (no LLM call, counters untouched); access answer failing open → `BASIC`; `maxKnowledgeTextLength` identical for both tiers
- Integration:
  - [ ] End to end on the fake LLM adapter: tier change takes effect on the next message; stored legacy override fields are ignored and counted
  - [ ] Existing chatbot integration suites pass unchanged — their tenants have no subscription row, and the existing `InMemoryTenantAccessPort` already answers an unseeded tenant as operational with `CHATBOT` at `BASIC` (its `ALL_FEATURES` default), so no double change is needed
- Tenant isolation: [ ] one tenant's tier never affects another's caps or model
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean
