## Wave 3 — Self-signup and the hotsite module (backend / BFF / packages)

### M26-S22 — Slug rules and availability (UC-128)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-128 and UC-127 (A1), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API (slug-availability), `docs/BFF_ARCHITECTURE.md` § Module & Controller Naming Conventions (`<module>.public.controller.ts`), `docs/VALUE_OBJECTS_REFERENCE.md` (`Slug`), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Schema-level enforcement, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built) (public throttles)
**Dependencies:** none within M26 (uses the existing `Slug` VO and tenant repository)
**Pattern:** A `SlugPolicy` domain service over the `Slug` VO; one shared reserved-word list; a throttled public read.

**Description:**
Today `Slug` is regex-only (`^[a-z0-9-]+$`) and the web proxy reserves only four top-level segments (`RESERVED_TOP_SEGMENTS` in `apps/web/proxy.ts`). Self-signup lets anyone pick a slug, so add the policy. **Rules (from the discovery, `docs/discovery/tenant-billing/tenant-billing.md`):** 3–30 characters, `a-z0-9-`, no leading or trailing hyphen; a **reserved list** made of (a) every real top-level segment of `apps/web/app` (today `api`, `auth`, `bookings`, `dashboard`, `select-staff-tenant`, `switch-tenant`, `v1`), (b) platform words (`ikaro-trial`, `www`, `login`, `account`) and (c) an **impersonation list** (`ikaro`, `admin`, `suporte`, `support`, …); and uniqueness against existing tenants. **Owner exception:** `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` (for example `ikaro`) is reserved for the self-signup path but is accepted by the **operator** path (`POST /internal/tenants`, UC-024), so Ikaro's own tenant and the local/staging seeds can still be provisioned; a test pins both directions. **Reading that value in Platform:** the env var is optional (`env.validation.ts`) and `IApplicationConfig` (`shared/ports/application-config.port.ts`) is `getOrThrow`-only, so an unset owner slug would throw; this story adds a nullable read to the port (`getOptional(key): string | null`), its `NestApplicationConfigAdapter` implementation and the test double, and `SlugPolicy` treats `null` as "no owner slug" (Billing reads the same variable through `ConfigService` in its module factory, which Platform must not import). The reserved list is a **single source of truth** in `@ikaro/validation` (`packages/validation/src/slug-policy.ts`) used by the backend policy; `apps/web/proxy.ts` keeps its own routing list. **Drift guard:** `apps/web` must never depend on `@ikaro/validation` (`packages/validation/package.json`: "never consumed by apps/web"), so the guard is a spec **inside `packages/validation`** that reads `apps/web/app` with `fs` and asserts every top-level directory (ignoring dynamic `[…]` segments and files) is in the reserved set. `GET /v1/public/signup/slug-availability?slug=` returns `{ status: 'available' }`, `{ status: 'taken' }`, `{ status: 'reserved' }` or `{ status: 'invalid', reason }` (e.g. `TOO_SHORT`), is **throttled at 30 requests per minute per IP** (the existing in-memory BFF throttler, `@Throttle`, best-effort and stated as such; the web's client-side suggestions each re-check, so the limit leaves room) and answers with `Cache-Control: no-store` (a cached `available` would be wrong). It carries no Turnstile (Turnstile is verified only on the final `POST /signup/tenant`, S24; UC-128 A3's Turnstile wording is aligned to the contract — throttled only). **One layer reports each condition** (`docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Schema-level enforcement): the BFF Zod schema checks only the query parameter's shape (present, string); the backend `SlugPolicy` owns `taken` / `reserved` / `invalid` and the codes `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID` / `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED` / `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN` (`422` / `422` / `409`, the statuses in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API). The same policy runs again inside the signup use case (S24), so a slug taken between the check and the submit lands in UC-127 A1. Backend: a small `CheckSlugAvailabilityUseCase` in Platform behind an internal endpoint (`TenantRepository.existsBySlug` is not cached, so the answer is live); the BFF route is the public face. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18) — one code per condition, no duplicates on the operator path:** the operator provisioning path (UC-024, `POST /internal/tenants`) keeps its existing `PLATFORM_SLUG_ALREADY_TAKEN` (`409`) and `SLUG_FORMAT_INVALID` (`400`) unchanged, and the **reserved-word rule is the one new rejection**, returning `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED` (`422`) on both the operator and the public paths; the public routes (this story's availability read and S24's signup) use `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN` (`409`) / `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID` (`422`) / `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED` (`422`). Why: the operator path already has documented, tested codes that `scripts/provision-tenant.sh` and the operator's tooling know, and a second code for the same condition on one path would be two answers to one question. `SlugPolicy` therefore returns a verdict (valid | invalid(reason) | reserved) and each caller maps it: the public use cases to `SIGNUP_SLUG_*`, `ProvisionTenantUseCase` keeps `Slug.create` (`SLUG_FORMAT_INVALID`) and the repository's uniqueness (`PLATFORM_SLUG_ALREADY_TAKEN`) and only adds the reserved check. `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-024 A9 already documents this and states that `scripts/provision-tenant.sh` must handle `422`: verified at the audit — the script's `relay_curl` runs `curl --fail-with-body` (`scripts/lib/relay-auth.sh`), which treats every non-2xx status alike, so `422` is reported like `400` / `409` and no script change is needed; the story keeps a test or a note proving it. **Error codes (this story is the first to throw them):** it adds `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID` to `SignupErrorCode`, to both `errors.json` locale files and to the web exhaustiveness spec (M25-S06 added only `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_REQUIRED` / `_INVALID`, and `SIGNUP_TERMS_NOT_ACCEPTED` already exists; none of the three slug codes does).

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`CheckSlugAvailabilityUseCase`**; 2. `SlugPolicy.validate(slug, { allowOwnerSlug })` — a pure function in `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/slug-policy.ts` over the shared list, used by this use case, by S24 and by `ProvisionTenantUseCase`.
**Backend HTTP surface:** new controller `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/controllers/internal-slug.controller.ts` — `GET /internal/signup/slug-availability?slug=` (`InternalApiGuard`; the path is fixed in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API; deliberately **not** under `/internal/tenants/…`, where a `:tenantId` route would shadow it). Carries `@NoFeatureGate()` and `@SuspensionAccess('ANY_AUTHENTICATED')` as the M25 route-completeness detector requires (the value every existing `internal-billing-*` and `internal-dev-clock` controller uses for a tenant-less route).
**BFF endpoint spec:** `GET /v1/public/signup/slug-availability?slug=` — `@Public()`, `@Throttle` 30/min, `Cache-Control: no-store`; response above. When `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is off the route stays readable (S24).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** i18n: the three `SIGNUP_SLUG_*` error entries in `packages/i18n/locales/{pt-BR,en}/errors.json`. No migration, env var or flag.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `packages/validation/src/slug-policy.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, including the drift guard that reads `apps/web/app` with `fs`) (new), `packages/validation/src/index.ts` (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/slug-policy.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new — the `SlugPolicy.validate` function over the shared list)
- `apps/backend/src/shared/value-objects/slug.vo.ts` (+ spec) (modify only if the policy needs a factory hook; keep `create()` regex-only for existing callers)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/check-slug-availability.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/provision-tenant.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — apply the policy; the operator path allows `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/controllers/internal-slug.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/platform/slug.http` (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/http/platform-error.mapper.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `SlugPolicy` domain errors to `SIGNUP_SLUG_*`; `error-mapper-coverage`)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts` (modify — `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID`), `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (modify only if the spec needs an entry)
- `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.controller.ts`, `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.schemas.ts` (+ specs) (new — **recorded deviation from `docs/BFF_ARCHITECTURE.md` § Module & Controller Naming Conventions**, which names a public controller `<context>.public.controller.ts` (here `platform.public.controller.ts`, which already exists for the manifest): the signup routes are one resource (`/public/signup/*`) that S24 and S25 extend and that spans Platform and Billing, so the file is named by resource like its siblings `hotsite-admin.controller.ts` and `lead-form.controller.ts`, keeps the `.public.` marker and the sibling `.schemas.ts`)
- `docs/BFF_ARCHITECTURE.md` (modify — one sentence in the naming table recording that a resource-named public controller (`signup.public.controller.ts`) is allowed when the resource spans contexts)
- `apps/bff/http/platform/signup.http` (new)
- `apps/bff/src/features/platform/platform.module.ts` (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/shared/ports/application-config.port.ts`, `apps/backend/src/shared/infrastructure/nest-application-config.adapter.ts` (+ spec) (modify — the nullable `getOptional`), and the config double the Platform specs use (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A visitor typing a slug learns immediately whether it is available, taken, reserved or invalid and why; reserved and impersonation words can never be registered, by any path.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Policy table: bounds 3 and 30 (2 and 31 rejected), leading/trailing hyphen, every reserved class (web top-level routes including `bookings`, platform words, impersonation list), invalid characters, case
  - [ ] Owner slug: reserved for the signup path, accepted by the operator path
  - [ ] Drift guard (in `packages/validation`): every `apps/web/app` top-level directory is reserved
  - [ ] Throttle decorator present on the route (30/min) and `Cache-Control: no-store` set
- Integration:
  - [ ] Availability against real tenants (taken) and against a slug taken during the call (the signup path catches it, tested in S24)
  - [ ] `POST /internal/tenants` with a reserved slug is refused with `422 SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED`; with the owner slug it succeeds; an invalid format is still `400 SLUG_FORMAT_INVALID` and a taken slug still `409 PLATFORM_SLUG_ALREADY_TAKEN` (unchanged codes and statuses); with `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` unset the policy still works (no owner slug)
- Tenant isolation: none for the public read (it returns a status only, never tenant data) — asserted: the response carries no tenant fields
- E2E: none — covered by integration; the page E2E is S35
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S23 — Public prices (UC-128)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** S
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-128 (A1), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API (prices), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: FeaturePrice (versions, `upcoming`), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.feature_prices`
**Dependencies:** M25-S04 (`ListSelectablePricesUseCase`, price versions), M26-S17 (adds the `upcoming` projection to that use case), M26-S02 (creates `billing-constants.ts`, where `SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES` joins), M25-S13 (creates the BFF `features/billing/billing.module.ts` this story extends)
**Pattern:** Cacheable read model; the exempt-from-`tenant_id` aggregate.

**Description:**
`GET /v1/public/billing/prices?country=BR` returns `{ countryCode, currency, taxBehavior: 'INCLUSIVE' | 'EXCLUSIVE', items: [{ featureKey, tier, amountMinor, upcoming?: { amountMinor, effectiveFrom } }] }` — the selectable list prices of the country (hidden prices excluded), with `upcoming` only when a dated change is scheduled. Feature descriptions are translated copy in the web app, not API data. The route is `@Public()`, **cacheable**, reads through the M25-S04 use case via an internal endpoint, and carries no tenant data. **Cache:** the BFF sends `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` (the manifest precedent in `platform.public.controller.ts`); the response varies by `country`, so the query string is part of the cache key, and a price that becomes effective is visible within five minutes. **"Current" date:** the route has no tenant, so "now" is the calendar date in `America/Sao_Paulo` for BR (the CountrySpec default timezone of the requested country), the same date basis the price-in-force lookup uses for a service-period start. **`upcoming`:** at most one per item — the earliest version with `effective_from` after today whose `announced_at IS NOT NULL` and `superseded_at IS NULL` (a scheduled-but-not-announced version never leaks), and **only on an item that has a version in force today**: `ListSelectablePricesUseCase` skips a price with no version in force, so a price whose only version is in the future is omitted from the list entirely, never listed with an `upcoming`. A country with no selectable prices, an unsupported `country`, or a country outside the signup list returns `200` with `items: []` (the page treats it as "not offered"); a malformed `country` value is `400`. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): this story owns the supported-country rule.** The US seed rows exist in every environment since M25-S04 (`1748500000040-SeedFeaturePrices`, selectable), so the absence of prices cannot gate the US before M28 (UC-127 A7); the rule is an explicit code constant `SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES = ['BR']` in `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/billing-constants.ts` (the home S02 created; M28 extends it), and **S24 (the signup use case) and S33 (the country selector) cite this constant, never a price-list check** (UC-127 step 1 is reworded to BR only). Why: one constant is the least machinery that keeps US signup closed without editing seed data that the manager plan catalog (S17) also reads. **Two country errors, two answers:** `CountryCode.create` throws `CountryCodeValidationError` with code `FORMAT_INVALID` (not two letters → `400`) or `UNSUPPORTED` (two letters the platform does not know → the endpoint **catches it and returns `200 { items: [] }`**, not a `400`); a known country outside `SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES` (for example `US`) also returns `200 { items: [] }`. **`taxBehavior` comes from the country, not a constant:** `CountrySpec` (`packages/i18n/src/country-defaults.ts`) has no tax-behaviour field today, so this story adds `taxBehavior: 'INCLUSIVE' | 'EXCLUSIVE'` to it (BR `INCLUSIVE`; US `EXCLUSIVE`, prices are net of tax, UC-117), read through `CountryCode.spec`. **Mapping (stated):** the backend use case returns `{ featureKey, tier, cycle, currency, amountMinor, effectiveFrom }` per item; the BFF mapper lifts `currency` to the top level and drops `cycle` (monthly only in M26) and `effectiveFrom` (the response carries the amount in force; the date appears only inside `upcoming`). A price-load failure on the page leaves signup possible (UC-128 A1, handled in S33). The route stays readable when `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is off (S24).

**Backend use case steps:** reuses `ListSelectablePricesUseCase` (M25-S04) and the `upcoming` projection S17 added to it (S17 is the first consumer: the manager's plan catalog), plus the supported-country gate and the `CountryCodeValidationError` handling above.
**Backend HTTP surface:** new `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices.controller.ts` — `GET /internal/billing/public-prices?country=` (`InternalApiGuard`; `@NoFeatureGate()` and the explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` the M25 detector requires for a tenant-less route).
**BFF endpoint spec:** `GET /v1/public/billing/prices?country=` — `@Public()`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300`; response above (enums and amounts only, no display strings).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/billing-constants.ts` (modify — `SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES = ['BR']`), `packages/i18n/src/country-defaults.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `taxBehavior` on `CountrySpec`), `docs/VALUE_OBJECTS_REFERENCE.md` (modify — the `CountrySpec` entry gains `taxBehavior`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/list-selectable-prices.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify only if S17 left the `upcoming` projection short of what the public route needs; the gate and the country handling live in the controller or a thin use case beside it)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices.controller.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new), `apps/backend/http/billing/prices.http` (new)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.public.controller.ts`, `billing.public.schemas.ts` (+ specs) (new), `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify), `apps/bff/http/billing/prices.http` (new)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] The public page can show the current price table for Brazil, including an announced increase with its date; hidden prices never appear.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit: [ ] projection with and without a scheduled version; hidden excluded; two future versions → only the earliest announced one; an unannounced future version never appears; a price whose only version is in the future is omitted; cache headers set; `US` (a known country outside `SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES`) returns `items: []` and `taxBehavior` is `INCLUSIVE` for BR; the BFF mapper drops `cycle` and `effectiveFrom`
- Integration: [ ] real seeded prices returned for BR; a future version appears only as `upcoming`; an unknown two-letter country (`CountryCodeValidationError` `UNSUPPORTED`) → `200 { items: [] }`, a malformed one (`FORMAT_INVALID`) → `400`; `US` returns `[]` although its seed rows exist
- Tenant isolation: none — platform-wide data with no tenant owner (the documented `feature_prices` exemption)
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S24 — Self-signup — session, trial rule, signup use case and BFF routes (UC-127)

**Agent:** `bff-ts + backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-127 (all, A1–A10), UC-110 and UC-024, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API (BFF, no JWT) and § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API (the registrations route and the `POST /internal/tenants` row), `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-A1), `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` § TenantProvisioned, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_FRONTEND.md` § CSP allowances for a new external UI resource and `docs/CI_TRAPS.md` § Cloudflare Turnstile's test sitekey, `docs/BFF_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built), `journeys/guest/ikaro-trial.md`
**Dependencies:** M26-S01 (the secrets and the env contract this story extends), M26-S22 (slug policy), M26-S23 (`SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES`, the supported-country rule), M26-S08 (the one-hour `RESERVED` release is the backstop), M25-S06 (`TaxId`, tax-id error codes), M25-S09 (registration reservation, B-A1), M25-S10 (provisioning with `registrationId`), M25-S11 (the trial handler), M25-S13 (tenant-info port, for `existingTenantSlug`)
**Pattern:** Orchestration in the BFF over internal endpoints (Billing reserves and releases, Platform provisions); a short-lived signed cookie signed with the existing `JwtService`; the existing Turnstile verifier port; B-A1 exercised end to end.

**Description:**
Anyone can start a free trial. **Session (Google step).** The visitor signs in with Google through the existing OAuth flow with a new `type=signup` branch (`GET /auth/google?type=signup`): no Turnstile on this step (Turnstile is verified only on the final `POST /signup/tenant`). The callback checks the Google profile's **email-verified flag** (the existing strategy reads only `emails[0].value`; a Google account whose email is unverified is refused with the "Google account unverified" error page (the page is owned by S33, which renders it on the `/ikaro-trial` route family with a spec; this story only redirects to it), because the email becomes the first manager), then keeps only `{ email, name, googleSub }` in a **short-lived signed cookie**: signed with the existing `JwtService` (`JWT_SECRET`, the `OAuthStateService` precedent — **no new signing secret and no new devops half**) with a distinct `typ`/`aud` claim (`signup-session`) so it can never be accepted as a session JWT or an OAuth state, a distinct cookie name (`__Host-signup_session` in production, `signup_session` elsewhere, mirroring `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME`), `httpOnly`, `Secure` outside local, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, 30 minutes. No tenant exists yet and **no tenant JWT is ever issued** (there is no existing "staff selection token" to reuse). The callback redirects to the `/ikaro-trial/entrar` page (S33 owns the form step on that route); that redirect is set by the signup branch itself, outside the slug-bound `isValidReturnTo` path (see the verified-files list below for the guard / state-encoder changes this branch needs). A **dev/test signup-session issuer** (`POST /auth/dev-signup-session`, `{ email, name, googleSub }`, gated exactly like `dev-login` by `ENABLE_DEV_AUTH` and refused when `APP_ENV=production`) sets the same cookie so Playwright (S33, S35) can obtain a signup session without Google.

**Read.** `GET /v1/public/signup/session` returns `{ email, name, trialAvailable, existingTenantSlug?, termsVersion }`. `trialAvailable` and `existingTenantSlug` come from a **read-only** Billing eligibility query (`GET /internal/billing/registrations/eligibility?googleSub=`, new here — the tax id is checked on the final `POST` only, never on this read: the same rule as M25-S09 but with no lock and no insert; `POST /internal/billing/registrations` inserts a `RESERVED` row and must not be called from a read). `existingTenantSlug` is the slug of the earliest unpaid self-signup tenant of that `sub`, resolved through the M25-S13 tenant-info port. `termsVersion` is the backend env `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` — the **single source**: the web renders the hosted text for that version and sends it back as `acceptedTermsVersion`; the web keeps no duplicate constant (S33). Without a valid cookie the route answers `401` (confirm at story discovery). The session read does not know the tax id yet, so the tax-id half of the trial rule is applied at reservation (below), and the page learns of it from `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` on submit. **Who maps the 409 (verified):** Billing's `POST /internal/billing/registrations` never answers `409`: it returns `201 { registrationId: null, trialAvailable: false }` for an ineligible visitor (`reserve-signup-registration.use-case.ts`). The **BFF signup orchestration** reads `trialAvailable: false` and throws `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` itself (so the code is also a `BffThrowableCode`); no registration exists then, so there is nothing to release.

**Trial rule.** A signup is refused with `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` (A3) when (a) an earlier self-signup tenant of that Google `sub` never paid (M25-S09), or (b) the **normalised CPF/CNPJ is already held by a never-paid `billing_accounts` row or by a `RESERVED` registration** (trial-farming rule). **Both halves are already built by M25-S09** (`evaluateTrialEligibility` and `isTaxIdHeldByUnpaid` in `typeorm-signup-registration.repository.ts`, reading `IDX_billing_billing_accounts_tax_id`); this story does not re-implement them and the old "and not purged" qualifier is dropped (a purged tenant has no `billing_accounts` row to match, and no purge exists before M27). The stored `tax_id` is the normalised value: digits for a CPF/CNPJ with digits only, **digits plus uppercase letters for the alphanumeric CNPJ** (`TaxId` accepts `^[0-9A-Z]{12}\d{2}$`), not digits-only. Both checks run inside the same B-A1 transaction, and the cross-Google-account tax-id check is best-effort under concurrency (B-A1 serialises per `sub`, not per tax id) — stated, not hidden (`docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` states the same best-effort caveat next to B-A1). Eligibility is released only by M27 discard or the operator offboarding runbook (M17-S51), never automatically (UC-127 A3).

**Create.** `POST /v1/public/signup/tenant` `{ businessName, slug, countryCode: 'BR', taxId (BR), acceptedTermsVersion, turnstileToken }`; email and name come **from the session, never from the body**; the language derives from `countryCode` (no language field); `countryCode` is `BR` only until M28: the check is `countryCode ∈ SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES` (the constant S23 adds to `billing-constants.ts`; this story cites it and never derives the rule from the price list, UC-127 A7), and a request outside it is `422` with the A7 "not offered" mapping (confirm the exact code at discovery). S23 must be merged before this story starts (a precondition for the constant, not a graph edge). The order is fixed so a recoverable error never costs the visitor their trial:
1. **Stateless validations first, nothing persisted:** the BFF Zod shape check, then Platform's `ValidateSignupUseCase` through the new stateless `POST /internal/signup/validate` (decided by the editor: a separate route, so reservation can sit between validation and provisioning without a `phase` flag) verifies Turnstile **once** (the only Turnstile verification in the flow — `POST /internal/signup/tenant` does not verify it again; the existing `ITurnstileVerifierPort`, token single-use, `clientIp` forwarded as the lead form does, BFF → backend timeout 15 s like `LEAD_FORM_SUBMISSION_TIMEOUT_MS`), the slug policy (S22) and availability (non-binding), the terms version against the current `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` (`SIGNUP_TERMS_NOT_ACCEPTED`, A5) and the tax id (`SIGNUP_TAX_ID_REQUIRED` / `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_INVALID`, A9).
2. **Reserve:** the BFF calls Billing `POST /internal/billing/registrations` (B-A1 lock on the Google `sub`, the trial rule above → `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE`), storing the Google `sub`, the BR tax id and the accepted terms version in `signup_registrations`. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18):** the acceptance timestamp is stamped by **Billing** (its own clock, at the reservation), and the BFF sends **only the version** — one clock for the legal record, and a client-supplied time is never trusted. **Also decided at the audit (reserve-first):** the tax id is validated **once, at the reservation** (Billing, `TaxId`); the `validate` pre-check (step 1) is an early-refusal convenience for the form, not a second source of truth.
3. **Provision:** the BFF calls `POST /internal/signup/tenant` with the `registrationId` (required); `SignupTenantUseCase` re-applies the cheap stateless checks (slug policy, terms version, tax id) but does **not** consume a second Turnstile token (verified in step 1), and calls the **existing** `ProvisionTenantUseCase` with the Google email as first manager and the `registrationId`. A slug taken between the check and the commit surfaces as a unique violation in `ProvisionTenantUseCase` (it does `existsBySlug` then `save`; `tenants.slug` is `unique`), which that use case now maps to `SlugAlreadyTakenError`; `SignupTenantUseCase` maps it to `409 SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN` (A1).
4. **On ANY failure after step 2** (including a network error), the BFF calls the idempotent `POST /internal/billing/registrations/{id}/release` in a `finally`, deleting the `RESERVED` row; the one-hour sweep in S08 (step 1b) is only the backstop. **Acceptance: a retry after A1 / A2 / A5 / A9 / A6 succeeds immediately.**

**Transaction and network I/O placement:** the Turnstile verification (a network call) and the BFF → Billing / BFF → Platform calls sit in separate use cases and **never inside `txManager.run()`**; the Billing reservation and Platform provisioning are two separate transactions. None of the billing identity data (Google `sub`, tax id, terms) travels in the event (`TenantProvisioned` carries only `countryCode`, `timezone` and `registrationId` as billing references, next to the tenant's `name`, `slug` and `adminEmail`). `201 { tenantSlug, nextPath: '/ikaro-trial/conta-criada' }`; the signup-session cookie is cleared and replaced by the **resend credential** cookie (a signed value bound to `{ tenantId, email }`, 30 minutes, same attributes and `JwtService`, `typ`/`aud` `signup-resend`, which S25 consumes); the first-manager invitation / login email follows asynchronously from `TenantProvisioned`. **Failures:** Turnstile fails → `400 SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED` (A2); rate limit exceeded → `429 SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After` (A4); `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` off → `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` on the session read, the Google step and both `POST`s (slug availability and prices stay readable; the real "stop signups" lever, drilled in S39); a country outside `SIGNUP_SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES` (US) → not offered (A7); tenant-creation fails → nothing is created and a retry is offered (A6 — the durable event retry completes a delayed `TenantProvisioned` consumer; the self-heal job S08 repairs a missing trial); a Google account that already manages other tenants → allowed, trial rule still applies (A8).

**Design notes carried from M25-S10 (2026-11-11; note (4) is decided below, the rest are confirmed at this story's `/story-discovery`).** (1) *One provisioning pattern.* Operator and self-signup both reserve a Billing registration first and create the tenant with its `registrationId` (M25-S10 made the id required in `ProvisionTenantUseCase`, so `SignupTenantUseCase` always passes it). Platform holds no tax id, no exempt flag and no feature list; the registration is the single holder of the billing intent. (2) *Why `validate` stays a separate first call.* It verifies Turnstile **before** the reservation, so a bot cannot spam `POST /internal/billing/registrations` (a lock on the Google `sub` plus a row) without being checked; the single-use token is verified once, there. Do not fold it into the provisioning call without moving the bot check elsewhere. (3) *The tax id is validated once, at the reservation* (Billing, `TaxId`); the `validate` pre-check and `SignupTenantUseCase` re-check are early-refusal conveniences for the form, not a second source of truth, so keep them to the shape check or drop them. (4) **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): reserve-first.** An ineligible visitor is refused before any tenant exists (UC-127: yes, hence reserve-before-create, the release call and the one-hour sweep), and the tax id is validated once, at the reservation. Why: the tenant-first alternative (create the tenant, let Billing's handler decide the trial) would change that rule and need another route for the tax id into Billing that is not the event, which is more machinery for the same outcome.

**Rate limits (numbers; stated honestly).** Signup attempts: **5 per IP per day** (best-effort — the existing in-memory BFF throttler, per instance; not a hard guarantee); **3 per Google account per day** (durable, counted from `signup_registrations` rows of that `sub` created in the last 24 h, so no new table or migration). **What counts (decided at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18):** every `POST /v1/public/signup/tenant` that reaches the BFF counts toward the 5 per IP per day, **failed ones included** (a validation retry burns an attempt: the limit protects the Turnstile verification and the reservation lock, not just completed signups); only **live or completed reservations** count toward the 3 per Google account (a released `RESERVED` row is deleted, so a visitor who corrects and retries is never penalised twice). Why: the per-IP limit is the bot control, the per-`sub` limit is the trial-farming control, and the two rows of `signup_registrations` are the only durable signal that needs no new table. The BFF throws `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` for the per-IP limit; the **Billing reserve use case also throws it** for the per-`sub` cap (backend thrower), so `billing-error.mapper.ts` maps it to `429` with a `Retry-After` the BFF passes through unchanged. The `/auth/*` routes (the Google step) keep the existing class-level `10/min` throttle of `auth.controller.ts` (`@Throttle({ default: { limit: 10, ttl: 60_000 } })`); the signup branch of the callback inherits that tier and needs no new limit. The hard anti-abuse controls are Turnstile, the per-`sub` B-A1 trial rule and the per-tax-id rule. **Verified against the code (the rate limit is real work, not a decorator):** the global `AppThrottlerGuard` (`apps/bff/src/shared/guards/app-throttler.guard.ts`) tracks **by IP only** (`getTracker`), throws `AuthErrorCode.RATE_LIMITED` with **no `Retry-After`**, and is skipped outside `APP_ENV` `staging`/`production` (`shouldSkip`); `app.module.ts` records that identity-keyed limiting was dropped. So this story must add (1) a **per-throttler `getTracker`** (the signup throttle keys on the client IP, the S25 resend throttle on the credential's tenant id plus the IP) and (2) a **custom exception** carrying the signup code `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` and a `Retry-After` header. **OPEN: decide at `/story-discovery`** — the tracker/enforcement design: (A) a named throttler registered in `ThrottlerModule` with a `signup-throttle.guard.ts` that overrides `getTracker`/`throwThrottlingException` (stays in the existing in-memory storage, per instance), or (B) a small dedicated guard with its own counter map keyed on the tracker. The in-memory per-instance behaviour is stated, not hidden, in both. **Local and CI cannot exercise the limits end to end** because the guard is skipped under `APP_ENV=local`; the guard is therefore unit-tested with its enabled flag taken from an injectable provider the test sets, and the real limit is observed once on staging (live check below), never in Playwright. The signup routes are platform-level and carry no tenant slug header; `@BypassWebOnlyGuard()` exists (`apps/bff/src/shared/decorators/bypass-web-only-guard.decorator.ts`; S04 uses it for the provider webhook), but the signup routes need no bypass because ikaro-web calls them server-side with its web key. The `/v1` web gateway has an 8 s timeout (`BFF_GATEWAY_TIMEOUT_MS`): the signup `POST` goes through the Next server-side fetcher (`bffServerFetch`-style, S33), not the gateway, because the Turnstile verification can take close to 8 s on its own.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`ValidateSignupUseCase`** (Platform): Turnstile, slug policy and availability, terms version, tax id — persists nothing. 2. **`SignupTenantUseCase`** (Platform): re-applies the cheap checks, provisions with the required `registrationId` through the existing `ProvisionTenantUseCase`, maps the slug race. 3. **`GetSignupEligibilityUseCase`** (Billing): the read-only eligibility query (no lock, no insert). 4. **`ReleaseSignupRegistrationUseCase`** (Billing): deletes a `RESERVED` row, idempotent, refuses a `COMPLETED` one. 5. BFF orchestration: session read → validate → reserve → provision → on any failure after reserve, release (`finally`) → set the resend credential and clear the session cookie.
**Backend HTTP surface:**
- new `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/controllers/internal-signup.controller.ts` — `POST /internal/signup/validate` (new here) and `POST /internal/signup/tenant` (path fixed in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`), both `InternalApiGuard` alone.
- extends the Billing registration controller (M25-S09, `internal-billing-registration.controller.ts`) — `GET /internal/billing/registrations/eligibility?googleSub=` and `POST /internal/billing/registrations/{id}/release`; `POST /internal/billing/registrations` is reused.
- Every new **backend** route carries `@NoFeatureGate()` and the explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` the M25 route-completeness detector requires for tenant-less internal routes (a BFF route carries none, M25-S33) (read M25-S16/S20/S24 for the exact decorator).
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.controller.ts` — `GET /v1/public/signup/session`, `POST /v1/public/signup/tenant` (`@Public()`, throttles as above, `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` when `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is off); the cookie service in `signup-session.service.ts`; `GET /auth/google?type=signup` and the callback branch in the auth feature; `POST /auth/dev-signup-session` (dev/test only).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:**
- env: the current Terms version `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` (backend only; added to backend `env.validation.ts` and the env contract; required when `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is true) and the flag `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` (default `false` in production until S39, `true` in local/CI/staging; **both** the backend and the BFF read it, so both `env.validation.ts` files, both services' `env_vars` blocks in each Terraform env root and the env contract carry it). In production both vars are ignored while `bootstrap_mode=true` (M17-S37 flips it, a prerequisite of S39), so the production value is a no-op until activation. `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is the existing backend variable the lead form already uses. **No new secret.**
- i18n (this story first throws four codes; `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_INVALID`, `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_REQUIRED` and `SIGNUP_TERMS_NOT_ACCEPTED` already exist in `error-codes.ts` and both locale files, so they are **not** added again): `SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE`, `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED`, `SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED`, `SIGNUP_CLOSED` added to `SignupErrorCode`, both `errors.json` locale files and the web exhaustiveness spec; the BFF-thrown ones (`SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` for the per-IP limit, `SIGNUP_CLOSED`, and `SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE`, which the BFF maps from the reserve answer `trialAvailable: false`) also go to `BffThrowableCode`; `SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED` is thrown by the backend `ValidateSignupUseCase` (it passes through the BFF unchanged, so it is not a `BffThrowableCode`); `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` is thrown by both layers (see Rate limits). Default HTTP statuses (confirm at story discovery): `SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED` 400, `SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` 409, `SIGNUP_TERMS_NOT_ACCEPTED` 422, `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` 429, `SIGNUP_CLOSED` 503.
- migration: none for the signup flow; the tax-id lookup index is owned by M25-S03 (see **Notes for other owners**).

**Notes for other owners (canonical docs / M25, not edited here):** (1) M25-S03 / `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`: the standalone partial index on `billing.billing_accounts (tax_id) WHERE tax_id IS NOT NULL` (cross-tenant, no `tenant_id` available at signup) is already documented as `IDX_billing_billing_accounts_tax_id`; a standalone index on `signup_registrations (created_at)` is not needed (the per-`sub` daily count uses the existing `google_sub` index). (2) `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API and § Provider Webhooks and Internal API: `POST /internal/signup/validate` (the row exists, marked planned; this story flips it to built; the validate route is the only Turnstile verification), the eligibility and release routes, `termsVersion` on the session response, `SIGNUP_CLOSED`, `countryCode` `BR` only until M28, Turnstile only on the final `POST`, the throttle numbers. (3) `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-127: Turnstile placement, A3 release is not automatic, the tax-id rule, the kill switch; `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` env list: `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED`, `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION`. (4) M25-S09 L612 limitation reworded: M26-S24 releases the reservation on failure.

**Devops half (env only, no secret).** `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` and `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` are plain env vars: `env_vars` in `infra/terraform/envs/staging/main.tf` and `envs/prod/main.tf` (prod: `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=false` and a placeholder terms version until S39), the env contract (`exemptKeys` for the local defaults); `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` goes into **both** the backend and the BFF service `env_vars` (the BFF throws `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED`, the backend reads it for its own routes). **PR sequence (decided at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18): at least 2 PRs** — the module path and the Foundation roots cannot share a PR, and CI's `no-infra-app-mix` job rejects app + Terraform without the `infra-app-mix-ok` label: PR1 = app code and the env contract (+ the `envs/*/main.tf` env vars, labelled `infra-app-mix-ok` per the playbook row for a plain env var, `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook), PR2 = the Foundation-only `additional_scripts` change for the relay VM (see the last technical criterion) with its Foundation apply. No new secret, topic or subscription. **Live-verification check:** after apply on staging, `gcloud run services describe <backend>` and `<bff>` show both vars; a smoke `GET /v1/public/signup/session` without a cookie returns the documented unauthenticated answer (and `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` once the flag is flipped off in a throwaway revision); the per-IP and per-Google limits are observed once on staging through the web gateway (the guard is skipped under `APP_ENV=local`, so staging is the only place they run). Pass = all four.

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A visitor with a Google account can create a tenant with a business name, slug, country and CPF/CNPJ after accepting the Terms, and receives the first-manager invitation email; no JWT is issued and the dashboard is not entered by signup.
- [ ] A second signup by the same Google account is allowed only if every earlier self-signup tenant has paid, and a signup whose CPF/CNPJ is already held by a never-paid account or a live reservation is refused; otherwise the visitor is told exactly why and offered the sign-in link (and, until M27, the "contact Ikaro" message instead of discard).
- [ ] A taken, reserved or invalid slug, a failed Turnstile, an invalid tax id, unaccepted Terms and a rate limit each produce their own clear error and create nothing — and the visitor can **correct and retry immediately** without losing their trial.
- [ ] With `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` off, signup returns `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` while slug availability and prices keep working.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Each of A1–A10 mapped to its code/status (`400`, `409`, `422`, `429` with `Retry-After`, `503`); email/name never read from the body
  - [ ] Cookie: signed with `JwtService` and a `signup-session` `typ`/`aud` claim (a session JWT or OAuth state is rejected by the signup reader and vice versa), httpOnly, `Secure` outside local, `SameSite=Lax`, 30-minute expiry, only the three fields, cleared on `201`; tampered cookie rejected; an unverified Google email refused
  - [ ] Throttle override emits `429 SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`, with its own `getTracker` (client IP for signup), and is exercisable in a unit test under `APP_ENV=local` through the injectable enabled flag; the real limits are not testable in local/CI E2E (observed on staging, live check)
  - [ ] The signup branch of `/auth/google` inherits the class-level `/auth/*` `10/min` tier (no new limit added)
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Retry after failure:** after each of A1 (slug race), A2 (Turnstile), A5 (terms), A9 (tax id) and a provisioning failure, an immediate corrected retry succeeds (the `RESERVED` row was released); release is idempotent and refuses a `COMPLETED` row
  - [ ] Read-only eligibility inserts nothing and takes no lock; `existingTenantSlug` is the earliest unpaid tenant's slug; the same CPF/CNPJ from a second Google account is refused while the first account is unpaid, and allowed once it has paid; an alphanumeric CNPJ is stored and matched in its normalised uppercase form
  - [ ] The 4th signup reservation of one Google account in 24 h is refused with `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` and a `Retry-After` the BFF passes through (`billing-error.mapper.ts` entry); a released (retried) reservation does not count toward the 3
  - [ ] **Reserve answer mapping:** `trialAvailable: false` from the reserve call becomes `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` in the BFF and no release call is made (nothing was reserved)
  - [ ] **Terms timestamp:** the acceptance timestamp stored in `signup_registrations` is stamped by Billing and the BFF request carries only `acceptedTermsVersion`
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-A1):** two simultaneous signups for one Google account produce exactly one reserved trial; the loser gets `SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE`
  - [ ] End to end through the in-memory bus: signup → `TenantProvisioned` → trial subscription (M25-S11) and a first-manager invitation, with the registration completed and the tax id cleared
  - [ ] Two simultaneous signups for one slug → one tenant, the other `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN`; a tenant-creation failure leaves no registration-completed row and no tenant
  - [ ] Turnstile via the fake verifier; the per-Google cap trips (the per-IP throttle is covered by the guard's unit test only, since the guard is skipped under `APP_ENV=local`)
  - [ ] No tenant-scoped `RequestContext` is read on any signup path
  - [ ] **Operator script releases on failure:** `scripts/provision-tenant.sh` has no automated test, so this is run once by hand against the local stack (`INTERNAL_KEY` exported, as the script header allows) with a slug that already exists: the tenant call fails, the script exits 1 with the tenant call's error, and the registration it reserved is gone (no `RESERVED` row for it). A second run with a free slug succeeds. If the release call itself fails, the script still exits 1 with the original error and names the registration id. The result is recorded in the PR.
  - [ ] **Staging provision-to-subscription check, carried from M25-S11 (its live check 3, deferred 2026-11-12):** from the relay VM, run `scripts/provision-tenant.sh` once on staging (throwaway slug and email, a non-exempt `BR` tenant) and confirm a `TRIALING` `billing.subscriptions` row for the new tenant within a minute, and the handler's `TenantProvisioned received` log line with the same `correlationId`. This is the first real run of the script on the relay VM and of the staging push delivery to `create-trial-subscription`; run it with the S24 version of the script, then delete the `⚠️` line under M25-S11 in `plan/M25-TENANT-BILLING-FOUNDATION.md`. Since M25-S15b the script sources `scripts/lib/relay-auth.sh` (the metadata token, backend URL, identity token and both keys, sent on curl's stdin, never as arguments), so this run is also the first real exercise of that refactor of `provision-tenant.sh`: it passed CI only up to argument validation. M25-S32 covers the other S15b script, `billing-operator.sh`.
  - [ ] **Relay VM boots from Foundation-applied metadata, carried from M25-S32 (2026-11-18):** M25-S32 fixed the relay VM startup script and added `additional_scripts` to `modules/relay-vm`, but validated its boots with the scripts set by hand as instance metadata, and no Foundation root passes `billing-operator.sh` and `lib/relay-auth.sh` yet. Before the relay VM is brought up for the check above: make the Foundation-only PR that adds `additional_scripts = { "billing-operator.sh" = file(…), "lib/relay-auth.sh" = file(…) }` to `foundation/envs/staging/main.tf` and `foundation/envs/prod/main.tf` (the module path and the Foundation roots cannot share a PR) and apply Foundation. After `relay-vm-up.sh staging`, confirm on the first boot: the startup run ends at "Finished running startup scripts" with no failure, `/usr/local/bin` holds `provision-tenant.sh`, `billing-operator.sh` and `lib/relay-auth.sh`, `systemctl is-active cloud-sql-proxy` answers `active`, `billing-operator.sh --help` runs, and the installed scripts are byte-identical to the repo's (`cmp`). Then reset the VM once and confirm the same results (idempotent second boot). Then delete the `⚠️` line under M25-S32's boot criteria in `plan/M25-TENANT-BILLING-FOUNDATION.md`.
- Tenant isolation: [ ] a signup creates a tenant and never touches an existing tenant's data; the registration is bound to the new tenant only

### M26-S25 — Signup resend (UC-127 A10)

**Agent:** `bff-ts + backend-ts`
**Complexity:** S
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-127 (A10), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API (resend), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Staff OAuth login URL format and § Adding a new notification type, `journeys/guest/ikaro-trial.md`
**Dependencies:** M26-S24 (the signup flow and the resend credential it issues at the `201`)
**Pattern:** A Staff-owned use case re-raising the existing `StaffInvited` event (the existing `StaffInvitedHandler` / Notification path sends the email); BFF orchestration — no new Platform → Staff call; a rate-limited public mutation authenticated by a signed credential.

**Description:**
`POST /v1/public/signup/resend` re-sends the first-manager invitation / login email from the account-created page. **Why Staff owns it:** no resend exists today. `InviteStaffUseCase` needs `firstName`/`lastName` and `Staff.reinvite()` throws on an empty name and overwrites name, role and `invitedBy`, while the first manager is created by `Staff.inviteFromProvisioning` with `name = null` (apps/backend/src/contexts/staff/domain/staff.aggregate.ts), and the signup cookie holding the Google name is gone after the `201`. So a new Staff `ResendFirstManagerInvitationUseCase` finds the pending MANAGER by `tenantId` + `email`, requires `googleOAuthId` to be null, **never touches name or role**, and re-raises `StaffInvited` (a new `Staff.resendInvitation(correlationId)` method that only adds the event). No new template; the existing email is sent.

**Identity of the caller (no enumeration):** the BFF reads the **resend credential cookie** issued at the signup `201` (S24: signed, `typ`/`aud` `signup-resend`, bound to `{ tenantId, email }`, 30 minutes). Without a valid credential the route answers one generic `401`; there is no body parameter naming a tenant or email, so the route cannot be used to probe whether an email belongs to a tenant. **Response:** `200 { sent: boolean, ready: boolean }` — `ready` is true when the first-manager row exists (so the account-created page can show the one-click Google sign-in to the existing staff login, S33), `sent` is true when the `StaffInvited` event was **re-raised** — it does **not** prove an email left the system: the Notification use case `SendStaffInvitationUseCase` returns `{ sent: false }` and acks when no `STAFF_INVITATION` template exists for the tenant yet (`send-staff-invitation.use-case.ts`, "No template found — skipping"), which at signup can race with the default-template seed that also reacts to `TenantProvisioned`; so a resend is exactly the recovery for a first email dropped that way, and a retry after the template exists sends it. If the row does not exist yet the answer is `{ sent: false, ready: false }` (the page keeps waiting); if the manager already linked their Google account the answer is `{ sent: false, ready: true }` and no email is sent. **Rate limits:** 3 per hour per tenant and 10 per day per IP. They use the **per-throttler `getTracker` and the `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` exception with `Retry-After` that S24 builds** (the global `AppThrottlerGuard` tracks by IP only, throws a code with no header and is skipped outside staging/production — S24's Rate limits paragraph): the per-tenant limit tracks the credential's tenant id, the per-IP limit the client IP; both are best-effort per instance (in-memory), stated, and the credential's 30-minute life bounds the abuse. Like S24's, they are **not testable in local/CI E2E** (guard skipped under `APP_ENV=local`): unit-tested through the injectable enabled flag. Over the limit → `429 SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`. When `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is off the route answers `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED`.

**Backend use case steps:** `ResendFirstManagerInvitationUseCase` (Staff): load by `tenantId` + `email`, return `{ sent, ready }`, save inside `ITransactionManager.run()` so the outbox publishes `StaffInvited` atomically.
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `apps/backend/src/contexts/staff/infrastructure/controllers/internal-staff.controller.ts` — `POST /internal/staff/resend-first-manager-invitation` `{ tenantId, email }` (`InternalApiGuard`; `@NoFeatureGate()` and the explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` the M25 detector requires). This replaces the `POST /internal/signup/resend` placeholder in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (listed under **Notes for other owners**).
**BFF endpoint spec:** `POST /v1/public/signup/resend` — `@Public()`, credential cookie, throttles above; response above.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none (`SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` / `SIGNUP_CLOSED` are added by S24).

**Notes for other owners:** `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` — the resend internal route is `POST /internal/staff/resend-first-manager-invitation` (it replaces API_CONTRACTS' placeholder `POST /internal/signup/resend`, already replaced there), `POST /v1/public/signup/resend` returns `{ sent, ready }` and needs the resend credential cookie; UC-127 A10 already states the credential-based resend and the `{ sent, ready }` answer (verified), so no `docs/USE_CASES.md` edit is needed.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/staff/domain/staff.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `resendInvitation(correlationId)`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/staff/application/use-cases/resend-first-manager-invitation.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/staff/infrastructure/controllers/internal-staff.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/staff/internal-staff.http` (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/staff/infrastructure/http/staff-error.mapper.ts` (+ spec) (modify only if the use case adds a domain error)
- `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.controller.ts`, `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.schemas.ts`, `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup-session.service.ts`, `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup-throttle.guard.ts` (+ specs — the resend tracker keyed on the credential's tenant id), `apps/bff/http/platform/signup.http` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A visitor who did not receive the email can ask for it again a limited number of times; the endpoint never reveals whether an email belongs to a tenant; the account-created page learns when the first manager exists.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit: [ ] rate limit trips; a missing or tampered credential gets the one generic `401`; the use case never changes name or role and refuses a manager whose Google account is already linked (`sent: false`); `ready` false when the row is absent
- Integration:
  - [ ] End to end through the in-memory bus: resend produces exactly one invitation email; a second within the limit window is refused
  - [ ] **First email dropped:** provision a tenant while no `STAFF_INVITATION` template exists (the first email is skipped, `sent: false` in Notification), then seed the template and call resend: exactly one email is sent, and the response is `{ sent: true, ready: true }` (event raised) in both cases
- Tenant isolation: [ ] the resend can only target the tenant named by the caller's credential (issued by its own signup); a credential for tenant A never re-sends tenant B's invitation
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S26 — `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` contract and owner-tenant restriction (UC-131)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-131 (all), `docs/HOTSITE_DYNAMIC_ARCHITECTURE.md` § 3 (module registry), § 4 `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` (the layout rules table is in § 4 "Validation: where each layer stops"), § 7 Adding a New Module (the checklist this story follows to the letter), § 12 (template catalogue), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Tenant Hotsite Manifest and the hotsite admin routes, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Schema-level enforcement and § `no-restricted-syntax` selectors, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned) (`PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG`)
**Dependencies:** M25-S07 (`PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` in the env contract), M25-S25 (module filtering by entitlement, whose map this type must stay out of), M26-S22 (the nullable `getOptional` config read the owner check uses)
**Pattern:** Registry extension (the module-type union is closed and mirrored in several packages) + a domain layout rule; the same mirror-consistency discipline as every previous module.

**Description:**
Add the 13th hotsite module type, `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` — a call to action, **not a billable feature** (no price, no entitlement). Data contract (mirrors `BOOKING_CTA`'s vocabulary): `variant` (`centered` | `left-aligned`), `title` (1–80), optional `subtitle` (≤ 200), `ctaLabel` (1–30); **the link target is fixed to `/ikaro-trial` and is not a field**. The section background is **not** a data field: it is the section-level `presentation.surface` every module uses; whether the type accepts `presentation.background` (the image slot) is decided in `hotsite-layout.rules.ts`'s `PRESENTATION_MODULE_TYPES`. **Touch points (the registry is closed and mirrored; the mirror-consistency detectors enforce them):** `packages/types/src/enums.ts` (`HotsiteModuleType`), `packages/validation/src/hotsite.ts` (`HOTSITE_MODULE_TYPES` and the discriminated data schema) and `hotsite-modules.ts`, `packages/types/src/hotsite.ts` (data type), the contract test `packages/types/src/testing/hotsite-module-contract.ts`, the BFF `hotsite-admin.schemas.ts` module union (via `HotsiteModuleSchema`), and the backend layout rules. **Owner restriction:** `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/hotsite-layout.rules.ts` rejects the type for any tenant other than the platform owner (slug equals `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG`). The flag travels use case → aggregate → rule: `UpdateHotsiteContentUseCase` already holds `tenantRepo`, so it loads the calling tenant's slug, compares it with `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` and passes `isPlatformOwner` in the existing `LayoutValidationContext` to `HotsiteConfig.updateContent(…, ctx)` → `validateLayout` → `assertValidLayoutStructure(layout, { isPlatformOwner })`; the other caller, `hotsite-template-materializer.service.ts` (line ~68), calls `assertValidLayoutStructure(safeLayout)` **directly** (not `updateContent`), so this story changes that direct call and the function's signature (`assertValidLayoutStructure(layout, { isPlatformOwner })`, with the context type `LayoutValidationContext` in `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/hotsite-config.types.ts`, currently only `maxBookingAdvanceDays`) — the materializer passes `false`; the aggregate's `validateLayout` (hotsite-config.aggregate.ts:257) passes the real flag. **Error (decided by the editor, simplest consistent with the existing hotsite errors):** a new `HotsiteModuleOwnerOnlyError` with code `PLATFORM_HOTSITE_MODULE_OWNER_ONLY`, field `layout`, mapped `422` by `platform-error.mapper.ts` as a single-cause error (`code` + `field`, not `violations[]`); confirm the status at story discovery. **Templates never carry it:** the template catalogue's test `packages/hotsite-templates/src/catalogue.spec.ts` ("lists all twelve module types exactly once") compares each template's layout to the full registry, so adding a 13th type breaks it for all six templates unless the test excludes this type explicitly (the test is reworded and the exclusion is a named constant `OWNER_ONLY_MODULE_TYPES`); applying a template that somehow carries it is **rejected** (the materializer passes `isPlatformOwner = false`, so the same error). The manifest includes the module when enabled; module filtering by entitlement (M25-S25, whose map is sparse: only `LEAD_FORM` and `CHATBOT`) already treats it as **not feature-gated** — a test pins it.

**How the editor learns the tenant is the owner (this story owns it):** `GET /tenants/hotsite` (backend `GetHotsiteContentUseCase`; BFF `GET /v1/tenants/hotsite`) gains `isPlatformOwner: boolean` — the same comparison as above, never a slug compared on the client — added to `HotsiteAdminContentResponse` in `@ikaro/types` (the `PATCH` response carries it too, since both return that type). **No staged rollout** (`docs/HOTSITE_DYNAMIC_ARCHITECTURE.md` § 7: a member missing from either side fails `pnpm architecture-check`, and the web has exhaustive switches), so this story also does the **minimal web registration** to keep `tsc` and the existing suites green, and S34 adds the real component, panel and preview: `apps/web/features/platform/hotsite/module-schemas.ts` (register `TrialSignupCtaModuleDataSchema` in `MODULE_DATA_SCHEMAS`; the parity spec `module-contract.parity.spec.ts` needs it because an unregistered type validates anything), `page-model.ts` (`MODULE_SCHEMAS` entry), `apps/web/shells/hotsite/components/HotsiteModuleView.tsx` (a `case 'TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA'` returning `null` until S34 — the switch is exhaustive), its spec's `EXPECTED` record, the `dashboard.hotsitePage.layout.modules.TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` label in both `web.json` files (beside `BOOKING_CTA`, not under a `hotsite.layout` key), and `default-layout.ts` / `manifest-schema.ts`: `MODULE_ORDER` stays the 12 shared types and a separate `OWNER_ONLY_MODULE_TYPES` set is added; `materializeLayout(existing, { isPlatformOwner })` appends the 13th disabled row **only for the owner** and `manifest-schema` accepts the type (and the owner-sized layout length) only when the draft belongs to the owner — otherwise a non-owner's Layout list would gain a row the backend rejects. S34's panel registry and visual editor consume the same flag.

**Backend use case steps:** `UpdateHotsiteContentUseCase` computes `isPlatformOwner` and runs the layout rule; `GetHotsiteContentUseCase` returns `isPlatformOwner`. **How it obtains the flag (decided):** today it only holds `HotsiteContentReader`, which carries no slug; it additionally injects `ITenantRepository` (the same port `UpdateHotsiteContentUseCase` uses) to read the calling tenant's slug and `IApplicationConfig` to read `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` and compares the two — one comparison, shared with the update path. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): the owner check is backend-side.** The backend env has the variable (optional, M25-S07) while the BFF env schema has no `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG`; the optional variable is read through the nullable `getOptional(key): string | null` that S22 adds to `IApplicationConfig` (the port is `getOrThrow`-only otherwise, and an unset slug would throw); `null` means "no owner tenant", so `isPlatformOwner` is `false` for everyone. Why: a layer never re-implements a decision another layer already makes, and the BFF would need a new env key to repeat it. S22 must be merged first (precondition for the port method, not a graph edge); this story adds no env entry.
**Backend HTTP surface:** reuses `PATCH /tenants/hotsite` (validation) and `GET /tenants/hotsite` (adds one response field) — no new route.
**BFF endpoint spec:** none new — `GET` / `PATCH /v1/tenants/hotsite` pass the typed response through.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** i18n: the non-owner error entry in both `errors.json` files and the module label in both `web.json` files. No migration (`layout` is JSON; existing hotsites are unaffected), env var or flag.

**Notes for other owners:** `docs/HOTSITE_DYNAMIC_ARCHITECTURE.md` § 4 (owner mechanism = `isPlatformOwner` on the admin GET; the component is `TrialSignupCtaModule.tsx`), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (the field on `GET /v1/tenants/hotsite`; `PLATFORM_HOTSITE_MODULE_OWNER_ONLY` `422`), `docs/ERROR_CATALOG.md` (the new code `PLATFORM_HOTSITE_MODULE_OWNER_ONLY`, `422` — entry added in the audit fix; confirm status at discovery), `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-131.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `packages/types/src/enums.ts`, `packages/types/src/hotsite.ts`, `packages/types/src/testing/hotsite-module-contract.ts` (modify)
- `packages/validation/src/hotsite.ts`, `packages/validation/src/hotsite-modules.ts` (+ specs) (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/hotsite-config.types.ts` (modify — `LayoutValidationContext` at :296 gains `isPlatformOwner`), `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/hotsite-layout.rules.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — owner rule and the new `assertValidLayoutStructure` signature, `PRESENTATION_MODULE_TYPES` entry), `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/hotsite-config.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `LayoutValidationContext` gains `isPlatformOwner`; `validateLayout` passes it), `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/errors/platform-domain.error.ts` (modify — `HotsiteModuleOwnerOnlyError`), `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/services/hotsite-template-materializer.service.ts` (+ spec) (modify — passes `false`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/get-hotsite-content.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `isPlatformOwner`)
- `packages/types/src/hotsite-schemas.ts` (modify — the strict module schema location per `docs/HOTSITE_DYNAMIC_ARCHITECTURE.md` § 7), `packages/types/src/hotsite.ts` (`TrialSignupCtaModuleData`, `isPlatformOwner` on `HotsiteAdminContentResponse`)
- web minimal registration (see the Description): `apps/web/features/platform/hotsite/module-schemas.ts`, `page-model.ts`, `default-layout.ts`, `manifest-schema.ts`, `apps/web/features/platform/hotsite/module-contract.parity.spec.ts` (if it needs the new cases), and the specs that enumerate the module types and fail when a 13th appears: `apps/web/features/platform/hotsite/module-field-coverage.spec.ts`, `apps/web/features/platform/hotsite/feature-availability.spec.ts`, `apps/web/features/platform/hotsite/template-fixtures.spec.ts`, `apps/web/shared/lib/billing/locked-modules.spec.ts` (modify each only where it iterates the closed registry; the last one pins that the type is not feature-gated), `apps/web/shells/hotsite/components/HotsiteModuleView.tsx` + `HotsiteModuleView.spec.tsx`, `apps/web/features/platform/components/hotsite/LayoutTab.tsx` (+ spec, passes the owner flag to `materializeLayout`), `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/web.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/web.json` (+ specs for each logic file)
- the non-owner error code plumbing (`PLATFORM_HOTSITE_MODULE_OWNER_ONLY`, M25-S06 pattern): `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts`, `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json`, `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/http/platform-error.mapper.ts` (+ spec) (modify — shared with S24, so S24 and S26 are **not** batchable)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/update-hotsite-content.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — pass `isPlatformOwner` into the layout rule)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
