## Wave 5 — Tests, operations and launch

### M26-S35 — Money-loop and suspended-experience E2E on the fake

**Agent:** `frontend-ts + backend-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` § Layer 5 — End-to-End Tests (Playwright), § Running Playwright locally, § E2E Selector Strategy, § Testing the billing provider — fake, sandbox, contract suite and clock, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_TESTING.md` § An E2E test that computes "today" and § An integration test seeding fixtures under fixed tenant UUIDs, `docs/CI_TRAPS.md` § Cloudflare Turnstile's test sitekey, `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-138
**Dependencies:** M26-S13 (simulate / seed / clock), M26-S26 (the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` contract and `isPlatformOwner`), M26-S27 (the billing page `billing-page-states.spec.ts` drives), M26-S28, M26-S29, M26-S30, M26-S31, M26-S32, M26-S33, M26-S34 (the pages under test — S29 for `billing-identity.spec.ts`, S34 for `hotsite-trial-signup-cta.spec.ts`), M25-S12 (the dev-clock reset it uses), M25-S31b (the suspended-experience spec it extends)
**Pattern:** Page-object / helper flows in `apps/web/e2e/helpers/billing/`; the spec files are test cases only; time and provider events are driven **only** through the dev endpoints, never by a test-only path inside a use case.

**Description:**
The spanning scenario the milestone promises, as Playwright specs against the local compose stack with the **fake provider** (`BRL_FAKE`): `ikaro-trial-signup.spec.ts` (S33's signup, from the Ikaro hotsite CTA to the account-created page and the first-manager invitation, Turnstile test sitekey); `billing-money-loop.spec.ts` (provision a trial tenant → trial banner → confirm the plan → advance the clock to ten days before trial end → the first charge exists → **Pagar agora** → simulate `PAID_PIX` → paid → advance past trial end → `ACTIVE`; a later charge unpaid → `PAST_DUE` banner → past 14 days → `SUSPENDED` with the recovery charge → pay → `ACTIVE`; and the **choose-a-smaller-plan-before-paying** path of UC-136 A14: a suspended manager opens the Plano tab, removes a feature, saves, the open recovery charge is re-issued at the lower amount, **Pagar agora** pays that amount, and the account is `ACTIVE` with only the saved features entitled); `billing-page-states.spec.ts`, `billing-identity.spec.ts`, `billing-plan.spec.ts`, `billing-cancel-reactivate.spec.ts`, `billing-banner-and-suspension.spec.ts` and `hotsite-trial-signup-cta.spec.ts` (the specs S27–S34 each named); and the **manager half of the suspended experience** that M25-S31b could not test (the billing page as the only reachable page). "Today" in any date assertion is computed in the **tenant timezone** the same way the app does, never from the runner's clock. Fixtures that use fixed tenant UUIDs have symmetric, complete setup / teardown (the shared Postgres container is reused across unrelated CI runs). Local E2E runs only in the story's worktree via `pnpm infra:e2e` and `pnpm infra:e2e:down` (CLAUDE.md §0).

**Clock isolation (the dev clock is process-global and only moves forward).** `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` runs `fullyParallel: true` with `workers: undefined` **locally only** (CI already runs `workers: 1`, so the race is a local-run hazard; the dedicated project still protects local runs and any future CI worker increase), and a +40-day advance followed by a manual sweep could suspend any other non-exempt tenant created by a concurrently running spec. The clock-moving specs (`billing-money-loop`, `billing-banner-and-suspension`, `billing-cancel-reactivate`, `billing-plan`, and any other spec that calls `billing-clock.ts`) therefore run in a **dedicated Playwright project `billing-serial`** (`fullyParallel: false`, `workers: 1`, declared to run **after** the default project via `dependencies`; the existing project is named `chromium` (`apps/web/playwright.config.ts` :32), so `dependencies: ['chromium']` must name it exactly), each on tenants created for that spec only (never the shared seeded tenants). M25-S12 exposes a dev-clock **reset** (note for the M25 owner: `POST /internal/dev/clock/reset`, local/staging only, behind `InternalApiGuard` + `PlatformAdminGuard` since M25-S15a, so `billing-clock.ts` sends both `X-Internal-Key` and `X-Platform-Admin-Key`); `billing-clock.ts` resets the offset in **`beforeAll` and `afterAll`** (decided at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18: the `beforeAll` reset means an aborted earlier spec can never leave a non-zero offset behind), and the **named mechanism** behind the "no leftover clock" AC is structural ordering plus one check: `billing-serial` runs only after the default `chromium` project (`dependencies`), and a third project `billing-clock-check` (`dependencies: ['billing-serial']`) holds one spec, `billing-clock-reset.spec.ts`, that reads `GET /internal/dev/clock` and fails unless `offsetMs` is `0`, so a spec that forgot to reset fails the run instead of poisoning the next one. **Under `--shard` (verify at story discovery with `playwright test --list --shard=1/2`):** each CI leg has its own stack, database and dev clock (`pr-tests.yml`: "each leg runs its share against its own stack, database and seed"), so the offset is per leg; the AC is that on every leg the `billing-serial` and `billing-clock-check` specs that land on it still run after that leg's `chromium` specs, and that two legs never share a clock. Asynchronous effects of the cron publisher routes (`POST /cron/billing-…` only publishes the trigger) are awaited with `expect.poll` against the page or an API read, never `waitForTimeout`. S33's **soft-navigation spec** (the Turnstile widget loads when the visitor arrives from the hotsite CTA) is owned here as `ikaro-trial-soft-navigation.spec.ts`. **Timing (verified against `.github/workflows/pr-tests.yml`, replacing the stale "30-minute cap" wording):** the Playwright job is a **2-shard matrix with `timeout-minutes: 20`**, and a leg takes about 11 minutes (about 2 of setup). The story records the added wall-clock per leg and **adds a third shard (`shard: [1, 2, 3]`) if any leg's wall-clock exceeds 16 minutes** (80% of the cap), instead of raising the cap; the `billing-serial` specs are the long ones (clock advances, polling), so their share is read from the leg reports. This is the only reason `.github/workflows/pr-tests.yml` is touched. The signup specs obtain the signup-session cookie through the dev/test issuer S24 adds (APP_ENV-gated), not through Google.

**Backend use case steps:** none.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none — uses the dev endpoints (S13, M25-S12).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

### M26-S36 — Alert policies, KPIs and as-built runbooks

**Agent:** `devops + backend-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md` § 7. Dashboards, alerts and uptime checks (the Billing alert policies and KPI paragraph) and § 4 Metrics, `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned) and § Simulate an incident (verify alerts), `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook and `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/`, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), `packages/observability/src/**`
**Dependencies:** M26-S04 (webhook metrics, dispute-opened log field), M26-S05 (stuck-creation signal), M26-S06 (sweep log fields it modifies), M26-S07 (drift, reconcile and `billing_kpi_daily` signals), M26-S08 (freeze, missing subscription), M26-S09 and M26-S10 (job log fields it modifies), M26-S12 (the `billing_template_missing` signal), M26-S24 (the relay-VM `additional_scripts` wiring the staging live check needs), M25-S15a (`billing.operator_audit`, source of the intervention KPI)
**Pattern:** Log-based metrics + alert policies in the existing `modules/monitoring/` (module variables for thresholds); each alert names its runbook.

**Description:**
Billing is operator-free by default: humans are paged only after the automatic response failed. **Log-based metrics only** — no application-defined OTel instrument is added (`docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md` §4: "No application code defines its own OTel instruments"; an SDK metric needs a matching collector pipeline), and `packages/observability` gains no counter. Each metric reads a named structured log message and field that the job stories emit; where one is missing S36 adds it (the six job use cases below). The webhook signal is a pair of log-based metrics, `billing_webhook_failure` (non-2xx other than `401`) and `billing_webhook_ok` (Cloud Run's `request_count` has no path label, so the existing 5xx policy cannot be reused). **Verified at the M26 audit: no per-request path/status log exists in the BFF** (`apps/bff/src/shared/request/request.interceptor.ts` and `middleware/correlation.middleware.ts` log nothing per request), so the plan's earlier "BFF webhook-route request logs" source does not exist today. **OPEN: decide at `/story-discovery`** — (A) read the **Cloud Run request log** (`run.googleapis.com%2Frequests`, filter on `httpRequest.requestUrl` ending `/v1/public/billing/webhooks/asaas` and `httpRequest.status`): the webhook URL Asaas calls is the **web gateway** path (S04, H2: both BFF services are internal-ingress), so the filter targets the **ikaro-web** service's request log, and it carries the status for the `401` exclusion with no code change; or (B) add one structured BFF log line (`billing_webhook_result` with `status` and `outcome`) in PR1 and list the BFF controller in Files. Default if undecided: (A), because it needs no code and the status is exactly what the policy reads.

Alert policies (thresholds are module variables, with these defaults, all **count-based** because webhook volume is a handful per day and a percentage over a tiny denominator is noise):
- **Webhook failure:** ≥ 3 failures (non-2xx other than `401`) **within 15 min** — a count over a window, because a log-based metric cannot express "consecutive" (decided at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18; the runbook and the docs wording follow) — or any `5xx`, on the webhook route, or the provider queue paused and no delivery has succeeded since it was re-enabled (S08 `provider_health` log). A BFF `401` (wrong or rotated token, scanners) is **excluded** and never pages: it is not a processing failure and a token rotation produces a short burst by design (Asaas documents a pause after 15 consecutive failures, not reproduced in the sandbox (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G3), which the "queue paused" leg covers; that leg stays raised until a delivery succeeds after the re-enable, so a wrong token that pauses the queue again keeps paging, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 4).
- **Reconcile failing:** 2 consecutive daily-pass failures or 24 h without a success (the suspension freeze starts at the same 24 h).
- **Reconcile drift:** any daily-pass charge difference (warning) and more than 5 in one run (critical).
- **Charge creation stuck:** a charge `PENDING` provider creation with ≥ 3 attempts or older than 1 h.
- **Missing subscription:** a tenant older than 30 min with none (self-heal could not create it).
- **Suspension freeze active:** self-heal logs the freeze state on every hourly run while it is set; the policy fires when the metric is > 0 in the last hour.
- **Missing platform template:** S11 / S12 emit `billing_template_missing`; one occurrence fires (billing emails are money-critical and a template can be absent at runtime for one locale).
- **Dispute opened:** S04 logs a structured `billing_dispute_opened` line for a chargeback request / dispute (no access change, no new table); one occurrence fires, so an operator can upload evidence in the provider console before its deadline.
- **Billing job did not run:** for the issue, sweep, reminders and reconcile jobs (all six jobs are created paused in production until S39; the alert is created disabled or filtered until then). **OPEN: decide at `/story-discovery` — the mechanism, not the intent.** Cloud Monitoring absence conditions are capped at about 23.5 h (verify), fire only on a series that has already reported (a job created paused never has), and the monitoring module has no `condition_absent` or `condition_matched_log` today, so a plain absence condition on a per-run line is not viable for the daily jobs. Options: (A) a **log-based counter** of each job's per-run line with a **`condition_threshold` of `count < 1`** over the job's period (sweep 2 h, daily jobs 26 h, the metric descriptor existing from the first apply so the series exists), or (B) the Scheduler's own `cloudscheduler.googleapis.com/job/attempt_count` metric with a threshold on failed/absent attempts. (A) also catches a job that fired but did nothing; (B) needs no application log line. This story does not pick one without that discovery; it does **not** use an absence condition.

The *purge step stuck* policy is M27. A missing price row and a live-adapter misconfiguration have **no alert policy** by design (CI / seed-consistency test and startup catch a missing price row; a wrong merchant profile key throws on lookup and surfaces as an ordinary backend error).

**Weekly KPIs, defined (numbers on the dashboard, never a worklist).** The reconcile **daily pass** (S07) emits one structured line `billing_kpi_daily` with counts; the dashboard widgets read it with log-based metrics, and the one KPI that does not come from it comes from `billing.operator_audit`:
- *share of charges paid with no manual action* = charges that became `PAID` in the period via webhook / reconcile ÷ charges that became `PAID` (an operator never marks a charge paid, so this is a drift indicator: PAID-by-reconcile ÷ PAID);
- *involuntary churn* = subscriptions suspended for `NON_PAYMENT` in the period and still `SUSPENDED` at its end ÷ active at its start;
- *webhook processing lag* = `received_at → processed_at` of `provider_events` (the table stores no provider timestamp, so a payment-to-webhook lag is **not** measurable and is dropped);
- *reconcile corrections per run* = the daily pass's corrected-charge count;
- *time from payment to reactivation* = `paid_at` → the `SubscriptionReactivated` time for reactivating payments;
- *open exceptions older than 24 h* = `PENDING` provider creations, `provider_cancel_pending` charges and held recovery charges older than 24 h;
- *operator interventions per 100 active tenants per month* = `billing.operator_audit` rows per month ÷ active subscriptions × 100, emitted by the same daily line (a count read through the audit repository), so no BigQuery or SQL dashboard is needed. **Verified at the audit:** `IOperatorAuditRepository` has only `save` and `findByIdAndTenant`, so this story adds a count method (`countSince(since: Date): Promise<number>`, a cross-tenant platform count) to the port, the TypeORM repository and the in-memory double, and — because the query has no `tenant_id` filter — a **standalone index on `billing.operator_audit (occurred_at)`** (the existing index is `(tenant_id, occurred_at DESC)`, which a query without `tenant_id` cannot seek; CLAUDE.md §7, standalone index for a cross-tenant system job; a migration, listed in Files, with `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.operator_audit — who changed what` updated in the same PR).

`docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned) is updated to **as-built**, and the section heading itself is renamed (every `§ Billing (planned)` pointer in the canonical docs and this plan is updated in the same PR, since the doc-pointer check resolves headings). **What exists today (verified at the audit):** the Alert runbooks list already has Webhook, Provider incident, Drift, Charge creation, Provisioning, Provisioned-but-no-subscription, Dev clock, Purge and Catalog, and the Operator cases list already has suspend / unsuspend / grace, exempt, **chargeback**, refunds, LGPD requests, interim offboarding and secret rotation — this story **verifies those against the built behaviour** and **adds the four runbooks the alerts above need and the list lacks: Dispute (opened), Job-did-not-run, Missing template and Freeze active** (the freeze entry says what clears it: one successful reconcile), using real commands (`gcloud scheduler jobs run ikaro-cron-billing-<name>`, the operator routes). Three entries change from the planned text: (1) **chargeback** — until the Asaas event that finalizes a lost dispute is verified (S37, S39), chargeback-driven `PAYMENT_REVERSED` suspension is **off**; an operator who learns a dispute was lost suspends the tenant manually (`OPERATOR` reason) and uploads evidence in the provider console; (2) **refund trap** — a **full** refund of the current period triggers the `PAYMENT_REVERSED` suspension, so the safe order is: prefer a partial refund; if a full refund is required, mark the tenant exempt — after `PAYMENT_REVERSED` the operator unsuspend is **not** allowed (`BILLING_UNSUSPEND_NOT_ALLOWED`), so exempt is the way back; a full refund of the **first** invoice while `TRIALING` suspends nothing (S04), the charge stays `REFUNDED` and is treated as unpaid at trial end, so a recovery charge is issued then (S06 rule 2); (3) **secret rotation** — the webhook token in S01's order, plus the **GitHub repository copy** of the sandbox key that S37's nightly job uses; the entry **names every copy** (Secret Manager `asaas-api-key` and `asaas-webhook-token` per environment project, S01; GitHub repository secrets `ASAAS_API_KEY_SANDBOX` and `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN_SANDBOX`, S37), so none is forgotten at a rotation. Cancelling an NFS-e after a refund / chargeback is the accountant's procedure recorded in S39. A new log-based metric can take up to 10 minutes to become queryable (`time_sleep.log_metric_propagation`, 600 s, runs on every apply that adds a metric); the module's `triggers` map gains each new metric the alert policies reference, and **`modules/monitoring/tests/resources_unit_test.tftest.hcl` (lines ~157-162) asserts the exact trigger keys, so that assertion is updated with them** (a failing `terraform test` otherwise).

**PR sequence:** 2 PRs, because the story spans `infra/terraform/**` and `apps/**` and CI's `no-infra-app-mix` job (`.github/workflows/pr-quality.yml`) rejects a mixed PR without the `infra-app-mix-ok` label: PR1 (app + docs) — the structured log fields / lines the job stories must emit (`billing_kpi_daily`, `billing_dispute_opened`, per-run job lines, freeze state) + `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` + `docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md`; PR2 (devops) — metric / alert definitions in `modules/monitoring` + envs thresholds, merged after PR1 so the log messages exist. (If the owner prefers one PR, it carries `infra-app-mix-ok` with the apply-first note.) Apply to staging and trigger **each** condition (see the check below) before trusting it; no new topic and no new secret.
**IAM / permissions:** none beyond the monitoring module's existing grants.
**Live-verification check:** on staging after apply, using `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Simulate an incident (verify alerts) as the base procedure. That section today only describes stopping Cloud SQL, which trips unrelated alerts; S36 therefore **adds a webhook-specific step** to it (an authenticated `POST` to the staging webhook route with the real `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, read with the operator's own identity, while processing fails) and uses that step here. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18):** the failure is produced through the **fake provider**, not by stopping Cloud SQL (which trips unrelated alerts): the staging test subscription uses `BRL_FAKE` (until S37 there is no Asaas adapter, so `simulate` works only on `*_FAKE` keys), and the fake's `PROVIDER_5XX` mode makes the webhook's `fetchChargeStatus` re-read fail, so the route answers a retryable `5xx`. **The staging BFF is internal-ingress only, so the replay needs the relay VM** (`scripts/relay-vm-up.sh` before and `scripts/relay-vm-down.sh` after; it carries the `additional_scripts` wiring S24 applies in Foundation, hence the S24 dependency), or the web-gateway path if S24's check shows it reachable. (1) Webhook failure fires and emails the `notification_email` channel, recovers, and a burst of wrong-token `401`s does **not** fire it; (2) missing template: trigger one missing-template failure (a throwaway locale / key) and confirm the alert; (3) reconcile failing / job-did-not-run: pause the staging reconcile scheduler job past the threshold (the freeze is global per provider, not per tenant: pause it on staging only and resume afterwards) and confirm both alerts and that `freeze active` is observable; (4) drift, creation-stuck, missing-subscription, dispute-opened: trigger each once on staging through the fake / seed paths (a drifted charge via `simulate` DELAYED, a held creation via the fake's `PROVIDER_5XX`, a subscription row deleted before self-heal, a `CHARGEBACK_OPENED` simulate) — or, for any alert that cannot be triggered live, state it in the PR as proven by the unit / terraform test only; (5) every new metric is queryable (`gcloud logging metrics list`). Pass = each alert observed firing (and recovering where it can), silent on the `401` burst, metrics listed, and the runbook link in each alert body resolves.

**Backend use case steps:** none.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** one expand-only migration (the standalone `billing.operator_audit (occurred_at)` index for the intervention KPI); no i18n keys, env vars or flags.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/main.tf`, `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/variables.tf`, `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/tests/resources_unit_test.tftest.hcl`, `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/tests/variables_unit_test.tftest.hcl` (modify), `infra/terraform/envs/staging/main.tf`, `infra/terraform/envs/prod/main.tf` (modify — thresholds, only if non-default)
- the job use cases created in S05–S10 (modify — `tenant_id`, counts and correlation fields added to their log lines): `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/issue-next-charges.use-case.ts`, `sweep-subscription-statuses.use-case.ts`, `reconcile-charges.use-case.ts`, `self-heal.use-case.ts`, `publish-billing-reminders.use-case.ts`, `purge-provider-events.use-case.ts`
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/process-provider-webhook.use-case.ts` (modify — the structured `billing_dispute_opened` line; S04 owns the file)
- the operator-intervention KPI: `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/operator-audit-repository.port.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/repositories/typeorm-operator-audit.repository.ts` (+ spec, `.integration.spec.ts`) and the in-memory double the specs use (modify — `countSince`), `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/migrations/<timestamp>-AddOperatorAuditOccurredAtIndex.ts` (new — the standalone `(occurred_at)` index; the timestamp is global, taken at implementation), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` (modify — the index row under `billing.operator_audit`)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.webhook.public.controller.ts` (+ spec) (modify **only if** the webhook-metric source is the BFF structured log line, option B of the OPEN item above)
- `docs/RUNBOOKS.md`, `docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md` (modify — as-built; log-based only; the alert list above; the webhook-failure policy excludes `401`; the missing-template policy is kept; the KPI definitions and the `billing_kpi_daily` line; the webhook-specific simulate-incident step)

### M26-S37 — Real `AsaasBillingProvider`, sandbox contract run and the nightly job **[GATE]**

**Agent:** `backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` (the verified Asaas facts, written by M25-S01), `docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10. PAYMENT PROVIDERS, `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` § Testing the billing provider — fake, sandbox, contract suite and clock, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_TESTING.md` § Billing provider tests, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), `docs/CI_CD_PIPELINE.md` § Nightly billing provider contract job, `docs/GITHUB_WORKFLOWS_GUIDELINES.md`, `docs/CI_TRAPS.md`, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § OpenRouter chatbot outbound HTTP resilience (the undici retry / timeout traps apply to any outbound HTTP client)
**Dependencies:** M25-S01 (verified provider facts), M25-S14 (port, fake, contract suite, config guard, `BillingProviderRegistry`), M26-S01 (secrets and env), M26-S04 (the webhook that normalises Asaas events), M26-S05 (the issue job the staging live check drives), M26-S07 (reconcile, the second consumer of `fetchChargeStatus`)
**Pattern:** Adapter implementing `BillingProviderPort` (Strategy-per-provider); the **same contract suite** runs against both implementations; a scheduled GitHub workflow runs the sandbox leg.

**Description:**
Replace the fake in production with the real thing, and keep the fake honest. **`AsaasBillingProvider`** implements the port against Asaas: `ensureCustomer` (create **or update**: `name` + `cpfCnpj` required, `notificationDisabled = true`, **our tenant / account id as the customer `externalReference`**, and the billing identity's **email and address (street, number, complement, neighborhood, `postalCode`/CEP, city, state)** because provider-automatic NFS-e needs the payer's full data — `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A15; an identity that changed since the customer was created updates the existing Asaas customer, S16 syncs it at the next issue). **Verified at the audit: `ProviderCustomerIdentity` has no address fields today** (`name`, `email`, `taxId`, `countryCode` only), so this story extends the **port type, the fake and the contract suite's `IDENTITY` fixture** (`apps/backend/src/test/contracts/billing-provider.contract.ts`) with the address, and updates the callers S05/S16 built (address optional on the type until the identity is complete, S05's address hold), `createCharge` (`billingType = UNDEFINED`, our charge id as `externalReference`, hosted `invoiceUrl`, **explicit zero interest and fine and no late-fee field** — `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A5: the tenant owes the list price only — amount sent as the decimal-real value converted from `MinorAmount`, and the `returnUrl` mapped to Asaas's `callback` where the account's domain rule allows it, M26-D10 (observed: the sandbox refuses a `callback` with 400 `invalid_object` until a site is registered on the account, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A25); both are marked *unverified* below), `regeneratePaymentLink`, `cancelCharge`, `fetchChargeStatus` (the truth the webhook is only a hint for), `parseWebhook` (the per-method sequences: Pix `PAYMENT_RECEIVED` only; boleto `CONFIRMED`→`RECEIVED`; card `CONFIRMED` then `RECEIVED` ~32 days later) and the provider-health read / webhook re-enable **as S01 of M25 observed them**. `startAutoBilling` / `revokeAutoBilling` throw a typed `NotSupported` (M29). **No duplicate provider objects (M26-S05 M8 rule).** Asaas does not enforce uniqueness on `externalReference` (two creates gave two customers and two charges in the sandbox), and only `POST /payments` honours an `Idempotency-Key` header, answering a repeat with 409 and an empty body (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A23, observed), so a retry after an ambiguous timeout or a crash between the call and storing `READY` could create a second payment or customer. Therefore `createCharge` sends the charge id as the `Idempotency-Key` and treats a 409 as "already received", then looks the charge up by `externalReference` (`GET /payments?externalReference=<chargeId>`, observed to filter) and returns the existing one; `ensureCustomer` looks the customer up by its `externalReference` before creating (the lookup is not atomic and `POST /customers` ignores the key, so two concurrent first calls for one tenant are serialized per tenant by the caller), and the HTTP client does **not** blindly retry a `POST` that may have reached the provider: it retries only connect-timeout / connection-refused (request never sent) and idempotent `GET`s; an ambiguous `POST` failure surfaces as a retryable error, and the next attempt starts with the lookup. **Status and event mapping (a table in the adapter and in `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`, unit-tested against recorded fixtures):** webhook events — `PAYMENT_CONFIRMED` / `PAYMENT_RECEIVED` → `PaymentConfirmed` (first of the two wins, the other is a no-op), `PAYMENT_OVERDUE` → `PaymentOverdue` (confirm-only), `PAYMENT_REFUNDED` → full refund, `PAYMENT_PARTIALLY_REFUNDED` → partial refund (no access change), `PAYMENT_REFUND_IN_PROGRESS` / `PAYMENT_REFUND_DENIED` → logged, no change, `PAYMENT_CHARGEBACK_REQUESTED` / `_DISPUTE` / `PAYMENT_AWAITING_CHARGEBACK_REVERSAL` → dispute opened (no access change, `billing_dispute_opened` log), the lost-dispute event → `PaymentChargeback` **only once verified** (fake-only until then), `PAYMENT_DELETED` → `PaymentCancelled` (**corrected at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18**: the earlier "ignored" disagreed with the built normalizer and with S04, and this story follows the code), `PAYMENT_CREATED` / `PAYMENT_UPDATED` / `PAYMENT_RESTORED` and **every unknown event type → ignored, still `200`**. **The normalizer already exists** (`asaas-event-normalizer.ts`, built by M25-S14 with the sandbox-observed events and today mapping `PAYMENT_CHARGEBACK_REQUESTED` to `PaymentChargeback`): this story **extends** it (and S04's split of opened dispute from lost chargeback) rather than creating `asaas-event.normalizer.ts`. `fetchChargeStatus` statuses (*the sandbox reached only `PENDING`, `RECEIVED`, the card `CONFIRMED` and `REFUNDED`; the rest of the list is from the documentation, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G16*): `PENDING` → `PENDING`; `CONFIRMED`, `RECEIVED`, `RECEIVED_IN_CASH` → `PAID` (the card `CONFIRMED` is access-restoring by design); `OVERDUE` → `OVERDUE`; `REFUNDED` → `REFUNDED`; `REFUND_REQUESTED` / `REFUND_IN_PROGRESS` → unchanged (`PAID`); `CHARGEBACK_REQUESTED` / `CHARGEBACK_DISPUTE` / `AWAITING_CHARGEBACK_REVERSAL` → `PAID` with `disputeOpen = true` (the field S04 adds to `NormalizedChargeStatus` with `amountMinor`, converted from Asaas's decimal `value`; the adapter, the fake and the contract suite all populate both — decided at the M26 audit: the type had neither, so S04's "amount matches" check could not otherwise work; this story states which of `CHARGEBACK` / `PARTIALLY_REFUNDED` the adapter ever returns once the sandbox shows it, and records the rest as documentation-only, G16); `DUNNING_REQUESTED` / `AWAITING_RISK_ANALYSIS` → unchanged; a payment returned with the `deleted` flag → `CANCELED`. **`Charge.method` source:** the charge is created `UNDEFINED`, so `method` stays null until the payer picks; it is filled from the payment's `billingType` on the paid fact (PIX / BOLETO / CREDIT_CARD) and, if Asaas still reports `UNDEFINED` (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G9), recorded as unknown and the settlement margin keys on `payment_return_seen_at` (M25-S01 check 7 observed which). The `receiptUrl` for `markPaid` is read from the payment's `transactionReceiptUrl` / `invoiceUrl` by `fetchChargeStatus` (`NormalizedChargeStatus.receiptUrl`). The provider-side "cannot cancel because it is already paid" error is raised as the typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError` (M26-S05/S06; the contract suite asserts it). **`ProviderPaymentLink.expiresAt` (H8, found at the audit):** the port type is non-null, but Asaas documents no expiry for `invoiceUrl` and `charges.payment_url_expires_at` is nullable. **OPEN: decide at `/story-discovery`** — (A) the adapter defines `expiresAt` as a stated rule (for example the end of the charge's due date plus a fixed number of days, a code constant in the adapter, recorded in `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`), or (B) the port type becomes `Date | null` and the fake, the contract suite and S15 handle `null` (a link with no known expiry is simply re-fetched on demand). Either way the unit test pins the choice and the contract suite asserts the fake and Asaas agree on it. Asaas requires a `User-Agent` header on API calls (an empty one gets 400 `user_agent_not_informed`, observed, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A24): the HTTP client always sets one. The outbound HTTP client carries an **explicit retry config and per-attempt timeouts** (undici excludes `POST` and connect-timeout errors from its retry defaults; a shared `AbortSignal.timeout()` caps total time, not per attempt) and never logs secrets or the tax id. Registered with `useClass`; **every key is registered in every environment, so the app always boots; `resolve()` rejects a live key outside production and a `*_FAKE` / `*_SANDBOX` profile key in production** (the M25-S14 guard, on lookup only, never at startup). A live adapter instance that needs the live secrets is provided only when `ASAAS_API_KEY` is set (optional, S01), a configuration condition, not an exception: without it the live key has no adapter and `resolve()` throws `ProviderNotSupportedError` (skip-and-log, S05), and the production boot does not require the Asaas secrets. **Contract suite run:** the existing `billing-provider.contract.ts` runs against the Asaas **sandbox** (staging keys), asserting that the fake and Asaas agree on every behaviour the money loop depends on **that a sandbox can produce**. A GitHub runner has no public URL (it cannot receive webhooks), the sandbox has no overdue action and rejects a past due date (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 5), the card `CONFIRMED` → `RECEIVED` ~32-day gap cannot occur in a nightly run, and a lost chargeback cannot be produced in the sandbox (M25-S01 deferred it). The story therefore ships a **case-by-case table** in `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`, each case tagged:
- *sandbox-live* (API calls and `fetchChargeStatus` reads): create customer, create customer twice yields one, create charge, create charge twice yields one (the same `Idempotency-Key` answers 409, then the lookup by `externalReference`), link, regenerate link, cancel, cancel of a paid charge raises `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`, paid (via `POST /v3/sandbox/payment/{id}/confirm`, observed in M25-S01 check 3) read back as `PAID`; **refund is not a sandbox-live case** (the contract driver's `capabilities.refund` is `false` for the sandbox: a boleto refund completes only after the payer opens the returned `requestUrl`, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G8, so it is not automatable) — a refund runs as *recorded-payload* (the `REFUNDED` / `PARTIALLY_REFUNDED` events and the `REFUNDED` status read-back, from the M25-S01 capture) and *fake-only* (driving it);
- *recorded-payload* (normaliser only, payloads captured from the sandbox / docs): the per-method event sequences, duplicate and out-of-order delivery, idempotent re-delivery, invalid token / signature, unknown event ignored;
- *fake-only*: overdue (unless the sandbox endpoint is captured), delayed card `RECEIVED`, chargeback lost, invalid location, provider 5xx.
Any divergence is fixed in the **fake** (and the corrected sequences in `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`) or reported as a provider fact. **Nightly job:** a scheduled workflow `.github/workflows/nightly-billing-contract.yml` runs the sandbox-live and recorded-payload cases using the sandbox keys as repository secrets; it is **not a required check** on `main`, a failure opens an issue rather than blocking merges (`docs/CI_CD_PIPELINE.md` § Nightly billing provider contract job). **Workflow hygiene (this repository and its logs are public, `docs/GITHUB_WORKFLOWS_GUIDELINES.md` §7):** `permissions: contents: read` plus `issues: write` for the failure step only, `persist-credentials: false`, SHA-pinned actions like `weekly-jobs.yml`, `schedule` + `workflow_dispatch`; the issue step de-duplicates (it comments on the open "nightly billing contract failing" issue instead of opening a new one each night); the suite output never prints invoice URLs, customer names, tax ids or keys (the logger redaction AC below also covers the test output), and a failure issue carries the failing case names and a run link only. The sandbox account is **shared with staging**, so nightly payments also fire webhooks at the staging BFF webhook (A2: unknown customer / charge → `200`, no state change); the nightly customers carry a recognisable `externalReference` prefix. The sandbox key exists in two places (Secret Manager for staging, S01; the GitHub repository secret for the nightly job); the S36 secret-rotation runbook entry covers both copies. **Gate:** M25-S01 already **observed** in the sandbox (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 2) `invoiceUrl` behaviour after cancel and after paid, the webhook payload fields including `externalReference`, the `externalReference` maximum length (100 characters) and the minimum charge per method (R$ 5,00); S37 **re-confirms** those results on the built adapter. Two items the sandbox could **not** show stay production gates and are not S37 sandbox checks: `invoiceUrl` behaviour after the due date (G1) and whether `notificationDisabled = true` silences paid receipts (G4); S37 also does not rediscover the results above (the recorded request / response fixtures are produced from them) and records any difference in the provider doc. What is genuinely **new** here and recorded in the provider doc: **the event that finalizes a lost chargeback** (A11, gate 5) — the sandbox cannot produce a lost dispute, so it is verified from Asaas's documentation / support or from the first real event, and until then the normalizer maps no chargeback event to `PAYMENT_REVERSED` (S04) and the S04 chargeback-suspension criterion stays carried open (the manual runbook path covers it, S36); Asaas's **callback-domain rule** for the `returnUrl` (S28; *unverified, own knowledge: the `successUrl` domain must match the account's registered site*); the sandbox payment-confirm endpoint and any force-overdue endpoint (*unverified*); and production-account behaviour, which S39 re-checks. Gate 4's follow-up stands: S12 sends Ikaro's own `ChargePaid` receipt; if Asaas still sends one the duplicate is recorded and the S12 copy adjusted. The webhook-queue re-enable through the API was in scope of M25-S01 check 5 and is re-confirmed on the real adapter, not treated as unknown; if the API cannot re-enable the webhook, the self-heal alert path (S08) and the runbook already cover it.

**PR sequence:** 2 PRs. PR1 (app): the adapter, its unit tests, the contract run, module wiring. PR2 (devops, `infra-app-mix-ok` not needed — `.github/workflows` only): the nightly workflow and the repository secrets (**manual step stated explicitly:** the operator adds exactly two GitHub repository secrets, `ASAAS_API_KEY_SANDBOX` and `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN_SANDBOX`, in GitHub → Settings → Secrets before the first scheduled run; they are the CI copies of the Secret Manager entries `asaas-api-key` and `asaas-webhook-token` of S01 (staging project), and S36's rotation runbook names all four).
**IAM / permissions:** GitHub repository secrets (sandbox only); no cloud IAM change.
**Live-verification check:** (1) trigger the workflow manually (`gh workflow run nightly-billing-contract.yml`) and confirm it completes green against the sandbox, with the suite's test names visible in the run log; (2) on staging with a `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` subscription, create a real sandbox charge through S05's job and pay it with the sandbox's payment simulator. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18, D1): until this story the staging test subscriptions use `BRL_FAKE`** (the registry knows only the fake, and `simulate` works only on `*_FAKE` keys), and S13's seed creates `BRL_FAKE` subscriptions, so this check uses a **fresh staging tenant provisioned after this story deploys** (it resolves `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` from the merchant-profile-key rule), not the seed. The staging BFF ingress is internal-only (R7), so Asaas **cannot** reach it: the webhook is verified by replaying a payload recorded from the sandbox (with the sandbox token) from the relay VM (`scripts/relay-vm-up.sh` / `relay-vm-down.sh`, wired by S24's Foundation `additional_scripts` PR) against the staging webhook route (`200`, `provider_events` row, charge `PAID` after the `fetchChargeStatus` re-read), and by the nightly recorded-payload cases. **Which URL Asaas calls (found at the audit):** the registered webhook URL is the **web gateway** path `https://<web-host>/v1/public/billing/webhooks/asaas` (the only public route; both BFF services are internal-ingress, `apps/web/app/v1/[...path]/route.ts`), not a BFF host; the gateway-resolved client IP makes `@SkipThrottle` mandatory on that route and `@BypassWebOnlyGuard` is then redundant for it (S04 states both). Real Asaas → web gateway → BFF reachability, and any Cloudflare / Cloud Armor / WAF rule on that path, is proven by S39's production smoke charge, before signups are opened; (3) confirm that on staging (which runs `NODE_ENV=production` with `APP_ENV=staging`) the app boots and `resolve()` of a live key throws `MerchantProfileKeyNotAllowedError`; (4) the ingress / `vpc_egress` check of `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), per environment: on staging the backend's outbound calls to the Asaas API leave through the configured egress (a real sandbox charge created proves it); the webhook-path reachability by the provider is S39's production check; (5) every open gate named in the Gate paragraph (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`) has its observed result written into the provider doc. **Pass condition, per item:** (1) the workflow run is green and the run link is in the PR; (2) the sandbox charge is `PAID` in `billing.charges` and the replayed webhook returned `200` with a `provider_events` row (the commands and the observed values in the PR); (3) the boot log line and the thrown error name are quoted; (4) the sandbox charge creation proves egress, with the `gcloud` output of the service's `vpc_egress` setting; (5) each gate has a line in `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` (observed, or carried with an owner). **Each of the five gets its own line in a PR comment** (check number, command or link, observed result), so "the five observed" is verifiable, not asserted. Pass = all five lines present.

**Backend use case steps:** none (adapter).
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none (env from S01).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/asaas-billing.provider.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`), `asaas-http.client.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/asaas-event-normalizer.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (**modify** — it exists since M25-S14: the corrected event table, the dispute mapping and the lost-dispute event once verified)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/billing-provider.port.ts` (modify — `ProviderCustomerIdentity` gains the address; `NormalizedChargeStatus` gains `amountMinor` / `disputeOpen` if S04 has not already; the `expiresAt` decision), `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/fake-billing.provider.ts` (+ spec) (modify — the identity address, `amountMinor` / `disputeOpen`, any divergence the sandbox run finds)
- `apps/backend/src/test/contracts/billing-provider.contract.ts` (modify — the `IDENTITY` fixture gains the address; each case gains its tag *sandbox-live* / *recorded-payload* / *fake-only*; the sandbox driver sets `capabilities.refund = false`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/asaas-billing.provider.contract.spec.ts` (new — the sandbox contract harness: runs the shared suite against the Asaas sandbox; tagged to run only when the sandbox env is present) and `asaas-billing.provider.contract.tagging.spec.ts` (new — the **E12 guard**: fails when a contract case has neither a sandbox run nor a `fake-only` tag, so "named, never silently skipped" is enforced by a test)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/billing-provider.registry.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — M25-S14 created it and said "M26 adds the Asaas keys": `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` and the live key now resolve to `AsaasBillingProvider`, guard unchanged)
- `packages/architecture-check/architecture-policy.json` (modify — M25-S14 says "M26-S37 appends the Asaas adapter path to each" `externalSideEffectPorts` entry, so `transactional-io` covers the Asaas adapter)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify — Asaas adapter registered with `useClass`)
- `.github/workflows/nightly-billing-contract.yml` (new)
- `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`, `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`, `docs/CI_CD_PIPELINE.md`, `docs/GITHUB_WORKFLOWS_GUIDELINES.md` (modify — observed results; "planned" becomes built), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (modify only if the built Asaas adapter or port differs from § Provider port — the method list, including `returnUrl`, the account-health read and webhook-queue re-enable, is already there, M26-D10)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] Charges for a Brazilian tenant are created at, and payments confirmed by, the real payment provider; nothing in the product changes shape from what the fake produced.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Each port method against recorded HTTP fixtures; the **full event and status mapping tables** (Pix / boleto / card sequences, refund, partial refund, dispute opened, `PAYMENT_CREATED/UPDATED/DELETED/RESTORED`, an unknown event type ignored with `200`, every `fetchChargeStatus` status, the `deleted` flag, `Charge.method` from `billingType`); retry and timeout behaviour (a `POST` that may have reached the provider is not blindly retried); no secret, tax id, invoice URL or customer name in logs
  - [ ] **No duplicate provider objects:** `createCharge` twice with one `externalReference` and `ensureCustomer` twice yield one provider object (lookup before create); `ensureCustomer` maps email + address (CEP) and updates an existing customer; amounts convert `MinorAmount` ↔ Asaas decimal value without rounding drift
  - [ ] The registry resolves `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` and the live key to the Asaas adapter, throws `ProviderNotSupportedError` for a live key while `ASAAS_API_KEY` is unset, and `architecture-check` flags a provider call inside `txManager.run()` on the Asaas adapter
  - [ ] `ensureCustomer` sends `notificationDisabled = true`; `createCharge` sends explicit zero interest and fine (`interest: {value: 0}`, `fine: {value: 0}`, accepted in the sandbox) and no late-fee field, and an `externalReference` within the verified maximum length of 100 characters (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 1, item 1; A5, A10), asserted on the recorded request fixtures
  - [ ] **PCI:** no card data touches Ikaro — no PAN, CVV or expiry field exists in any request / response DTO, form, log line or database column of the Billing context, and the only payment surface is the provider-hosted invoice page (`docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10: Ikaro never stores card data and never renders a card form); a unit test over the Billing DTO and entity schemas asserts no such field name
  - [ ] Chargeback: the normalizer maps the verified lost-dispute event to `PaymentChargeback`; if it could not be verified, S04's chargeback-suspension criterion is recorded as carried open
  - [ ] Config guard: `resolve()` rejects a live key outside production and fake / sandbox keys in production; the app boots in every environment
- Integration:
  - [ ] The contract suite passes on the **fake** on every PR, and its sandbox-live and recorded-payload cases pass on the **Asaas sandbox** in the nightly job; each case is tagged in the table in `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` (fake-only cases are named, never silently skipped)
  - [ ] **Tagging guard (named, never silently skipped):** `asaas-billing.provider.contract.tagging.spec.ts` fails when a case of `billing-provider.contract.ts` has neither a sandbox run nor a `fake-only` tag
  - [ ] The identity address (street, number, complement, neighborhood, CEP, city, state) flows from S16's identity through the port, the fake and the contract `IDENTITY` fixture into `ensureCustomer`; a changed identity updates the existing customer; `PAYMENT_DELETED` normalises to `PaymentCancelled`, an opened dispute to the dispute-opened event (never `PaymentChargeback`)
  - [ ] Nightly workflow: least-privilege `permissions`, `persist-credentials: false`, SHA-pinned actions, de-duplicated failure issue, no PII / URL / key in the log (workflow lint clean)
  - [ ] The sandbox contract run creates a customer with an alphanumeric CNPJ (`12ABC34501DE35`) and a charge for it, and records the result as launch gate G17 in `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`; a refusal is a stuck condition for the BR charge path, not a silent skip
- Tenant isolation: none — the adapter carries no tenant data beyond the provider customer reference
- E2E: none — live check above
- [ ] Checkov clean (workflow lint)
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S38 — Operator price changes — exact, percentage and the announcement fan-out (UC-112)

**Agent:** `backend-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-112 (all, A1–A8), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API (the three price endpoints and both request / response shapes), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: FeaturePrice (versions, `batch_id`, supersession), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.feature_prices`, `billing.feature_price_versions`, `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` § SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (partly built) and § Transactions
**Dependencies:** M25-S04 (`FeaturePrice` aggregate, seed, read side), M25-S06 (error-code plumbing pattern), M25-S15a (`PlatformAdminGuard` in shared, operator audit pattern), M25-S16 and M25-S20 (gating decorators and the route-completeness detector for the new internal routes), M26-S03 (`SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced` topic), M26-S05 (the end-to-end AC: the issue job reads the version at the period start), M26-S12 (the announcement email handler)
**Pattern:** Command use cases with a `dryRun` mode computing the same result as the real run; one transaction writing versions + per-tenant outbox events; `previewHash` guarding the apply.

**Description:**
**Last wave, and a launch prerequisite (decided at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18: S38 is a dependency of S39).** Why: the Terms state the 40-day price-change notice this story enforces, so launching with a Terms promise no code keeps would be wrong; the launch prices themselves are seeded in M25-S04, and this story is the operator's way to change them later without a release. `PUT /internal/billing/feature-prices` creates a price (feature × country × tier × cycle, first `amountMinor`, currency) or sets `selectable = false` (A2: existing subscribers keep renewing it); `POST /internal/billing/feature-prices/adjust?dryRun=` is the normal way to raise prices (`percent`, `roundToMinor` default one whole real, `effectiveFrom`, `reason`, optional `cycles` / `exclude`): it selects every price of the country in force today that is `selectable` or still used by a non-exempt item, rounds half-up, and — **in one transaction** — inserts one `feature_price_versions` row per price sharing a `batch_id`, supersedes any pending version (A5) and writes **one `SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced` outbox event per affected tenant** with only that tenant's changed features, new total and its resolved `recipientEmail` (S03); `POST …/{featureKey}/{countryCode}/{tier}/versions?dryRun=` schedules one exact price. A **dry run** returns every new price, the affected tenants (`TRIALING`/`ACTIVE`/`PAST_DUE` with an active or next-period scheduled item in that country, not `exempt`; suspended tenants are not notified, trial tenants are), sample tenant totals and, for a percentage run, a `previewHash` — saving and sending nothing. Guards: currency must match the country (A1); cycle other than `MONTHLY` rejected until M30 (A3); an increase with less than `PRICE_CHANGE_MIN_NOTICE_DAYS + CHARGE_ISSUE_LEAD_DAYS` (30 + 10 = 40) days notice (**constants owner, found at the audit — neither exists in code yet:** `CHARGE_ISSUE_LEAD_DAYS = 10` is added by S05 to `billing-constants.ts` and imported here; this story adds `PRICE_CHANGE_MIN_NOTICE_DAYS = 30` and `PRICE_ANNOUNCE_MAX_TENANTS = 5000` to the same file) → `422 BILLING_PRICE_NOTICE_TOO_SHORT` (a decrease needs none but still an explicit future `effectiveFrom`, A4); a price that would not change → `422 BILLING_PRICE_UNCHANGED` (A7); the active prices moved between dry run and apply → `409 BILLING_PRICE_BATCH_CHANGED` (the apply carries `expectedPreviewHash`, A8); no affected tenants → versions saved, no events (A6). `effectiveFrom` is a **service-period start date**: an invoice snapshots the new amount only when its period starts on or after it, and issued invoices are untouched (S05's line computation already reads the version at the period start). The change is audited on `feature_price_versions` (`created_by`, `reason`, `batch_id`), not `operator_audit`. **`announced_at` semantics (stated at the audit; the schema only says "when managers were notified, NULL for seed versions"):** the apply sets `announced_at` to the apply time on every version it inserts, **in the same transaction** that writes the per-tenant events, **including when no tenant is affected** (A6: versions saved, no events) — otherwise S23's public `upcoming` (which requires `announced_at IS NOT NULL`) would never show a scheduled price; a version scheduled by `ScheduleFeaturePriceVersionUseCase` follows the same rule; a dry run never sets it. **Value the S39 launch checklist cites:** S38 now precedes S39 (see Dependencies there), so the Terms' 40-day notice wording is enforced by this story's code before launch.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`CreateFeaturePriceUseCase`**, 2. **`AdjustFeaturePricesUseCase`** (dry run + apply), 3. **`ScheduleFeaturePriceVersionUseCase`** (dry run + apply).
**Backend HTTP surface:** new controller `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices-admin.controller.ts` — the three routes above (`InternalApiGuard` + `PlatformAdminGuard`, each also `@NoFeatureGate()` with `@SuspensionAccess('ANY_AUTHENTICATED')` — the value the built `/internal/*` operator controllers already use, e.g. `internal-billing-operator.controller.ts` — as the M25 route-completeness detector requires).

**Concurrency primitive (decided here).** `previewHash` only detects that the prices changed **between** the dry run and the apply; it is not a lock — two applies that both read before either commits would both pass it. The apply therefore takes a **row lock on the affected `feature_prices` rows**: `featurePriceRepo.findByCountryForUpdate(countryCode, ids?)` selects the country's price rows `FOR UPDATE` in one batched query with a deterministic `ORDER BY id` (CLAUDE.md §7 race primitives), then re-computes the hash **after** the lock and compares it with `expectedPreviewHash`; the existing `UNIQUE (feature_price_id, effective_from) WHERE superseded_at IS NULL` on `feature_price_versions` (`docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`) is the second line of defence against two pending versions for one date. The lock is **already catalogued** in `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (the section `B-P1 — price-list rows, one batched FOR UPDATE`, written ahead of the code; it never nests with B-R1 — the price apply touches no subscription row): this story flips it from planned to **built** and names its concurrency test (below). **Fan-out batching:** the affected tenants are selected through the existing subscription-item indexes in pages (keyset by `tenant_id`), and the per-tenant outbox rows are written in batches inside the same transaction; the dry run reports the count so the operator sees the size. The operator route is rare and bounded by the tenant count (low thousands, the milestone's scale assumption), so one transaction is kept for atomicity (a rollback leaves no events); if the count ever exceeds a configured ceiling (`PRICE_ANNOUNCE_MAX_TENANTS`, a code constant of 5000 beside the other defined-once Billing constants, not an env var) the apply is refused with a clear message rather than partially applied.

**Error codes (this story is the first thrower, so it owns the plumbing — M25-S06 translated only the two signup tax-id codes):** `BILLING_PRICE_NOTICE_TOO_SHORT` (default `422`), `BILLING_PRICE_UNCHANGED` (`422`), `BILLING_PRICE_BATCH_CHANGED` (`409`), plus the currency-mismatch and annual-cycle rejections (A1, A3) as `422 BILLING_PRICE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH` / `422 BILLING_CYCLE_NOT_AVAILABLE`; the statuses are the defaults of `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` and are confirmed at story discovery. Docs: `docs/ERROR_CATALOG.md` already lists all five (the `BILLING_*` entry carries `PRICE_NOTICE_TOO_SHORT`, `PRICE_UNCHANGED`, `PRICE_BATCH_CHANGED`, `PRICE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH` and `CYCLE_NOT_AVAILABLE`, verified at the audit), so no catalog edit is made here; `BILLING_CYCLE_NOT_AVAILABLE` here means an annual price-change request while annual cycles do not exist, which is a different meaning from the one slice H of the plan-change flow gives it, and the catalog defines both uses.

**Plano tab.** UC-112 requires "the Plano tab and the public prices show the current amount and the upcoming one". The Plano tab's `upcoming` per item is supplied by **S17** (`GET /v1/billing/plan`) and rendered by **S30**; S23 supplies it for the public prices. This story only produces the dated versions they read; its integration test asserts that after an apply the three reads (`GET /plan`, public prices, summary `upcomingPriceChanges`) carry the upcoming amount.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none migration; i18n error keys in `packages/i18n/locales/{pt-BR,en}/errors.json` for the five codes above (both locales).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/create-feature-price.use-case.ts`, `adjust-feature-prices.use-case.ts`, `schedule-feature-price-version.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts` each) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/feature-price.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `scheduleVersion`, `supersedePending`, rounding)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/feature-price-repository.port.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/repositories/typeorm-feature-price.repository.ts` and its in-memory double (+ specs) (modify — `findByCountryForUpdate`, the affected-tenants selector)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/billing-constants.ts` (modify — `PRICE_CHANGE_MIN_NOTICE_DAYS`, `PRICE_ANNOUNCE_MAX_TENANTS`; `CHARGE_ISSUE_LEAD_DAYS` is S05's)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (modify — the five price codes), `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts`, `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify — the five codes in both locales), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (modify only if the spec needs the new codes)
- `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (modify — flip the existing B-P1 section from planned to built and name its concurrency test)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices-admin.controller.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new), `apps/backend/http/billing/price-admin.http` (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] The operator can preview and apply a percentage or exact price change for a country; every affected manager gets exactly one consolidated email with the dated change; issued invoices and the current period never change; the public price page and the Plano tab show the upcoming amount.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Rounding half-up to `roundToMinor`; notice rule for increase / decrease / (annual rejected); each of A1–A8; `previewHash` stability
- Integration:
  - [ ] Dry run saves and sends nothing (row and outbox counts unchanged); apply writes versions and N events in one transaction; a rollback leaves none
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-P1, a test that fails without the lock):** two applies on the same country started together serialise on `findByCountryForUpdate`; the second re-computes its hash after the lock and is rejected with `409 BILLING_PRICE_BATCH_CHANGED`; exactly one batch of versions and one set of per-tenant events exist afterwards
  - [ ] An apply with more affected tenants than `PRICE_ANNOUNCE_MAX_TENANTS` is refused and writes nothing; below it, events are written in batches in one transaction
  - [ ] After an apply, `GET /v1/billing/plan` (S17), the public prices (S23) and the summary `upcomingPriceChanges` (S14) each carry the upcoming amount and date
  - [ ] End to end on the fake: S05 issues the next invoice at the old price before `effectiveFrom` and the new price for a period starting on or after it
  - [ ] Missing operator keys → `401`
  - [ ] `announced_at` is set on every version an apply inserts (including an apply with no affected tenant) and never by a dry run
- Tenant isolation: [ ] per-tenant events carry only that tenant's features and total; exempt and suspended tenants get none
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S39 — Launch readiness **[GATE]**

**Agent:** `devops + frontend-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/discovery/tenant-billing/tenant-billing.md` (launch gates and open threads), `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook, `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-110, UC-111, UC-126, UC-127, `docs/DEPLOYMENT_STRATEGY.md`, `docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10. PAYMENT PROVIDERS
**Dependencies:** M26-S29 (the billing-identity form the address / tax id and NFS-e depend on), M26-S34 (the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` component the owner publishes, item 5), M26-S35 (E2E green), M26-S36 (alerts and runbooks live), M26-S37 (the real adapter and nightly job), M26-S38 (decided at the M26 audit, 2026-11-18: the Terms state the 40-day price-change notice that S38 enforces, so the code must precede the promise), M17-S37 (production activation, `bootstrap_mode = false`: until it flips, production ignores `secret_env_vars` and `env_vars`, so S01's secrets, S24's `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` and the scheduler enablement here are no-ops) and M17-S36 (Cloud Armor origin lockdown: the production webhook path goes through the edge); transitively S24 / S33 (the signup flag it drills).
**Pattern:** A checklist story: every item is a verifiable fact with a named check; nothing here is "write the code" — it is the go/no-go gate.

**Description:**
The go / no-go for charging real customers. The story is complete when each item below is **observed**, not asserted: **Prerequisite (found at the M26 audit): production is still `bootstrap_mode = true`** (`infra/terraform/envs/prod/terraform.tfvars`), and `cloudrun-service` drops `secret_env_vars` while it is true; M17-S37 (production activation) flips it. So "production deploys were never blocked by unpopulated secrets" holds only because of that: when M17-S37 flips it, a revision that mounts `asaas-api-key` / `asaas-webhook-token` with **zero secret versions fails at creation**. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18):** the activation runbook step populates placeholder secret versions before the flip; the choice is recorded in the activation PR. **Order matters.** Until then production boots without the Asaas secrets, because the Asaas secrets are optional (S01) and `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` defaults to `false` in production (S24). This story opens production in this order: items 1-3 and 4 (account, NFS-e, legal, Ikaro's tenant), then populating the real Asaas secret versions (the webhook route and the live adapter start working as soon as they are set), then the production smoke charge (item 8, which also proves real Asaas → BFF webhook reachability), and only then `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=true`.

**(1) Production provider account** — the Asaas production PJ account is approved (KYC done) and its production API key and webhook token are in Secret Manager (production values populated here, after S01 populated only sandbox); the webhook is configured at Asaas pointing at `https://<web-host>/v1/public/billing/webhooks/asaas` — the **web gateway** path, the only public route to the internal-ingress BFF (found at the audit; not a BFF host) — with the `authToken`; whether Cloudflare / the WAF / Cloud Armor (M17-S36) lets Asaas's source addresses through that path is **unverified** and is checked by item 8's real webhook; the production `merchant_profile_key` is the live key; the contracted **fee table** and notification fees are confirmed in the account (PROVIDERS §4 item 3) and recorded. The Asaas facts S37 re-confirmed on the sandbox (launch gates G1–G17 of `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`; G17 is the alphanumeric-CNPJ check S37 runs) (`notificationDisabled` vs paid receipts, `externalReference` length, webhook payload fields, R$ 5 minimum, `invoiceUrl` after the due date) are re-checked against the **production** account and each is either closed in the provider doc or carried with a named owner; also verify whether `notificationDisabled = true` **silences the payer's NFS-e email**. An unverified lost-chargeback event keeps chargeback-driven `PAYMENT_REVERSED` **off**, and the manual `OPERATOR`-suspension path is the runbook's chargeback entry (S36). **(2) NFS-e** — provider-automatic NFS-e emission is configured once in the Asaas account (launch gate; no per-charge work), and the accountant has confirmed it satisfies the Brazilian duty (**record location, found at the audit as unnamed:** the confirmation's date and the accountant's role are written as an observed line in the PR description and in `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` (the account-owner row); the confirmation itself stays with the owner, no personal data in the repository; the contracted fee table of item 1 goes into the same doc). Prerequisites checked first (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A15 and its account-owner row, `docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10): a PJ account whose CNPJ has been active 6+ months (also what Pix Automático needs, M29), the municipal registration, the prefeitura's NFS-e homologation, the registered service, and the payer data Ikaro already collects (name, valid CPF / CNPJ, email, full address with CEP — S16 / S29 and the S05 address hold exist for this). The accountant also states the **NFS-e cancellation procedure** for a refund or a chargeback (recorded in the runbook), and the **fiscal retention period in years** for the charge history that survives a tenant purge — which must be recorded in `docs/DATA_RETENTION_AND_PRIVACY.md` **before the first real charge** (that doc marks it open until then). **(3) Legal** — the final Terms of Use and Privacy Policy texts and version are in place (replacing the S33 placeholders, which S33 creates under `/ikaro-trial/termos` and `/ikaro-trial/privacidade`), the Terms state the cancellation / no-refund policy, the **40-day price-change notice** S38 enforces (consistent now that S38 is a dependency), and name **Asaas as a data processor** (owner / legal text — this story is the gate, M26 does not write it), and the production backend env `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` (the single source; the web has no duplicate constant, S24 / S33) is set in the production Terraform to the version of the hosted text. **Verifiable check:** the version the web renders at `https://<web-host>/ikaro-trial/termos` equals `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` in `gcloud run services describe <backend>` (the session read returns the same `termsVersion` to a visitor with a signup session); the pages are no longer `noindex` placeholders. **(4) Ikaro's own tenant** — create the platform-owner tenant with `scripts/provision-tenant.sh` using the `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` slug (`ikaro` in `infra/terraform/envs/prod/main.tf`, already set), the `exempt` flag and **country BR / locale pt-BR** (the `/ikaro-trial` pages inherit the owner tenant's locale and currency, S33; the local seed's `ikaro` is US/en, so the production tenant must not copy it); confirm it is `ACTIVE`, exempt, and cannot be un-exempted (D3: no production tenants exist before this point, so no production backfill is needed; the M25 backfill only ever ran on local / staging data). **(5) Hotsite CTA** — the owner's manager adds and publishes the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` module on Ikaro's hotsite; **verifiable:** the published hotsite manifest (`GET /v1/public/platform/manifest/<PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG>` through the web host) lists a `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` module with `enabled: true`. **(6) Alerts and schedulers live** — the six billing scheduler jobs, created **paused** in production by S05–S10, are enabled here — `paused` is Terraform-managed (`paused_job_keys`), so enabling is the prod `paused_job_keys` removal in PR-A, not a `gcloud` command — each alert policy exists in the production root (`module "monitoring"` is **already instantiated** in `envs/prod/main.tf`, so this item checks that S36's policies appear in `gcloud alpha monitoring policies list`, not that the module is wired), and one throwaway production-safe **dry run** has been observed. **Dry run, defined (found at the audit as undefined):** a freshly provisioned **staging** tenant on `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` goes issue → sandbox pay → `PAID` → reactivation with staging's alert policies standing in for production's; observed = the charge is `PAID`, no alert fired, and `gcloud logging read` shows the per-run log line of each of the six jobs on staging. **(7) Rollback** — the rollback procedure (`docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Rollback) is rehearsed for the Billing release, including the two levers that stop new exposure: **stop signups** — set `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=false` (the real kill switch, S24 / S33; unpublishing the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` module is **not** one, because `/ikaro-trial` stays reachable from existing links, UC-131 A3) and verify by loading the hotsite and `/ikaro-trial`, which must show the "cadastro indisponível" notice while the signup routes return `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` and prices / slug availability stay readable — and **pause the six scheduler jobs** so no charge is issued and no suspension applied while the cause is investigated. **Decided at the M26 audit (2026-11-18): the rollback lever is a one-line `paused_job_keys` PR** (documented in `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Rollback), because the Scheduler job's `paused` state is Terraform-managed and a `gcloud scheduler jobs pause ikaro-cron-billing-<name>` is only a **temporary stop that the next `terraform apply` undoes** (drift) — use it for the first minutes of an incident, then land the PR. Whether `gcloud scheduler jobs run` works on a paused job is **unverified**: the rehearsal checks it and records the answer in the runbook. **(8) Production smoke charge** (`docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` § Testing the billing provider, "Live mode cannot be sandboxed") — with signups still closed and the real Asaas secrets populated, create a **dedicated non-exempt production test tenant** (never Ikaro's own, which is exempt and cannot be charged), issue one small real charge, pay it at Asaas, observe the **real webhook** reach the production BFF (`200`, `provider_events` row, charge `PAID`, the receipt email), check the NFS-e emission (**only observable in the provider console**: no API read exists, so the observation is the invoice's presence and number noted in the PR), then **refund** it (the safe refund order is in the runbook: this is a full refund of the first invoice of a `TRIALING` tenant, which suspends nothing) and cancel the NFS-e per the accountant's procedure; only then open signups (`FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=true`). A "dispute opened" alert (S36) is wired in production. The M27 purge dependency is recorded: the first possible erasure is 90 days after the first suspension, so M27 must be scheduled before that date. No code is written here except small fixes the checklist exposes (each in its own tiny commit referencing this story).

**PR sequence:** per item, and **never one PR mixing `infra/terraform/**` with `apps/**`** (CI's `no-infra-app-mix` job): PR-A (devops) — production Terraform (scheduler jobs enabled via the `paused` switch from S05, `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION`, the two flags) following the playbook rows cited in S05–S08 / S36; PR-A touches only `infra/terraform/envs/prod/main.tf`, so **no Foundation apply is made unless a grant is found missing** (S01 already wired the secret accessors); PR-B (frontend) — the final legal text pages (owner-supplied); `docs/**` changes ride with the PR they describe. Production secret population is a manual step (no PR).
**IAM / permissions:** production secret versions readable by the production runtime service accounts (already wired in S01); no new principal.
**Live-verification check:** first the two prerequisites (`bootstrap_mode = false` in `infra/terraform/envs/prod/terraform.tfvars` after M17-S37, and the Cloud Armor origin lockdown of M17-S36 in place); then the eight items above, each with its command / observation recorded in the PR description — notably the production smoke charge (item 8) with the real webhook observed, the signup kill-switch drill (item 7), and `gcloud scheduler jobs list --project=<prod>` showing the six billing scheduler jobs `ENABLED` (issue charges, status sweep, reminders, reconcile, self-heal, provider-events purge; the seventh, tenant purge, is M27 and stays absent), `gcloud pubsub topics get-iam-policy` populated for every billing topic in **production**, and a real sandbox-to-staging money-loop run. Pass = all eight observed; a failed or un-runnable item is a stuck condition, not a skip.

**Backend use case steps:** none.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none new.

**Files to create/modify:**
